## Grammar / lint contradictions (2026-07-03)

- ✅ **`f(1)(2)` curried-call juxtaposition is now a newline-sensitive parse error (2026-07-06).** Previously §4.7's "`f(1)(2)` does not parse" was imprecise: in operand/argument position (`println(adder(1)(2))`) menhir correctly rejected it (`I got stuck here` at the second `(`), but in bare block-statement position (`let r = adder(1)(2)`) the parser silently mis-split it into two independent `block_expr`s with **no diagnostic** — `r` bound to `adder(1)` alone and the trailing `(2)` discarded (a side-effecting `adder(1)(side(99))` proved `side` really ran as its own statement). Per user decision, this is now a **parse error** in both positions. Fix lives in `lib/parser/token_filter.ml` (not `parser.mly`), because the newline-sensitivity is only available before the token filter deletes the `NL`: `f(1)(2)` and `f(1)⏎(g(2))` are token-identical by the time menhir sees them. The filter threads three pieces of state through its emit boundary — a paren-kind stack (`Call` if the `LPAREN`'s preceding significant token is value-ending, else `Group`), the previous significant token, and a saw-newline flag — and raises `` `f(...)(...)` is not a chained call — March functions are not curried. `` when a `LPAREN` immediately follows a **call's** closing `RPAREN` with no intervening newline. The guard deliberately preserves an IIFE `(fn x -> x)(5)` (the `)` closes a group, not a call) and a two-line `f(1)⏎(g(2))` (newline = two statements). Corpus: `reject/r14_curried_call_not_chained.march` + `parse/p23_iife_lambda_call.march` + `parse/p24_two_line_call_juxtaposition.march` (grammar corpus 35→38). Docs: `specs/lang/grammar.md` §4.7 + §7.3 (marked RESOLVED) + "Known parser findings". Four `test/test_compiler.ml` arity tests that had relied on the old `add(1) ()` silent-split idiom rewritten to the direct call (`add(1)`), which already carries the arity error. Full suite green: **420 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib**, grammar 38/38, types 39/39.

- [ ] **`style/no-redundant-else` lint advises code the parser rejects** — found during Wave 4 Task 4. The lint (`lib/lint/lint.ml` ~262-320) and its doc (`docs/coding-standards.md` "style/no-redundant-else") tell users to remove `else` after a Never-typed branch (guard-clause pattern), with an auto-fix that "removes else, dedents body" — but `else` is mandatory in the grammar, so the advice/auto-fix produces a hard parse error ("March `if` expressions always need an `else` branch"; the doc's own "Good" example doesn't parse). Decide: retire the lint, grow an else-less statement-position `if` (language design — needs user sign-off), or make the auto-fix produce valid code; then fix the doc example. Check whether the LSP code-action path exposes the auto-fix.
- ✅ **Undocumented `if c then e1 else e2` production removed (Wave 4 Task 4, 2026-07-03)** — the parser silently accepted the then-form (contradicting both syntax docs and da7ce42b's targeted then-error, which only fired on incomplete forms). User decision: REMOVE + MIGRATE. Accepting production deleted from `lib/parser/parser.mly` (THEN token + targeted error production kept — every then-form now gets "I don't recognize `then` here — March uses do/end blocks instead."); live uses migrated (`stdlib/config.march` + `share/march/` mirror, `test_properties.ml` generators/oracles, `forge new` scaffold template, June-17-cutover leftover twins in four test files, LSP fixture); `then` dropped from REPL/LSP keyword completions and the REPL multiline heuristic; both syntax docs + tour + SKILL + linear-types doc corrected to verified truth (else MANDATORY). Menhir conflicts unchanged (7/59). **385 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots.** Full report: `.superpowers/sdd/w4-task-4-report.md`.

## Compiled-backend codegen bugs in HTTP handler paths (2026-06-29)

- ✅ **Publish-path Perceus UAF — FIXED (2026-06-29): it was a monomorphization gap, not a Perceus/borrow bug.** `Forgepm.Api.Upload.get_file` crashed at `"--" ++ boundary` (UBSan: `march_string_concat`). The real cause: the request header is read via a generic helper `get_req_header(conn, name) = lookup(conn.hdrs, name)` whose row-polymorphic `conn` gives the projection `conn.hdrs` a fresh type var; monomorphising `conn → Conn` left `lookup`'s value type abstract, so `Option('V)` was emitted **Boxed** while the concrete caller read it as a **Niche** — the Some-box pointer was dereferenced as the payload String (SIGSEGV). The "boundary string freed" framing was wrong; `boundary`'s argument was a heap `Some` box read as a string. Fix in `lib/tir/mono.ml`: `refine_field_types` (resolve record-field projections against the now-concrete record before call specialisation) + a `TRecord` case in `match_ty`. The three committed repros (`specs/repros/perceus_tuple_proj_rc_{min,full,control_ok}.march`) pass compiled, stable across 100 runs; regression test in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` ("record-field poly projection: Option niche/box repr consistent"). See `specs/2026-06-29-perceus-tuple-projection-rc-bug.md`. **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen pass.**
- [ ] **Home-route empty render** — compiled `GET /` returns a 200 with an EMPTY body (`octet-stream`); isolated to `list_popular(8)` / `home_page(...)`. `/packages` (also DB+HTML) and `registry_stats()` (in `forge test`) are fine. Likely the same monomorphization class as the publish-path fix above (a generic projection feeding a generic callee) — re-test against a fresh compiled forgepm binary before assuming it still reproduces.
- [ ] **Native `task_cancel_by_id` codegen gap — blocks `Task.race`/`Task.any`/cancel-tokens compiled (2026-07-06)** — found while restoring `test/apps/actor_stress.march`. Any program using `Task.race`, `Task.any`, or the cancel-token builtins (`task_cancel_token_new`/`task_spawn_with_cancel`/`task_cancel`/`task_is_cancelled`) fails to compile: `error: use of undefined value '@task_cancel_by_id'`. `Task.race`/`Task.any` (`stdlib/task.march` ~156/184) cancel losing tasks via `List.each(rest, fn t -> task_cancel_by_id(t))`; the runtime symbol `march_task_cancel_by_id` is declared, but the closure-wrapper `@task_cancel_by_id$clo_wrap` (needed when the builtin is passed as a first-class value) is never emitted — see `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` + `lib/tir/defun.ml` `builtin_names` + the "builtin multi-site" gotcha (emit in `emit_module` AND all 5 `llvm_repl` finalizers). Interpreter runs all of these fine. Repro: compile a 2-task `Task.race`. Once fixed, restore the omitted tests in `actor_stress.march` (see its NOTE comments) + regenerate `actor_stress.expected`.
- [ ] **ptr-payload `Task.await` runtime OOM (compiled) (2026-07-06)** — distinct from the i64 Ok-payload double-tag fixed above. Compiled `Task.await` on a task whose result is a heap value (String, List, …) crashes at run time with `march: out of memory`; the interpreter prints correctly and `Task.await_unwrap` on a String compiles+runs fine (`s=hi`), so it's specific to the `Result`-returning path (`march_task_await` → `mk_ok(task[3])`, `runtime/march_runtime.c` ~1631-1642) for ptr payloads. Repro: `Task.await(async(fn () -> "a" ++ "b"))` compiled → OOM. Likely an RC/GC mis-handling of the `Ok` heap payload (the trampoline stores `(addr<<1)|1`; the destructure conditional-untags, but investigate whether an incrc/decrc on a mis-tagged pointer blows up allocation). Start from `march_task_await` + how `Ok(a)` destructures RC-manage a `Result(String, _)` payload.

## P0 — Blocking / Active

- [ ] **Compiled `tcp_recv_http` panics "non-exhaustive pattern match" on a real read (2026-07-06)** — found while live-publishing `marathon` to production `forgepm.org` via the newly-wired `forge publish`. Minimal 15-line repro: `mod Main do needs IO.NetConnect fn main() do match tcp_connect(...) do Ok(fd) -> match tcp_send_all(fd, ...) do Ok(_) -> match tcp_recv_http(fd, 16777216) do ... end end end end` — compiled with `march --compile`, run against a real local HTTP server (not even TLS), panics inside the `tcp_recv_http(fd, ...)` call itself (never reaches the caller's own exhaustive `Err`/`Ok` match — confirmed via debug `println`s bisecting each step). Interpreted (`march file.march`, no `--compile`) does NOT panic on the identical program/server. `tcp_recv_http` is a native builtin (`march_tcp_recv_http`, declared `lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml:430` — its `ret_ty`/`declare_sig` are already correctly `Result(String,String)`, unlike the `tls_write` bug below, so the cause is elsewhere in the compiled ADT-construction/match-compiler path). Blocks any compiled program from safely using plain (non-TLS) HTTP receive — does NOT block a real `https://` registry publish (that path uses `Tls.read`/`send_tls`, not `tcp_recv_http`/`send_plain`; confirmed fixed and working, see Done below). Root cause not yet found — needs a dedicated debugging session with `--emit-llvm` on the minimal repro.

- [x] **Dynamic-record RC/GC crash in TCO loops (chip `task_0b1491ee`, follow-up from Wave 1 Task 5, 2026-07-02)** — a dynamic/erased-shape record used inside a TCO'd (tail-recursive) loop crashes under RC/GC; spawned as a separate background chip by the Wave 1 Task 5 implementer (`0d1a829e`, the B5 `EUpdate`-on-erased-record fix) rather than fixed inline, since it's a distinct code path from the `EUpdate` OOB write B5 addressed. Tracked only in `.superpowers/sdd/progress.md`'s internal ledger until this closeout entry — no commit has landed and no prior `specs/todos.md`/`specs/progress.md` entry existed for it. **Disposition (Wave 4 Task 6 sweep, amended same-day): VERIFIED FIXED — closing.** Controller probe at HEAD `f145439a` (the closeout amendment's parent): a 10k-iteration self-TCO loop doing `record_from_list` + `record_put` + two `record_get`s per iteration compiles and runs clean (exit 0, arithmetically exact sum `100030000`, cross-checked). The crash was resolved by the record-builtin uniform-value handoff that landed on main during Wave 2 (merge `c28595bb` era — `record_put` values UNIFORM/tagged, fixing the even-int-≥4096 misclassified-as-heap-pointer incrc SIGSEGV that made these loops "crash above ~10k iterations": that is when `i*2` crossed 4096). The 20k-iteration guard test (`test_compiled_record_put_large_even_int`, test/test_stdlib_suite.ml) pins it. Chip `task_0b1491ee`'s session contributed that fix; the loop was only ever missing this cross-reference.
- [ ] **Monomorphization limit reached compiling a self-recursive nested closure (2026-07-02)** — found during Wave 2 Task 2 (test-harness loud-skip hardening, `specs/plans/2026-07-02-wave2-testing-infra.md`) when a previously-vacuous `if compile_rc <> 0 then ()` skip in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`'s `test_compiled_recursive_closure_capture` was converted to a loud failure and immediately caught a real, independently-reproducible compiler crash: `march --compile` on a trivial nested closure that recurses via a captured outer-fn parameter —
  ```march
  mod RcClosure do
    fn f(m) do
      fn go(mi, acc) do
        if mi >= m do mi else go(mi + 1, acc + 1) end
      end
      go(1, 0)
    end
    fn main() do println(int_to_string(f(11))) end
  end
  ```
  — dies with `Fatal error: exception Failure("Monomorphization limit reached: function 'List.fold_left' has more than 512 specializations. This usually indicates polymorphic recursion...")`, exit code 2 (uncaught OCaml exception). Reproduces even with the module's only diagnostic-triggering call (`process_exit`, which additionally needs `needs IO.Process` — a separate, harmless, non-blocking hint/warning) replaced by `println`, so the crash is intrinsic to the nested-closure-capture shape itself, not to the capability check. `go` is not polymorphic-recursive in the source (`Int -> Int -> Int` throughout) and never mentions `List.fold_left`, so `lib/tir/mono.ml`'s specialization-count blowup for `List.fold_left` must come from how this shape lowers/defuns before monomorphization — needs investigation starting from `lib/tir/mono.ml`'s worklist/`done_set` dedup for closure-captured self-recursive local fns (compare against the already-fixed `ECallPtr` self-recursive-return-type case this same test targets, `specs/2026-0?-...` — the test's *original* regression, which the interpreter/compiled parity check below the crash was written to guard). Test converted to a documented `Alcotest.skip` (matching on the exact "Monomorphization limit reached" message so any OTHER compile failure at that site still fails loudly) rather than a silent no-op, pending a fix. **New confirmed trigger (2026-07-04, differential-oracle Task 7 full-corpus sweep):** `examples/stats_basic.march` hits the exact same ICE compiled (`march --compile examples/stats_basic.march` exits 3, same "Monomorphization limit reached: function 'List.fold_left' has more than 512 specializations" message) while the interpreter runs it clean (exit 0). Added to the oracle's `known_divergence` list (`test/test_oracle.ml`) rather than filed as a new bug — same root cause, just another program shape that happens to trip the same `mono.ml` guard.
- [ ] **DataFrame `group_by` + `Stats.mean`: compiled-only RC-misclassification SIGSEGV (2026-07-04)** — found during differential-oracle Task 7's full-corpus conformance sweep (`test/test_oracle.ml`, `specs/plans/2026-07-04-differential-oracle-plan.md` Task 7). `bench/dataframe_bench.march` runs clean under the interpreter (exit 0, full expected output through "Done.") but crashes compiled with `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` partway through the GroupBy+agg stage (banner prints, then dies before any group row prints). `lldb` backtrace on the compiled binary:
  ```
  * thread #3, stop reason = EXC_BAD_ACCESS (code=1, address=0x4010000000000000)
    frame #0: march_incrc + 48        (runtime/march_runtime.c — atomic ldadd into a bad pointer)
    frame #1: go$apply$4104 (closure inside Stats.mean's fold)
    frame #2: Stats.mean + 104
    frame #3: DataFrame.eval_agg + 608
    frame #4: go$apply$2583 / $lam19634$apply$2580
    frame #5: DataFrame.apply_group_by + 520
    frame #6: bench_groupby + 292
  ```
  The faulting address (`0x4010000000000000`) has the shape of a tagged/erased immediate being dereferenced as if it were a heap pointer — the same RC-misclassification *class* as the sort-family crashes (`bench/heapsort.march` et al., "heapsort RC underflow" below), but a **distinct call site**: this one is reached through `DataFrame.eval_agg`'s `Mean` aggregation path calling `Stats.mean` (`stdlib/stats.march`) inside a per-group fold, not through `Sort`'s heap/merge internals. Repro: `march --compile bench/dataframe_bench.march -o /tmp/df && /tmp/df` (exit 139); `march bench/dataframe_bench.march` (exit 0, prints all 4 benchmark sections including correct GroupBy output — `cat_0`..`cat_4`, 200 rows each, mean values `47.5`..`51.5`). Needs investigation starting from why a value flowing into `Stats.mean`'s internal fold closure is misclassified as owning a heap pointer specifically under the `DataFrame`→`eval_agg`→per-group-closure call shape — not yet root-caused to a specific `perceus.ml`/`borrow.ml` site. Added to the oracle's `known_divergence` list (`test/test_oracle.ml`) so the conformance sweep stays green while this is open.

### Recently fixed
- ✅ **`forge` registry-dependency resolver — `bastion = "0.2.1"` now fetches + builds (2026-07-08)** — Registry-dep resolution was an explicit placeholder on this branch (a prior session built it but never committed — lost to churn); a bare-string dep `name = "0.2.1"` was silently dropped, so `forge build` errored `Unknown module Bastion`. Re-implemented (`forge/lib/{project,cmd_deps,cmd_build,registry_client}.ml`): `project.ml` parses bare-string and `{ version = "..." }` deps as `RegistryDep`; `cmd_deps.ml`'s `resolve_registry_deps` fetches `<reg>/api/v1/packages/:name` metadata via the embedded `forge_registry.march` `fetch` action, PubGrub-solves (path/git deps pinned as overrides), downloads each release, sha256-verifies against the release checksum (digestif), and extracts with `--strip-components=1` into `~/.march/cas/deps/<name>`; `cmd_build.ml` maps `RegistryDep → ~/.march/cas/deps/<name>/lib`. External commands run under a `fork`+`setsid`+kill-process-group timeout so a hung march→clang child can't orphan. Verified end-to-end against the LIVE registry: a minimal app AND the full **forgepm** app both resolve `bastion = "0.2.1"` (fetch metadata → PubGrub → download 485852 B → sha256 verify → extract) and compile with 0 errors (forgepm binary built). 239 forge tests green (1 pre-existing `qualified-call cycle` failure confirmed unrelated via `git stash`). Commit `d3f705d5`.
- ✅ **`march_tcp_recv_http` returned a bare String / NULL instead of `Result(String,String)` → compiled `forge publish` client PANICKED `non-exhaustive pattern match` (2026-07-08)** — `tcp_recv_http` is declared `Result(String, String)`, but `runtime/march_http.c`'s `march_tcp_recv_http` returned a bare `march_string` on success and `NULL` on every error path — unlike its sibling `march_tcp_send_all`, which wraps in `make_ok`/`make_err`. A bare String / NULL matches neither `Ok` nor `Err` of the boxed `Result`, so a compiled `match tcp_recv_http(...) do Err(e) -> .. | Ok(raw) -> .. end` hit the non-exhaustive fallback and PANICKED — this bit the `forge publish` client's plain-HTTP (`--insecure`) path (`forge_registry.march`): the publish succeeded server-side but the client crashed. Never seen in production because the TLS publish path reads via `read_chunks`, not `tcp_recv_http`. Fix: wrap the success return in `make_ok(result)` and every error path in `make_err(<reason>)`, honoring the declared Result contract (also protects stdlib `http_transport.march`, which matches it the same way). Verified: the `--insecure` fetch now fails GRACEFULLY (`transport: read failed: tcp_recv_http: connection closed …`) instead of panicking. Commit `ed778f10`. **Residual (separate, deeper): the plain-TCP path still can't complete the read over an SSH tunnel (connection-closed) — a `send_plain`/recv-behavior issue distinct from the panic; and a large multipart POST through the Cloudflare tunnel still returns 502 (likely cloudflared re-chunking a body forgepm's Content-Length-only parser can't frame). Production publishing uses the direct/TLS path; these affect `--insecure`/tunneled publishing only.**
- ✅ **Compiled HTTP server capped every request at a fixed 64KB → registry package uploads >64KB got a 413 (2026-07-08)** — `runtime/march_http.c`'s per-connection read buffer was a fixed `CONN_BUF_SIZE = 64 * 1024`; when it filled without a complete request parse, the server returned an immediate 413, bounding the WHOLE request (headers + body) at 64KB. So forgepm (the package registry) rejected any tarball upload larger than 64KB — a 486KB `bastion` publish `413`'d at the buffer (the `forge publish` client then saw the 413 and, through Cloudflare, a 502). Fix: grow the buffer by doubling on demand up to a new `MAX_REQ_SIZE = 32 * 1024 * 1024` cap (one `realloc` in the recv step), only 413 past the cap; sub-64KB requests still start at and stay within 64KB, so the hot path is unchanged and only an overflowing request pays a realloc. Verified live on the deploy droplet: a 600KB POST now reaches the handler (401 for a bad token) instead of 413, and `forge publish` of `bastion` 0.2.1 (485KB, with the islands-dispatch fix) succeeds server-side (registry lists 0.1.0–0.2.1). **Follow-up (not this fix): the `forge publish` client (`registry.march`) panics `non-exhaustive pattern match` parsing the publish SUCCESS response — the publish completes server-side but the CLI reports failure; a client-side response-shape gap to fix separately.**
- ✅ **`--check` type-erasure through an ENTRY-module self-qualified reference (`EntryMod.fn`) — an unannotated (or distinct-tvar-annotated) entry-level fn laundered any type through the qualified name, so a memory-unsafe mismatch typechecked clean (2026-07-08)** — `./_build/default/bin/main.exe --check` exited 0 on `mod Main do fn id(x) do x end  fn need_str(s : String) : Int do string_length(s) end  fn attack() : Int do need_str(Main.id(42)) end end` — an `Int` laundered into a `String` parameter (a memory-safety break); the bare `need_str(id(42))` correctly rejected. All three soundness classes reproduced through `Main.id`: general `Int`→`String`, the `Cap(IO)`→`Cap(Db.P)` proof-cap forge, and an ADT `Box(String)`→`Box(Int)` arg; a nested sibling reference to the entry name (`T.id` from `mod App` inside entry `T`) reproduced too. **Root cause** (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, DFn branch of `check_decl`): the entry module is UNWRAPPED at the combined module's top level, so — unlike a wrapped sibling module, whose public members are re-exported under `Sib.fn` with their real body-checked schemes when the sibling's `DMod` is checked (verified: `Lib.id` from a sibling correctly rejects) — its own top-level fns are only ever seeded by `check_module_core`'s Pass 1b (`prebind_mod_members m.mod_name.txt`) as a bare `Mono (fresh_var 1)` placeholder (`prebind_fn_scheme` returns None for an unannotated fn; a distinct-tvar annotation `fn f(x:a):b` yields an un-body-validated `a -> b` from annotation syntax). That decoupled `?a -> ?b` was never reconciled with the fn's real scheme, so any `EntryMod.fn` reference (produced by desugar's `qualify_module_refs`, or hand-written to disambiguate a shadowing nested local) instantiated it and ERASED the laundered type. The pinned placeholder also over-rejected legitimate polymorphism (`Main.id` used at both `Int` and `String` spuriously failed). **Fix:** the DFn branch now rebinds the entry-qualified name (`current_module ^ "." ^ fn_name`) to `sch`, the scheme `check_fn` validated against the body, so it is exactly as polymorphic as the bare name. Guarded to fire only at the ENTRY level (`cap_qual_prefix = "" && current_module <> ""`: nested/loaded modules already re-export real schemes) and only when the qualified key already exists (`Some _`: a private `pfn` gets no qualified prebind and is untouched; the incremental `check_module_with_env` path never seeds the entry's own top-level fns, so the fix is inert there). Codegen-safe: mono/TIR key on the per-span `type_map`, not this env scheme — `llvm_ir_validity_gate` stays at 0 failures. **Sibling of the still-open link-time finding above** (entry file self-qualifying its own top-level mod name → undefined symbols at link time): same `entry_prefix = "" / cap_qual_prefix = ""` root, but that is a codegen/lowering gap this typecheck fix does not address, so it remains open. New non-vacuous RED→GREEN group `entry_mod_qual_erasure` in `test/test_compiler.ml` (8 cases): the three launder shapes + the C2 distinct-tvar variant + the nested-sibling `T.id` shape (all flip clean→rejected), plus three green guards — `Main.id` at a single type stays clean, `Main.id` used polymorphically at Int AND String is RESTORED to clean (RED pre-fix: placeholder pinning over-rejected), and an annotated `a->a` entry fn stays clean. **Full suite: 451 compiler (+8) / 231 eval / 394 codegen (IR-gate 0 failures) / 805 stdlib / 29 snapshots pass, 0 failures** (`test_stdlib_march`'s lone `global_registry` `unbound variable` failure is pre-existing — identical at HEAD without this change).
- ✅ **A single-field ADT defined in a NON-ENTRY module was constructed boxed but tuple-nested-destructured as a newtype — compiled `Bytes.concat` returned an empty `Bytes` (2026-07-08)** — Compiled `Bytes.concat(a, b)` returned an EMPTY `Bytes` (`Bytes.length`/`to_list` = 0) while the interpreter was correct (5); this emptied depot's Postgres wire packets (`Encode.encode(Startup)` builds them via `Bytes.concat`), hanging forgepm's native PG handshake — the actual deploy blocker (distinct from the mono `go`-collision fix above; the pre-diagnosis conflated them). Root cause in `lib/tir/llvm_case.ml` `emit_case`: a type defined in a non-entry module (a nested `mod`, or any MARCH_LIB_PATH library incl. all of stdlib) is registered in `tm_types` under its MODULE-QUALIFIED name (`lower_mod_decls` prepends the prefix → `Bytes.Bytes`), but that module's own value types reference it BARE (`Bytes`) because the typechecker drops the prefix on same-module references — entry-module types are registered bare, so this only bit non-entry types. Construction (`llvm_emit.ml` EAlloc) and top-level patterns resolve the type by the **bare** name (from the `TypeName.CtorName` ctor key) → `repr_of_ty` misses → **Boxed** (which is ALSO what the C runtime uses — `make_bytes_from_raw`/`bytes_to_raw` build/read `Bytes` as a box with the `List(Int)` at offset 16, and crypto/compress/tcp all depend on that). But `emit_case`'s newtype-recovery path — which fires for a TVar-erased tuple-element sub-scrutinee and identified the type by looking up the owning typedef by CTOR name → the **QUALIFIED** registration — classified it `Newtype`. So `match (b1,b2) do (Bytes(xs),Bytes(ys)) -> …` bound `xs` to the BOX POINTER (Newtype identity) instead of projecting the payload field, and the boxed header (tag 0) then read as an empty `Nil`. Entry-module ADTs registered bare → both sides agreed → worked, which is why it was invisible in single-file/entry tests and only surfaced through stdlib `Bytes`. Fix: make the recovery classify the owning type by the **bare** (last dot-segment) name, exactly as construction and the runtime do — so a non-entry single-field type stays consistently **Boxed** (a genuine entry-module newtype is registered bare, so stripping is a no-op and it still classifies `Newtype`). Keeping `Bytes` boxed is required: the runtime constructs and consumes it boxed pervasively (an earlier attempt to reconcile the names toward `Newtype` in `Repr.find_variant` made compiled `Bytes` a raw list, which then mis-read every runtime-constructed `Bytes` — `tcp_recv_exact`'s PG-handshake buffer decoded as an empty `Nil` → `Bytes.get: index out of bounds`). Minimal repros: nested-`mod` single file (`(Wrap(xs),Wrap(ys))` tuple match → `pair=0` compiled pre-fix, `5` post-fix / interp); `/tmp/bytes_probe.march` (`Bytes.concat` → `tolist_len` 0→5). New compile-and-run regression `test/test_codegen.ml` `nonentry_newtype_repr_codegen` (`test_nonentry_newtype_tuple_destructure_compiled`) — a nested `mod` single-field ADT tuple-destructured must print `5` compiled (RED `0` pre-fix). **Full suite: 443 compiler / 231 eval / 393 codegen / 805 stdlib / 29 snapshots pass, 0 failures; property oracle no new divergences; RC benchmarks unchanged; verified against the live Postgres on the deploy droplet (native forgepm clears the handshake).**
- ✅ **Monomorphization resolved a nested `fn`'s call against the top-level `fn_table`, so a user top-level `go`/`loop`/`aux` captured every stdlib nested helper of the same name — compiled tail-recursive list accumulators returned garbage (2026-07-08)** — A user program with a top-level `pfn go(xs, acc)` reversing a list via `Cons(h, acc)` compiled to a binary where `List.length(r)`/`List.sum_int(r)` returned a garbage pointer reinterpreted as an `Int` (observed `len=4745871568`); the interpreter was always correct (`len=3`), an interpreter-correct / compiled-wrong divergence that blocked the native forgepm deploy (its Postgres wire packets are built with `Bytes.concat` → `list_append_go`, a lifted top-level helper — the same collision emptied every startup packet). Root cause in `lib/tir/mono.ml` `rewrite_calls`: the `EApp`/`ECallPtr` callee is resolved by **bare name** against the module-level `fn_table`, but the recursion into `ELetRec`/`ELet`/`ECase` bodies never tracked which names are bound by a lexically-enclosing nested `fn`. March's stdlib uses the conventional helper name `fn go(...)` inside dozens of functions (`List.length`/`reverse`/`map`/`sum_int`/…); each is normally left un-resolved by mono (`fn_table` miss) and lifted to a distinct `go$apply$N` closure by `Defun`. But the moment ANY top-level `go` exists, `fn_table["go"]` hits, and every stdlib helper's nested `go(...)` call is silently rebound to the user's top-level `go` — the callee body is replaced wholesale, so `List.length` ran the user's reverse-accumulator against an `Int` accumulator and returned junk. Confirmed decisive: renaming the user fn `go`→`myrev` made the bug vanish; the buggy `--emit-llvm` showed `List.length`'s helper mangled to `go$List_Int$List_Int` (the user's mangling) with NO distinct length helper, and `go$List_Int$Int` (length's correct name) appeared in **zero** of the 35 dumped phases — the collision is present from `tir-mono` onward. Fix: thread a `shadowed : SSet.t` set of lexically-bound names through `rewrite_calls`; skip the `fn_table` resolution when the callee name is shadowed (leaving it for `Defun`, exactly as when no collision exists). The set accumulates `ELetRec` function names (in the inner bodies + the ELetRec body + — critically for a MONOMORPHIC helper like `sum_int`, whose block-`fn` lowers to `ELet("go", ELetRec([go], AVar go), go(xs,0))` — the continuation, via the `ELet` binder `v`), and `ECase` `br_vars` (constructor-arg pattern binders). Deliberately does NOT seed function *params* — that would short-circuit the intentional `ECallPtr` erased-type interface-dispatch fallback. New compiled-vs-interpreted regression `test/test_codegen.ml` (`nested_fn_name_collision_codegen`): a top-level `go` reversing `[1..5]` then `List.length`/`List.sum_int` on the result must print `5|15` compiled, matching the interpreter — RED pre-fix (`5|5308956944`) / GREEN post-fix. **Full suite: 443 compiler / 231 eval / 393 codegen (+1) / 805 stdlib / 29 snapshots pass, 0 failures; RC benchmarks unchanged (tree_transform=104857600, list_ops=333333666666, binary_trees depth-16 check=131071).**
- ✅ **`alias Long.Path as Short` inside a non-entry module body → undefined-symbol link failure (2026-07-07)** — `forge build` of forgepm (via bastion) failed to link with `Undefined symbols: _PubSub.subscribe / _PubSub.unsubscribe / _PubSub.unsubscribe_all / _PubSub.broadcast_from`, referenced from `IslandSocket.handle_text`/`ws_loop`. bastion's `mod IslandSocket` declares `alias Bastion.PubSub as PubSub` and calls `PubSub.subscribe(...)`; typecheck resolved the alias fine, but codegen emitted the call as `_PubSub.subscribe` while the definition (in `mod Bastion.PubSub`) was emitted as `Bastion.PubSub.subscribe` — the two names never converged. Root cause: TIR lowering resolves module aliases via `_use_aliases` + `resolve_use_alias` (`lib/tir/lower.ml` / `lower_state.ml`), and there IS a top-level `DAlias` handler — but `lower_mod_decls`, which lowers every NON-entry module (auto-discovered MARCH_LIB_PATH files and nested `DMod`s), had **no `DAlias` case** in its inner decl match (it fell into the `_ -> ()` catch-all). So an `alias` at the entry file's own top level resolved correctly, but the identical alias inside any auto-discovered module body was silently dropped. Minimal repro: `mod AcBase.Target do fn answer() do 42 end end` + a separate lib module `mod AcCaller do alias AcBase.Target as T; fn go() do T.answer() end end` + an entry calling `AcCaller.go()` → `_T.answer` undefined; entry-file aliasing of the same module linked fine — the exact entry-vs-lib asymmetry. Fix: added an order-independent module-alias PREFIX table (`_module_aliases`: short name → full path, e.g. `"PubSub" → "Bastion.PubSub"`) populated by `DAlias` in BOTH the top-level and (newly-added) `lower_mod_decls` handlers, plus a fallback in `resolve_use_alias` that rewrites `Short.member → Long.Path.member` by prefix substitution when every exact-name lookup misses. Prefix rewrite is deliberately order-independent (unlike the existing exact-entry approach, which scans `!fns` and therefore only covers aliases whose target module was lowered first — true for the entry file, processed last, but not for a lib module whose target sibling may be lowered afterward). Exact entries still win when present; the prefix table only backstops. New native golden `test/dune` rule + fixtures in `test/alias_codegen/` (`ac_caller.march` is the non-entry aliasing module; compiled with MARCH_LIB_PATH, run, diffed to `42`) — RED pre-fix (`_T.answer` undefined → clang link failure) / GREEN post-fix. Verified end-to-end: forgepm now compiles+links to a native binary (was the last blocker). **Full suite (complete `dune build`): 443 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 805 stdlib pass, 0 failures.**
- ✅ **`march check`/`forge build` quadratic hang on multi-hundred-file MARCH_LIB_PATH projects (2026-07-07)** — `march check`/`forge build --target ...` on a large multi-repo project (forgepm + bastion + depot + march_doc, ~200 auto-discovered `.march` files) burned 99.9% CPU for 30+ minutes instead of completing in seconds, confirmed non-deadlocked via `sample <pid>` repeatedly showing `List.mem_472 -> caml_c_call -> caml_compare -> compare_val` as the dominant cost. Root cause: `Typecheck.record_use` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`), called on **every single `EVar`** during `infer_expr` (i.e. once per variable reference in the WHOLE combined program — stdlib + every file pulled in via MARCH_LIB_PATH auto-discovery, since `check_module_core` typechecks all of it as one shared `env`), did `List.iter (fun ie -> if ie.ie_matches name then ...) !(env.import_tracker)` — a full linear scan, with each `ie_matches` itself doing a `List.mem`/polymorphic-`=` scan, over `import_tracker`, a list holding ONE ENTRY PER `use`/`import`/`alias` DECLARATION ACROSS THE ENTIRE PROGRAM (never scoped per-file, only ever appended to). That pairing is O(total-var-refs × total-imports) — genuinely quadratic in project size, dominating everything else in the pipeline (parse/desugar/resolve-imports/stdlib-load were all sub-100ms per `--timings`). Fix: index `import_tracker` entries at creation time (the four `ie_matches` construction sites: `DUse`'s `UseAll`/`UseNames`/`UseExcept`, and `DAlias`) into a new `env.import_idx` — two `Hashtbl`s, `ie_exact_index` (every literal name an entry can exact-match: rebound short names, the bare qualified module/alias name itself) and `ie_prefix_index` (keyed by the first-dot-split "prefix root" of a qualified reference, mirroring `split_qualified`'s existing convention elsewhere in this file) — so `record_use` does two O(1)-average Hashtbl lookups instead of an O(n) scan; the prefix-index path still calls the entry's real `ie_matches` closure to disambiguate (defends against same-root-prefix collisions), so semantics are byte-for-byte preserved, not approximated. Verified via a synthetic scaling harness (many auto-generated modules each `import Shared` + several EVar refs, CAS cache cleared between runs so timings are real, not cache hits): 400 files ~1.2s→~0.6s, 1200 ~8.2s→~1.4s (6x), 2000 ~21.1s→~2.3s (9x), 3000 ~45.5s→~3.7s (12x) — pre-fix time fits ~quadratic (k≈5e-6·n²), post-fix is linear. Separately investigated and ruled out as a distinct bug: a cascade of spurious `Unknown module` errors (e.g. `DateTime`, `UUID`, `IOList`, and in some invocations `Config`) observed when scoping down a killed run — traced NOT to `import_tracker`/`record_use` but to `find_stdlib_dir()` (in both `bin/main.ml` and `lib/modules/module_registry.ml`) failing to locate the bundled `stdlib/` directory when `march`'s compiled binary is invoked from a cwd outside its own source tree AND the binary was built via a partial `dune build <specific-targets>` rather than a full build / `@install` (which is what actually copies `stdlib/` under `_build/default/`) — confirmed by reproducing the exact same cascade with the FIX applied but `_build/default/stdlib` absent, and confirming it disappears with a complete build; this is a pre-existing build/packaging rough edge, not a compiler correctness bug, and is out of scope for this fix (noted here for whoever hits it next). New regression test `test/test_compiler.ml` (`resolver` suite, `Slow`): `test_large_multi_file_check_is_not_quadratic` builds a synthetic MARCH_LIB_PATH (2500 auto-discovered modules each importing a shared module and referencing 30 of its functions) and asserts the check completes under 30s — confirmed RED pre-fix (measured 30.78s, matching the calibration) / GREEN post-fix (~3-5s) by temporarily stashing/restoring the fix. **Full suite (complete `dune build`, no partial-target gaps): 429 compiler (+1) / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 805 stdlib pass, 0 failures** — diffed against a throwaway clean baseline worktree off the same `main` commit (428/231/391/805, also 0 failures with a complete build) to confirm zero regressions and isolate the pre-existing `march: cannot find runtime/march_runtime.c` failures (14-15, non-deterministic count) as an unrelated partial-build artifact that disappears with a full build on BOTH branches.
- ✅ **Interpreter `http_server_listen` — single slow/idle client wedged the whole dev server (2026-07-07)** — The OCaml `http_server_listen` builtin (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, used by `forge run` / plain `march file.march`, NOT the compiled/LLVM path) was fully sequential: `select` only watched the *listening* socket, then `accept`ed exactly ONE client and blocked synchronously inside `handle_http_connection`'s `recv`/`send` for that client's entire request-response cycle before accepting the next. A client that opened the TCP connection but delayed sending bytes (e.g. a WebSocket client pausing before its upgrade request during reconnect backoff) left the interpreter's one thread parked in a blocking `recv()`, starving every other connection — confirmed via `sample <pid>` showing the sole thread inside `camlUnix.recv_1225`, with unrelated routes hanging 90+ seconds. Fixed by replacing the accept loop with a single-threaded, non-blocking, multiplexed event loop (`run_http_event_loop` in `lib/eval/eval.ml`), mirroring the same cooperative-scheduling pattern already used for actor mailboxes (`BlockedOnReceive`/`run_scheduler`): every open connection is a small state machine (`HCReadingRequest` accumulating bytes until a full request is parsed via `http_try_parse_request`, then `HCWriting` draining a queued response), and one `Unix.select` per iteration multiplexes the listen socket plus every live client socket — no connection's `recv`/`send` can block progress on any other connection. WebSocket upgrades are unchanged: once a `WebSocketUpgrade` conn is detected, the handshake is written and `handler_fn` still runs synchronously/blocking on that one connection for its lifetime, exactly as before (this was already the pre-fix behavior post-upgrade and is out of scope — only the plain-HTTP accept-and-parse path was non-blocking-ified). `handle_http_connection` and `http_server_spawn_n` (the fork-based N-request test variant) are untouched. Regression test in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` (`test_interp_http_server_idle_client_does_not_block_others`, `Slow`): launches the interpreter as a real subprocess, opens an idle TCP connection that never sends a byte, then opens a second connection that sends a complete request and asserts the response arrives within 1s — confirmed to fail (times out empty at the 2s bound) on the pre-fix code and pass post-fix by temporarily stashing/restoring the fix. Out of scope / follow-up: keep-alive (each connection is currently closed after one response, matching prior behavior), chunked transfer-encoding request bodies (not handled by `http_try_parse_request`, matching prior `Content-Length`-only behavior), and partial-write backpressure is now handled (writes queue and drain non-blocking across iterations) but large response bodies under a persistently-full socket buffer haven't been load-tested.
- ✅ **Channel payloads corrupted every odd Int + every Bool compiled (session-types F1/F2, 2026-07-06)** — a single `Chan.send(c, 43)` / `Chan.recv(s)` round-trip returned `43` interpreted but `21` compiled (`v → (v-1)/2`), and Bool flipped `true→false`; even Ints and heap/String payloads passed by luck (even-aligned). Root cause (IR-confirmed): `march_chan_send(ptr %ep, ptr %val)` is declared with a `ptr` payload slot, but `chan_send` had NO dedicated emit arm in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` and fell through the general `EApp` path, which passes the Int payload as a raw untagged `i64` (e.g. `43`). Meanwhile `march_chan_recv`'s returned payload goes through the standard erased-i64 CONDITIONAL-UNTAG restore (`ptrtoint`→`and 1`→`select (ashr x,1) x`) — asymmetric: send stores `43` raw, recv sees low-bit-set and `ashr`s → `21`. Fix: add three additive emit arms (`chan_send`/`chan_choose`/`mpst_send`), placed BEFORE the general `EApp` fallthrough, that coerce the payload/label/value arg via `emit_atom_as ctx "ptr"` (`coerce i64→ptr` = `(n<<1)|1`, the exact inverse of recv's conditional-untag; short-circuits to identity for values already `ptr`, so String/record/ADT payloads pass verbatim). Endpoint/role args stay `ptr`; return type mirrors the general path (`Chan` → `ptr`). Because the arms are ADDITIVE and sit ahead of the shared path, `record_put`/`task_await`/every other builtin is untouched by construction. Mirrors the existing `vault_set`/`actor_reply` precedent arms (same void*-round-trip tag bug). Verified interp==compiled for odd Int (43→44 echo), Bool (`true`→TRUE), String (`ping-pong`), and choose/offer atom-label + Bool (`BRANCH-ok-true`). Un-vacuumed the previously-vacuous session tests: `test_session_eval_send_recv` bumped to an odd payload; two new compile-and-run value assertions in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`session_compile` suite: `compiled odd Int payload round-trips (F1)`, `compiled Bool payload round-trips (F2)`) that RUN the compiled binary and check the printed value — both RED on the pre-fix binary (43→10, true→FALSE) / GREEN after. F3 (MPST compiled segfault), F4–F8 remain open (session-types survey findings). **`scripts/run-tests.sh` full six-runner suite GREEN: 426 compiler (+2 session compile-and-run cases) / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib pass; 29 snapshots pass.**
- ✅ **Compiled `Task.await`/`await_many`/`async_stream` returned double-tagged i64 results (2026-07-06)** — found while restoring the `test/apps/actor_stress.march` stress fixture and compiling it natively. The `Result`-returning `Task.await` family left an `Int` `Ok` payload double-tagged: the thunk trampoline stores `task[3] = (apply_ret << 1) | 1`, and for an `Int` the `apply` fn already returns a tagged scalar (`2*n+1`), so `task[3] = 4*n+3`; `march_task_await` (`runtime/march_runtime.c`) wraps `task[3]` straight into `Ok(...)` and the normal `Ok(n)` destructure untags only once, leaving `2*n+1`. Compiled `Task.await(async(fn () -> 6*7))` → `Ok(85)`, `await_many([10,20,30])` → sum `123` (21+41+61), `async_stream` squares-sum → `2×correct+N`; the interpreter was always correct, and `Task.await_unwrap` was already fixed (commit 291f6b5f) but the `Result`-returning paths — all funnelling through the one `task_await` builtin — never got it. Fixed in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s `task_await` case: for a statically-known `i64` `Task` inner type, strip the extra tag from the freshly-allocated `Ok` payload field (offset 16) with one `ashr` (`4*n+3` is always odd → unconditional shift is the exact inverse of the double-tag). Ptr-payload path left byte-identical (verified the String/List task IR carries none of the new instructions) → zero RC risk. New native compiled-run golden `test/native/task_await_int.march` (+ `.expected`, wired in `test/dune`, run under `MARCH_NUM_SCHEDULERS=1`), RED pre-fix (`85/123/115`) / GREEN post-fix (`42/60/55/99`, matching the interpreter). Also restored `test/apps/actor_stress.march` (stale `=`→`:` `init`/record syntax) as a second native golden `native_actor_stress`. **421 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib pass** (alcotest unchanged; +2 `test/dune` native golden-diff rules).
- ✅ **Cond-form and `when`-guard arms with binary operators failed to parse (2026-07-06)** — found while live-verifying `specs/lang/pattern-matching.md`. Two or more consecutive cond arms (`match do BoolExpr -> body end`) or match-arm guards (`Pattern when GuardExpr -> body`) whose expression used one of `>=`/`<=`/`==`/`!=`/`&&`/`||`/`++`/`|>`/`+`/`*`/`/`/`%` failed to parse: cond `score >= 90 -> "A"` / `score >= 80 -> "B"` errored "I got stuck here" at the 2nd `>=`; guard `x when x == 1 -> …` / `x when x == 0 -> …` errored "I was expecting `end` to close the match here" at the 2nd guard. A single such arm, or arms using strict `<`/`>` (LT/GT), always worked — which is why it went unnoticed. Root cause in `lib/parser/token_filter.ml`'s `lookahead_is_new_arm`: the contextual-newline filter decides whether the tokens after an arm body's NL start a NEW arm (scan reaches a depth-0 `ARROW`) or continue the current body (depth-0 `NL` first), and it *bailed early to "body continuation"* the moment it saw one of a set of binary-operator tokens (`LEQ/GEQ/EQEQ/NEQ/AND/OR/PLUSPLUS/PIPE_ARROW/PLUS/STAR/SLASH/PERCENT`). That heuristic is correct for a plain `Pattern -> body` arm (a bare pattern can never be followed by `>=`), but WRONG where the thing before `->` is a full boolean expression: the cond form (no scrutinee) and a guard's expression. The early-bail fired *before* the scan could reach the same-line `->`, so the second arm got glued onto the first arm's body. Fix: thread a per-match `ms_is_cond` flag (set by peeking one token after `MATCH` — cond iff `DO` immediately follows — carried on a `pending_match_conds` stack popped in lockstep with `pending_match_depths` at the matching `DO`), and within the lookahead track a `seen_when` flag set at a depth-0 `WHEN`. The binary-operator bail is now suppressed when `is_cond || seen_when`, so the scan continues to the same-line `ARROW`; the structural-keyword bails (`EQUALS/DO/LET/IF/MATCH/FN/PFN/ASSERT`, which guard against a spurious `ARROW` from a nested lambda/construct) stay unconditional. Plain scrutinee-match arms (not cond, no guard) are byte-for-byte unchanged. Multi-line arm-body continuations still terminate correctly at the depth-0 `NL` (verified: a `a == 5` statement on a body-continuation line stays in the body, in both plain and cond matches). 6 new parser regression tests in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`token_filter_cond_guard_operators` suite): 2+ consecutive `>=`/`==`/`<=` cond arms and 2+ consecutive `==`/`>=`/`<=` guards, each asserting the arms stay SEPARATE (correct `ECond`/`EMatch` branch count, not merely that the module parsed), all RED pre-fix (parse error) / GREEN post-fix. `docs/pattern-matching.md`'s own Cond/Guards examples (which showed the clean paren-free form and thus previously did not parse) now compile and run correctly. **406 compiler (+6) / 230 eval / 389 codegen / 804 stdlib / 29 snapshots pass.**
- ✅ **Compiled `println(:atom)` / `show(atom)` — link failure + typecheck gap for `Show(Atom)` (2026-07-05)** — `println(:ok)` ran fine interpreted (`VAtom a -> ":" ^ a`) but failed to LINK compiled (`Undefined symbols: "_show", referenced from _march_main / _println$Atom`): `Atom` was absent from BOTH `lib/tir/lower.ml`'s builtin `Show` injection (`show_specs`) and `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s `builtin_impls`, so a `show(atom)` call resolved to a bare, undefined `show` symbol. Same class as the tuple/`None` `_show` link failures (P1 below) but for atoms. Atoms compile to nameless FNV-1a i64 hashes (`Llvm_ctx.atom_hash`), so `Show$Atom.show` cannot reconstruct `:name` from the runtime value — the fix collects every atom `(hash, name)` seen during codegen (`emit_atom` + `emit_case`, into a new `ctx.atom_names`) and emits a generated `@march_atom_to_string(i64)` switch (returning `march_string_lit(":name")`, mirroring `march_int_to_string`'s RC shape) at module finalization via `Llvm_toplevel.emit_atom_show_table`, gated on the call actually appearing in the emitted IR (robust to the `atom_to_string` body being inlined). Emitted into `ctx.extra_fns` so BOTH the AOT (`emit_module`) and JIT/REPL (`llvm_repl.ml`, all five emitters) finalizers define the symbol in-module — JIT fragments are compiled standalone, so a call with no in-module definition is a clang error. Also added `atom_to_string` to `lib/tir/defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` (else it's treated as an unknown free var → `ECallPtr`, which leaf-detection flags non-leaf → a spurious reduction check in `Show$Atom.show`, breaking `phase4_reduction_codegen` "all-leaf module"). 2 new compiled-parity regression tests in `test/test_codegen.ml` (`iface_impl_mono_codegen` suite): `println(:ok)` and multi-atom `show(:hello)++show(:world_123)`, both RED pre-fix (link failure) / GREEN post-fix, verified interp == compiled; plus a `Slow` REPL/JIT parity test `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` (`repl_compiler_parity` "atom show", `show(:ok)`/`show(:hello_world)`/`show(:ok)++show(:err)`) pinning the JIT-fragment path that regressed to an undefined `@march_atom_to_string` during the fix. **400 compiler / 230 eval / 387 codegen (+2) / 804 stdlib (+1) / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots pass; generated IR verified clean by `opt -passes=verify`.**
- ✅ **`float_to_string` interpreter-vs-compiled divergence on whole-number Floats (2026-07-05)** — surfaced while writing golden conformance programs for `specs/lang/core-march.md` (Core March spec). The same source `float_to_string(1.0)` printed **three different strings** across backends: `1.` interpreted (`eval.ml` OCaml `string_of_float`, the declared reference semantics), `1` compiled (`runtime/march_runtime.c` used C `%g`, which drops a bare `.0` AND caps at 6 sig-figs), and `1.0` wasm (`runtime/march_runtime_wasm.c`'s hand-rolled formatter). JS (`runtime/march_runtime.mjs`, `toPrecision(6)`) gave `1`. Beyond whole numbers this was a full **precision** divergence too — `float_to_string(0.123456789)` was `0.123456789` interpreted vs `0.123457` compiled (12- vs 6-sig-fig). Decision: **match the reference** (§0/§1 of the spec: `eval.ml` IS the reference, and the golden oracle's contract is compiled == interpreter), i.e. OCaml-style `1.`, rather than changing the interpreter to a conventional `1.0` (which would mutate the reference and every doctest depending on it). Fix reproduces `string_of_float` exactly = `valid_float_lexem (format_float "%.12g" f)`: `runtime/march_runtime.c` now `snprintf("%.12g")` + appends `.` when every char is a digit/`-` (fixes whole-number AND precision divergence, byte-identical to interp); `runtime/march_runtime_wasm.c` trims all trailing fractional zeros to the bare `.` (`1.0`→`1.`; freestanding 6-decimal precision cap for fractional values is a documented deferral); `runtime/march_runtime.mjs` mirrors via `toPrecision(12)` + trailing-dot rule (extreme-exponent fixed-vs-exp notation edge deferred). The REPL/JIT value-display formatters (`lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`'s two `%g` sites) are a distinct value-inspector surface (not the `float_to_string` builtin; pinned by `test/repl_smoke_test.sh`) and are intentionally left — noted as a remaining related inconsistency. Differential test: new golden `specs/lang/golden/g09_float_show.march` (auto-discovered by BOTH `specs/lang/golden/verify.sh` and the `@oracle` sweep `test/test_oracle.ml`), RED before / GREEN after (`9/9` golden match; oracle `g09` MATCH, 0 un-triaged failures). `march_value_to_string` (generic `to_string`) has no float branch — the builtin path funnels solely through `@march_float_to_string`, so this one symbol was the whole compiled surface. `specs/lang/core-march.md` §5 updated (8→9 golden). **400 compiler / 230 eval / 385 codegen / 803 stdlib pass; golden 9/9; oracle 0 un-triaged failures.**
- ✅ **Go-style Linux cross-compilation — P3: OpenSSL/TLS + zlib/gzip (2026-07-07)** — `march --compile --target linux/amd64|linux/arm64` (and `forge build --target …`) now cross-link TLS (OpenSSL 3) and gzip (zlib) into the main binary, so a real app (forgepm) that uses native HTTPS + gzip tarball extraction no longer fails to link with `undefined symbol: march_tls_write / march_gzip_decode` (the P1 pure-compute behaviour). **Sysroot fetch:** new `scripts/fetch-cross-sysroot.sh <amd64|arm64>` populates `~/.cache/march/cross-sysroot/linux-<arch>/{lib,include}` from Debian **bookworm** `libssl3`/`libssl-dev`/`zlib1g`/`zlib1g-dev` `.deb`s via `ar`+`tar` (no `dpkg-deb`), resolving exact filenames from the suite `Packages.gz` index (pinned to bookworm; pinned fallbacks if offline) so the soname/ABI matches `debian:bookworm-slim` (`libssl.so.3`/`libcrypto.so.3`/`libz.so.1`), and copies the multiarch `openssl/opensslconf.h` (Debian ships it under `usr/include/<multiarch>/openssl`, not `usr/include/openssl`). **Compiler** (`bin/main.ml`): a `cross_sysroot_dir` resolver (env override `MARCH_CROSS_SYSROOT_<ARCH>`/`MARCH_CROSS_SYSROOT` → cache) with a clear actionable error on cache-miss (names the fetch script; no silent download); the `is_cross` link block now KEEPS `march_tls.c` + `march_compress.c` (they cross-compile against the target headers) and links OpenSSL/zlib by **direct positional `.so` paths** (`<sr>/lib/libssl.so.3` etc. — lld can't find them via `-l:`) with `-I<sr>/include` and no `-L/opt/homebrew` host paths; still drops `march_blake3.c` (needs a target `libblake3` we don't vendor) + `march_reload.c` (HCR-over-cross out of scope); zstd/brotli stay off (zlib is the only mandatory codec). **glibc floor 2.31→2.36** (`parse_target`): mandatory for TLS — the target `libcrypto.so.3` references `GLIBC_2.34`/`2.33` symbols a lower floor's libc lacks, so `ld.lld --no-allow-shlib-undefined` rejects the link; 2.36 matches bookworm (`zig_target` already appends the floor). **Portability implication:** cross binaries now require glibc ≥ 2.36 (bookworm+). **CAS:** a 12-hex digest of the three sysroot `.so` is folded into `cas_flags` (both the source-level early cache and the inner link cache) so a sysroot re-fetch invalidates cached cross binaries (glibc floor already in `target_label`). **Verification** (structure-only on the Mac — the Linux ELF can't run there): the TLS+gzip probe cross-links to a valid `ELF 64-bit … x86-64 … GNU/Linux` (and `ARM aarch64` for arm64) whose `DT_NEEDED` lists `libssl.so.3`/`libcrypto.so.3`/`libz.so.1`; forgepm's `.ll` references the full `march_tls_*`/`march_gzip_*` API (all previously undefined-symbol errors, now resolved — forgepm's linux/amd64 build reaches link-parity with its native build, failing only on a pre-existing unrelated `PubSub` module-qualification issue that native also hits). New regression test `test/test_codegen.ml` (`cross_compile` suite) cross-compiles the TLS+gzip program and asserts the ELF type + all three `DT_NEEDED` sonames, SKIPPING cleanly (tool-absence ledger) when zig or the sysroot cache is absent. Runtime (not just link) validation of the HTTPS/gzip round-trip happens on the actual droplet at deploy time. Spec `specs/2026-07-04-cross-compile-linux-hot-deploy-design.md` §5/Phasing updated. **Follow-ups:** P2 (`forge deploy hot --target`); optional zstd/brotli codecs; soname-manifest + glibc-floor deploy-time runtime checks; arm64 verified structurally but not run under QEMU. **445 compiler / 231 eval / 393 codegen (+1) / 804 stdlib / 29 snapshots pass** (full `dune build` first — the ~15 "cannot find runtime/march_runtime.c" stdlib fixtures are the documented partial-build artifact, green after a complete build; native/JS compile paths byte-identical, change scoped to `is_cross`).

- ✅ **Final-review fixes: `in_tail` blind spot in mutual-TCO group formation + REPL desugar-diagnostic rendering (2026-07-02)** — Three items from a final whole-branch review pass over Wave 1. **(1)** `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` `has_non_tail_group_call`'s dec-chain-wrapper arm (`ELet (tmp, EApp (f, _), dec-chain-returning-tmp)`, the Perceus post-call-DecRC wrapper recognizer shared with `tail_calls_in`) matched at ANY position without checking `in_tail`, always treating the wrapped call as tail regardless of where it actually sat. A group member that tail-calls its partner (forming a legitimate SCC edge) but ALSO non-tail-calls the same partner elsewhere (Perceus-wrapped, result bound and fed into further computation) would pass `find_mutual_tco_groups`'s "no non-tail call" gate — the mutual-TCO back-edge emitted for that pair then strands the continuation in a dead block. Fixed by checking `List.mem f.Tir.v_name group && not in_tail` in that arm too, mirroring the bare-`EApp` arm above. New regression test `test/test_codegen.ml` "final-review: non-tail dec-chain-wrapped group call rejected" (`mutual_tco_codegen` suite): `build_loop`/`consume_loop` where one branch is a genuine tail call (forms the SCC edge) and a sibling branch dec-chain-wraps the same call but binds the result to `r` and returns `r + 1` (non-tail) — confirmed RED pre-fix (`mutual_loop` wrongly formed) / GREEN post-fix (rejected), by temporarily reverting the one-line guard. **(2)** `lib/repl/repl.ml`: `run_simple`'s `ReplExpr`/`ReplLetQ` branches call `March_desugar.Desugar.desugar_expr` outside any local `try/with`, so a desugar-time diagnostic (e.g. the B6 pipe-into-match check, which raises `March_errors.Errors.ParseError`) escaped to the loop's outermost catch-all and printed a bare `internal error: March_errors.Errors.ParseError(...)` instead of the span-rendered diagnostic the batch driver (`bin/main.ml`) shows for the same error. Added a `last_src` ref (set alongside the existing per-form `src` binding) and a `ParseError` case in the outer catch-all that calls `March_errors.Errors.render_parse_error` the same way `bin/main.ml`'s `fmt_file`/compile paths do. New subprocess-based test `test/test_eval.ml` "final-review: renders desugar ParseError, not internal error" (`repl integration` suite) pipes the repro through the real `march repl` binary (the lightweight `repl_eval_exprs` helper used by the other REPL tests calls `desugar_expr` directly and would not reproduce the escape) — confirmed RED/GREEN the same way. **(3)** Stale comment in `test/imports/record_native/rn_entry.march:9` said the erased-shape `EUpdate` fallback "routes through march_record_put"; corrected to `march_record_update_dyn` (the actual B5 fix). **383 compiler / 227 eval (+1) / 335 codegen (+1) / 788 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march pass. RC benchmarks (tree_transform=104857600, list_ops=333333666666, binary_trees standard output, mutual_recursion even/odd N=10M + state machine + collatz) all compiled+run correctly; `bench/mutual_recursion.march`'s legitimate mutual groups confirmed still forming (`mutual_loop` present in `--emit-llvm` output) — the `in_tail` fix does not reject genuine mutual tail recursion.**
- ✅ **Three P0 items from the 2026-07-01 pipeline deep review (2026-07-01).** **(1) Differential oracle skipped compiled crashes** (`test/test_properties.ml` `oracle_check`): a signal-killed compiled binary (exit ≥ 128 via `sh`: SIGABRT=134, SIGSEGV=139) when the interpreter succeeded is now a FAILURE with the signal number in the error output, not a skip; clean nonzero error exits (and `timeout`'s 124) still skip. **(2) Perceus dual-position owned+borrowed double-consumption** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`, EApp + ECallPtr-extern `post_dec_vars` sites): a var passed at both an owned and a borrowed position of the same call, dead afterwards, was consumed twice (owned transfer + borrowed post-call dec) against the caller's single reference — `both(s, s, 1)` with `both(a:own, b:borrow, n)` aborted exit 134 ("RC underflow") compiled while the interpreter was fine. Normal calls now add one balancing `EIncRC` and keep the post-dec; self calls drop the post-dec (TCO makes the trailing dec dead code — an inc would leak per iteration). **(3) FBIP `same_arity` conflated type-parameter count with constructor field count** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`): the dead-binding FBIP path fed a binding's raw declared type, so a dead 1-field cell of a multi-param type was reused for a wider constructor — heap overflow past the cell (observed as the compiled binary printing the wrong match arm). `add_scrutinee_free_for`'s arity encoding now carries an unforgeable `$fbip$` marker and `same_arity` accepts only marked encodings; `Borrow.has_matching_alloc` audited (name-prefix ownership gate only — no arity use, no change needed). Compiled Slow regression tests (parity + exit 0) in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`; `fbip_p8` unit tests updated + `same_arity_raw_type_refused` added in `test/test_codegen.ml`. **372 compiler / 224 eval / 323 codegen / 790 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 36 properties pass; tree_transform/list_ops/binary_trees outputs and timings unchanged.**
- ✅ **MARCH_SANITIZE binaries aborted at process exit on macOS arm64 (2026-07-01)** — ASAN-linked binaries printed correct output then died with SIGTRAP/exit 133: `AddressSanitizer failed to deallocate 0x10000 bytes ... (error code: 22)` + `CHECK failed: sanitizer_common.cpp:81 "unable to unmmap"`. Root cause (lldb backtrace: `_pthread_exit → _pthread_tsd_cleanup → AsanThread::Destroy → UnsetAlternateSignalStack → UnmapOrDie`): the scheduler's `setup_alt_stack()` (`runtime/march_scheduler.c`) installed a **malloc'd** 64 KiB per-thread `sigaltstack`, replacing the mmap'd altstack ASAN installs on every thread; at thread exit ASAN blindly `munmap()`s whatever altstack is current — our non-page-aligned malloc'd buffer → EINVAL → CHECK-abort. Fix: under ASAN (`__has_feature(address_sanitizer)` / `__SANITIZE_ADDRESS__`) `setup_alt_stack()` is a no-op — ASAN's own altstack is SA_ONSTACK-capable and serves the lazy stack-growth SIGSEGV handler (verified: 2000-deep non-tail recursion grows the 4 KiB initial stack and exits 0 under ASAN). Non-sanitized builds unchanged (preprocessed out). Regression test (Slow, compiled): "MARCH_SANITIZE binary exits 0" in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` — red without the fix (exit 133), green with it. Also fixed a fresh-checkout build failure: `lib/ed25519/dune` foreign stubs needed `(extra_deps %{project_root}/runtime/tweetnacl.h)` (the header was never copied into `_build`; main repo built only via a stale `_build`). **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen / 53 stdlib_march pass.**
- ✅ **Compiled-backend RC/codegen bugs corrupting `(String, TomlValue)` pair lists — Toml `get_str` returned wrong values (2026-06-28)** — `Toml.parse` of a `[section]` with 4+ keys then `get_str` returned a sibling key's NAME (or crashed with OOM / RC underflow) **only when compiled**; the interpreter was always correct, so this blocked compiled `forge.toml` parsing. Three independent compiler bugs combined to corrupt the pair list, all fixed: **(1) TCO mis-applied to non-tail self-calls** (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) — the loop back-edge fired for EVERY self `EApp` in a TCO'd function, including a non-tail one inside `Cons(x, f(t))` (an ELet rhs in ANF), silently dropping the surrounding construction. Added `ctx.tco_in_tail` (set at TCO body entry, cleared around ELet rhs / ESeq prefix) and gated the back-edge on it. **(2) Niche/newtype scrutinee double-free** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`) — for niche- (`Some(x)≡x`) / newtype- (`S(x)≡x`) represented scrutinees the payload branch var IS the scrutinee object, so the separate `add_scrutinee_free_for` dec double-freed it (toml `get_str`'s `Some(TStr(s))`). Skip the scrutinee free when `Repr.repr_of_ty` is `Newtype`/`Niche`. **(3) Borrow inference over-owned linear-search helpers** (`lib/tir/borrow.ml`) — `owned_in` treated a `let p = name` alias (Lower's destructured-field binding, often behind sibling/join-point bindings) as an *owning* use, so `table_get`/`tget` were inferred `pairs:own` and consumed/freed the list spine + elements that `set_nested`/`table_has` then reused. Fixed `owned_in`'s `ELet` to flow through an alias (`owned_in v body`) instead of marking the source owned. Exposed a 4th issue (escaping field of a borrowed-derived tuple not inc'd; dead tuple-field dec corrupting a borrowed list) fixed in `perceus.ml` by treating `TTuple`/`TRecord` scrutinees as borrowed (they are `needs_rc=false`, so fields belong to the never-freed aggregate). Regression tests: compiled guard `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` "Toml [section] with 4 keys…" (interpreter tests can't catch a compiled-only bug); eval-level "Section with many keys (RC regression)" in `test/stdlib/test_toml.march`. Also fixed a stale `test/stdlib/test_dataframe.march` (commit e1e5b589's `IntCol`→`NativeIntArr` migration missed it — `typed_array_from_list`→`native_int_arr_from_list`, `typed_array_get`→`native_int_arr_get`) that blocked `forge test`. **320 codegen / 224 eval / 372 compiler / 787 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march pass; benchmarks leak-neutral.**

- ✅ **Refinement types: record param refinements in postcondition VCs — Gap #1 Part B (2026-06-25)** — `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`: the `scope` entry gains an optional record-sort tag `(binder, pred, sort_opt)`. `refined_scope_ty` admits both `Int` and registered-TyCon record refinements. `scope_add_param`/`scope_add_binding` use it; `sig_of_clause` keeps `refined_int_ty` (Decision R3 — no call-site VCs for record params). `scope_facts` returns `(decls, assumptions, has_record: bool)`: record entries declare an opaque `SData sort_name` const, build a `make_field_resolver`, and reflect the predicate via field selectors. `check_post` triggers the preamble when RETURN OR scope is record-sorted; a composite `scope_field_resolver` threads into `scalar` so `old.count` in the return resolves via selectors. Added `march --version`; install.sh uses it as a post-install run check. Bootstrap URL switched to stable `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../main/install.sh`. Docs corrected: `march --compile` needs clang/LLVM. Unit tests for `Toolchain.platform_of_uname` + `expected_hash_for`.

Release archives now bundle `runtime/*.c`+`*.h` (`build.yml`) so `march --compile` works from a prebuilt. Fixed `github.com/march-lang/*` → `march-language` (org renamed), standardized the checksums file to `<hash>  <file>`, updated release-note wording to `forge toolchain`, and dropped the phantom `darwin-x86_64` manifest entry. Verified end-to-end on darwin-arm64 against the live nightly (download→verify→install→interpret→native compile; all four toolchain subcommands).
- ✅ **`lib/refinecheck/return_infer.ml` — Z3 return-refinement inference (2026-06-25)** — New `March_refinecheck.Return_infer` module: given a function with Int-refined parameters, probes 6 sign candidates (`r > 0`, `r >= 0`, `r >= 1`, `r != 0`, `r < 0`, `r <= -1`) for the return value via Z3. Single-pass: (1) collects refined params → `(decls, assumptions)`; (2) walks block body for let-binding equalities; (3) reflects return expression as SMT term; (4) calls `Refine.discharge` for each candidate. Result type `inferred_return = { fn_name; verified_preds }`. Public API: `infer_module ?root m`. If Z3 is absent, returns `[]` (no false positives). 6 new tests in `return_refine_infer` suite. **359 compiler tests pass.**
- ✅ **`lib/refinecheck/cap_infer.ml` — standalone capability-inference hint pass (2026-06-25)** — New `March_refinecheck.Cap_infer` module walks function bodies and `DLet` bindings after typecheck, maps direct calls to capability-requiring builtins (`cap_table`, ~90 entries mirroring `builtin_cap_table` in typecheck.ml) against the module's declared `needs`, and emits `Err.hint` (not warning/error) at the exact call site. Deduplicates per-capability-per-module to suppress repeated hints. Respects the IO hierarchy: `needs IO` covers all `IO.*` children (`cap_subsumes`). Recurses into `DMod` children with their own `DNeeds` scope. Wired into `bin/main.ml` at both `--check` and `--compile` call sites (immediately after `Refine_check.check_module`). `march_refinecheck` added to `march_test_compiler` deps in `test/dune`. `has_hints`/`has_hint_with` helpers added to `test/test_helpers.ml`. 7 new `cap_infer` tests in `test/test_compiler.ml`; all 328 compiler tests pass.
- ✅ **Perceus RC use-after-free on cross-module opaque types** — a value whose concrete type isn't propagated across a module boundary (e.g. `let gate = Gate.cast(...)` where `Gate` is an opaque type from another module) keeps an unresolved type-var `'_NNNN` in monomorphic TIR. `needs_rc (TVar _)` returned `false` ("skip RC"), so Perceus never emitted an `EIncRC` for such a heap value; when the binding was consumed twice (e.g. two `Gate.get_change(gate, …)` calls) the first consume freed the box and the second double-freed it (`RC underflow in march_decrc_freed`, bastion `Gate.cast` test). Fix: `needs_rc (TVar _) = true` (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`) — `llvm_ty (TVar _) = "ptr"`, so the value is a heap pointer and must be RC-managed; safe for genuine scalars because RC ops are guarded by `if ty = "ptr"` in llvm_emit and `IS_HEAP_PTR` in the runtime. Mirrors the earlier `TFn` fix. Regression test: `perceus` group "incrc for unresolved-tvar value used twice (cross-module opaque)" in `test/test_march.ml`.
