## P1 — High Impact / Near-Term

### Compiler (found during Core March language-spec Phase 1 golden corpus, 2026-07-05)

- [ ] **Nested tuple destructured in a block `let` fails to compile (`use of undefined value` in emitted LLVM).** Found by the Core March golden corpus (Phase 1 Task 2, tuples; chip `task_2bf891d1`). `let ((a, b), (c, d)) = ((1, 2), (3, 4))` inside a `fn main() do … end` block runs correctly interpreted (exit 0, prints `10` for `a+b+c+d`) but the compiled path emits invalid LLVM — `error: use of undefined value '@a'`, clang rejects the `.ll`, exit 1. **Isolated:** a FLAT tuple in `let` (`let (a, b) = (1, 2)`) compiles fine; a NESTED tuple via `match` (`match ((1,2),(3,4)) do ((a,b),(c,d)) -> …`) compiles fine. ONLY nested-`PatTuple`-in-block-`let` breaks — so the bug is in the `ELet`/`EBlock` pattern-destructuring lowering path (`lib/tir/lower.ml`), which appears to emit a reference to an inner sub-pattern variable (`a`) without binding it, unlike the `match_pattern`/`EMatch` lowering (which recurses into nested sub-patterns correctly). The golden corpus keeps `g16_tuple_nested.march` on the `match` form to stay green; a nested-tuple-in-`let` golden program should be added once fixed. Repro confirmed by hand (interp 0 vs compile-exit-1).
- [ ] **`>=`/`<=` as an `ECond` (scrutinee-less `match do`) arm condition fails to parse.** Found by the Core March golden corpus (Phase 1 Task 7, conditionals). In a `match do cond -> body … end` boolean chain, an arm whose condition uses `>=` or `<=` (e.g. `s >= 90 -> …`) is a parse error, while `>` / `<` (e.g. `s > 89 -> …`) parse fine. Root cause is in the token filter's arm-boundary lookahead (the `->`-scan distinguishing arm boundaries from block continuations gets stuck on the two-char `>=`/`<=` operator before the next arm's `->`). Interpreter and compiler are both unaffected once it parses — a pure front-end (lexer/token-filter) limitation, not a semantics divergence. Workaround: use `>`/`<` with an adjusted threshold. Lower severity (clean workaround) but a real surface-syntax gap worth a token-filter fix.

### Compiler (found during Core March language-spec typing track, 2026-07-05)

- [x] ✅ **RESOLVED (2026-07-05, commit `f0f5299c`) — `let`-binding type annotations (`let x : T = e`) are parsed but never enforced by the typechecker.** Fix in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`: new helper `infer_let_annotated` (used at both `infer_block`'s `ELet` arm and the tail-position `ELet` arm) resolves `bind_ty` via `surface_ty` and CHECKs the RHS against it with `check_expr` (a proper checking context, so a polymorphic RHS bound at a more specific instance still works, e.g. `let f : (Int) -> Int = fn x -> x`). The annotation is resolved into a scratch error context first; if `surface_ty` can't express it (e.g. a phantom/typestate tag `Handle(Open)` where `Open` is a data constructor, not a type name), it falls back to plain inference — preserving the pre-fix behaviour for grammar-inexpressible annotations so no legitimate program (e.g. the `tag_and_typestate` tests) starts being rejected. Corpus: `reject/t16_let_annot_mismatch` (`let x : Int = "foo"` rejected) + `accept/t21_let_annot_ok`. Unit tests in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`let_annotations` group). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §4.1 finding 16 (marked RESOLVED). ORIGINAL FINDING: Found by the Core March typing corpus (typing Task 2, tuples/records). The parser accepts a type annotation on a `let` binding and stores it as `Ast.bind_ty`, but `typecheck.ml` never consulted it — `grep -c bind_ty lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` = 0. The binding's type is fully *inferred* from the RHS and the annotation is silently discarded, so `let x : Int = "foo"` and `let pair : (Int, Int) = (1, 2, 3)` both `--check` at exit 0 (independently confirmed by the typing-Task-2 implementer and reviewer). This is **not a soundness hole** — the inferred type still governs, so a later *use* of `x` at the annotated-but-wrong type is still caught; the annotation is merely decorative rather than a checking context. Lower severity (no miscompile, no crash), but a real enforcement/ergonomics gap: a programmer writing `let x : Int = compute()` reasonably expects the annotation to *check* the RHS against `Int` (a `check_expr env e (surface_ty bind_ty)` at the `ELet` arm, `typecheck.ml` ~:4293), and an annotation that documents-but-doesn't-verify is a footgun. The typing corpus routes around it — the tuple-arity reject (`specs/lang/types/reject/t08_tuple_arity_mismatch.march`) uses a *function-parameter* annotation (which IS enforced via param-vs-arg unification) rather than a `let` annotation to elicit the mismatch. Fix: at the `ELet` arm, when `bind_ty` is present, check the RHS against it (`check_expr`) instead of (or in addition to) inferring, and unify the pattern against the annotated type. A typing-corpus `reject/` program pinning `let x : Int = "foo"` should be added once this is enforced.
- [x] ✅ **RESOLVED (2026-07-05, commit `8cbd6dd2`) — An explicit `when Interface(a)` bound on a generic function parameter is not re-checked at call sites — only enforced when it happens to be violated at the function's OWN declaration (SOUNDNESS bug).** ROOT CAUSE (pinned this task): in `check_fn`'s `when`-clause processing, the constraint argument `a` in `when Eq(a)` was resolved ONLY against `fn_tvars` (signature type-variable names). For an UNANNOTATED value parameter named `a`, `fn_tvars` has no `"a"` entry, so the code minted a FRESH, disconnected placeholder var and attached `CInterface("Eq", …)` to THAT — a variable never unified with the parameter's actual type. The constraint therefore rode on a phantom var: `generalize` quantified it away, and at each call site `instantiate` substituted it with an independent fresh var that never bound to the argument type, so `discharge_constraints` always saw an `Unbound` TVar and skipped it ("still polymorphic"). FIX (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`): when the `when`-clause argument name is not a signature type var, resolve it against the value-parameter binding in `body_env` (`lookup_var`) and attach the constraint to the parameter's own type variable; it then survives generalization onto the scheme, rides through `instantiate` onto the caller's `pending_constraints`, and is discharged at the call site. Falls back to a fresh registered placeholder only when the name is neither a signature type var nor a bound value. SAFETY VALVE verified: bug now rejects (`` `Hue` does not implement interface `Eq`. ``); satisfiable generic constraints still accept interpreted AND compiled (`max(1,2)→2`, `same(1,1)`, `same("x","y")`); unconstrained generics unaffected; full fast suite (408 compiler / 230 eval) + types corpus (39/39) green. NOTE: user-defined `impl Eq`/`derive Eq` on a locally-shadowed ADT name (e.g. `impl Eq(Color)` for a module-local `Color`) is a SEPARATE pre-existing limitation (app-type-namespace collision — `expected \`Color\` but got \`Color\``) that already rejected before this fix and is out of scope. Corpus: `reject/t17_generic_when_constraint_unsatisfied` + `accept/t22_generic_when_constraint_satisfied`. Unit tests in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`generic_when_constraints` group). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §4.1 finding 15 (marked RESOLVED). ORIGINAL FINDING: Found by the Core March typing corpus (typing Task 6, interface constraints). Repro: `fn same(a, b) when Eq(a) do a == b end` (params left UNANNOTATED, so `a` stays a generic type variable at `same`'s own declaration) then `same(Rood, Rood)`, where `Rood` is a variant of a bare ADT `type Hue = Rood | Bloo` with no `impl Eq(Hue)`, **typechecks at exit 0** — even though a direct `Rood == Rood` (bypassing the `same` wrapper) correctly rejects with `` `Hue` does not implement interface `Eq`. `` Contrast: `fn same(a : Hue, b : Hue) when Eq(a) do a == b end` (params CONCRETELY annotated) correctly rejects at `same`'s own declaration with the identical message — so the constraint is genuinely attached to `same`'s scheme (`check_fn`'s `all_constraints` branch, `typecheck.ml:4752–4757`, folds the `when`-clause's `CInterface` into the returned `Poly` scheme when the type variable isn't otherwise resolved) but is not re-verified when `instantiate` (`typecheck.ml:901–937`) substitutes a concrete type in for that quantified variable at a later call site and pushes the freshly-instantiated constraint onto the caller's `env.pending_constraints` — the caller's own `discharge_constraints` call (`typecheck.ml:4932–5049`) should then see and reject `CInterface("Eq", Hue)`, and does not. Root cause not pinned down (docs-only task, `typecheck.ml` unmodified): likely implicates `generalize`'s fresh-isolated-ref-per-quantified-id copy (`typecheck.ml:845–895`, esp. :866–869) interacting with the ORIGINAL constraint-bearing `TVar` ref captured earlier while processing the `when`-clause (`typecheck.ml:4687–4712`) — both share an integer id (which `instantiate`'s `List.assoc_opt id subst`, :915, keys on), so in principle the constraint should still resolve correctly; something breaks that between the immediate-declaration path (works) and the deferred generic-call path (silently drops). Reproduces identically for a plain ADT value and (separately confirmed) is not `TArrow`/function-value-specific. This is a genuine, if narrow, typechecker SOUNDNESS gap for user-declared `when`-bounds specifically (the four BUILT-IN interface constraints arising from `+`/`==`/`<`/`show` themselves are unaffected — those are always checked at THEIR OWN enclosing declaration, never re-instantiated through an intermediate user-defined polymorphic wrapper function the way `same` is here). Full live-verified repro and analysis in `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §4 finding 15.

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — interfaces/impls declaration checking, Task 4, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **No impl-coherence check: overlapping impls of the same interface for the same type are silently accepted, and the interpreter/compiled backends disagree — deterministically, but DIFFERENTLY — on which impl's method body runs.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.4.3 (the "Known divergence: impl coherence" subsection) and `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.3's `(T-Impl)` step 1 note, widening the interface/impl declaration-checking reference (`.superpowers/sdd/interface-impl-survey.md` §4, findings D1–D3). `env.impls : ty list StrMap.t` (`typecheck.ml:455`) registers every impl by unconditional prepend (`typecheck.ml:7081`–`7084`) with no lookup-before-insert step anywhere in `(T-Impl)`'s ordered checks — two `impl Speak(Dog)` blocks for the identical `(interface, type)` pair both typecheck with **no diagnostic of any kind** (confirmed live, `--check` exit 0, only unrelated "unused variable `self`" warnings).

  **Minimal repro** (confirmed live this task, both backends, 2 runs each for determinism):
  ```march
  mod M do
    interface Speak(a) do
      fn speak : a -> String
    end

    type Dog = Dog(String)

    impl Speak(Dog) do
      fn speak(self) do
        "FIRST"
      end
    end

    impl Speak(Dog) do
      fn speak(self) do
        "SECOND"
      end
    end

    fn main() do
      println(speak(Dog("Rex")))
    end
  end
  ```
  ```
  $ march --check file.march        # exit 0, both backends' shared typechecker accepts
  $ march file.march                # interpreted: SECOND
  $ march --compile -o /tmp/ovl file.march && /tmp/ovl   # compiled: FIRST
  ```

  **Root cause, both selection sites cited live:**
  - Interpreter (last-registered wins): `DImpl`'s eval handler does `Hashtbl.replace impl_tbl (idef.impl_iface.txt, type_name) fn_val` (`lib/eval/eval.ml:8324`, mirrored at the letrec-style path `lib/eval/eval.ml:8640`); for a user interface (not on the `Show`/`Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash` allowlist) the real effect is the adjacent ordinary `env`-binding branch (`eval.ml:8309`–`8311`) prepending a fresh `("speak", v)` binding per impl, so ordinary lexical "first binding in `ρ` wins" scoping resolves to whichever impl was declared LAST (closest to the front).
  - Compiled backend (first-registered wins): TIR lowering's `collect_iface_impls` (`lib/tir/lower.ml:1005`–`1014`) reads `already = List.mem_assoc type_name l` against the accumulating dispatch table BEFORE registering, and only lowers/registers `if not already` (`lib/tir/lower.ml:1014`) — the FIRST impl for a given `(iface, type)` claims the slot; every subsequent overlapping impl is silently skipped.

  Confirmed the same divergence holds for **generic-vs-specific overlap** (`impl Speak(Box(a))` + `impl Speak(Box(Int))`, called on `Box(42)`: interpreted `"int box"`, compiled `"generic box"` — whichever backend "looks right" is accidental, since neither implements real specificity resolution) and **derive-vs-manual overlap** (`derive Show, Eq for Color` + hand-written `impl Eq(Color) do fn eq(a,b) do true end end`, `Red == Blue`: interpreted `true`, compiled `false`).

  **This is an OPEN divergence, deliberately left unfixed by the documentation slice that found it** — unlike `core-march.md` §4.2.1's `ERecordUpdate` case (investigated, adjudicated, and CONVERGED; its todos entry is closed), there is no clearly-normative backend to converge on here. Fixing this requires an actual language-design decision: add a coherence/overlap-rejection check at `impl` declaration time (Rust-style), define one deterministic selection policy shared by both backends (e.g. always-first or always-last, formalized rather than incidental), or embrace intentionally-overlapping instances with an explicit specificity order (Haskell `OverlappingInstances`-style) — each a real design trade-off (e.g. backward compatibility with any code, such as test-mocking patterns, that happens to rely on the interpreter's current last-wins behavior). Filed here in the same spirit as the existing open `hash()`/`to_string` cross-backend entries above and `test/test_oracle.ml`'s `known_divergence` list (`test/test_oracle.ml:138`–`174`) — pinned and tracked, not silently fixed or hidden. Deliberately NOT added to `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/` (`check_types.sh`'s single-`--check`-invocation harness can't witness a runtime interp/compiled split — both backends accept the program at typecheck time; the divergence only appears once the program is actually RUN on each backend).

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — interfaces/impls declaration checking, Task 5, 2026-07-06)

- ✅ **finding 17 (FIXED, 2026-07-07) — `derive X for UnknownType` now ERRORs instead of silently no-opping.** Fix locus: `expand_derive`'s `None` branch (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`) now emits `Err.error` at the type-name span (`` Unknown type `%s` in `derive` — is it declared in this module? ``) before returning `[]`, mirroring the sibling unknown-derive-INTERFACE case. Witness: `specs/lang/types/reject/t49_derive_unknown_type.march` (`derive Show for NoSuchType`, EXPECT-ERROR `` Unknown type `NoSuchType` in `derive` ``) — flips exit 0 → exit 1. `check_types.sh` 104 → 107 (with F6/revoke_cap witnesses). Original finding below.

  **[ORIGINAL FINDING]** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.4 (the `derive`/`satisfy` widening section) and §4.1 finding 17, widening the interface/impl declaration-checking reference (`.superpowers/sdd/interface-impl-survey.md` §5, finding S1). `expand_derive`'s `None` branch (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml:1659`) is a bare `[]` when the derive TARGET TYPE isn't found in the module's own collected `type_defs` — no `Err.error` call, unlike the very same function's own unknown-derive-TARGET-INTERFACE case just a few lines away (`derive_impl`'s `_ -> Err.error …` catch-all, `desugar.ml:1641`–`1647`), and unlike `expand_satisfy`'s unknown-interface and missing-function checks (`desugar.ml:1687`–`1690`, `:1694`–`1699`), both of which DO reject.

  **Minimal repro** (confirmed live this task, both `--check` and RUN):
  ```march
  mod M do
    derive Eq for Ghost           -- `Ghost` is never defined anywhere

    fn main() do
      println("no error, no Ghost type defined")
    end
  end
  ```
  ```
  $ march --check file.march   # exit 0, no diagnostic
  $ march file.march           # exit 0, prints: no error, no Ghost type defined
  ```
  The `derive Eq for Ghost` line has zero observable effect — the program behaves exactly as if it were deleted. Confirmed reproducible; not test flakiness (re-run, same result).

  **Cited derive-expansion site:** `expand_derive` (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml:1651`–`1664`):
  ```ocaml
  match List.assoc_opt type_name.txt type_defs with
  | None -> []   (* type not found — silently skip *)          (* desugar.ml:1659 *)
  | Some (tparams, td) -> List.concat_map (fun iface_name -> …) ifaces
  ```

  **Why this is a real gap, not intentional leniency:** every OTHER "the target of a declaration doesn't exist" case documented in this widening slice IS rejected — `impl` of an undeclared interface (`core-march-types.md` §2.3 item 2, `` Unknown interface `NotDeclared` — is it declared above this impl? ``), `derive` of an undeclared INTERFACE (this same function, `` Unknown derive target `Frobnicate` for type `Color`. `` — confirmed live, `reject/t23_derive_unknown_interface`), and `satisfy` of an undeclared interface (`` Unknown interface `Bogus` in satisfy declaration. ``, confirmed live). The derive-unknown-TYPE case is the one asymmetric exception, and it fails silently rather than loudly — the worst direction for a typo (a misspelled type name in a `derive` line is currently indistinguishable from a no-op comment).

  Filed here in the same spirit as the impl-coherence entry immediately above (a real, pinned gap, deliberately left unfixed by this documentation-only slice) — fixing it (rejecting an unknown derive TARGET TYPE the same way an unknown derive target INTERFACE already is, i.e. an `Err.error` call in `expand_derive`'s `None` branch instead of `[]`) is a small, well-scoped compiler change, but out of scope here. Deliberately NOT added to `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/` as a `reject/` program — a `reject/` witness would assert behavior this finding identifies as WRONG (the program currently, incorrectly, `--check`s clean); once fixed, a `reject/derive_unknown_type`-style program should be added per `core-march-types.md` §4.1 finding 17's note.

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — actors: declaration + spawn typing, Task 1, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **`spawn(Actor)` returns `Pid[<fresh var>]`, NOT `Pid[state]` — the actor's state type never reaches an observable `Pid`.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.6.3 (the actor declaration + spawn typing widening) and §4.1 finding 18. The `ESpawn` typing arm returns `TCon ("Pid", [fresh_var env.level])` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml:4203`) — a fresh, unconstrained variable computed with no reference to the actor's state type. Separately, the `DActor` arm binds the actor NAME to `Pid[state_ty]` (`typecheck.ml:6821`), but that value binding is unreachable from a bare name occurrence because the nullary-constructor registration at `:6769` (actor name ⇒ type `<Name>`) shadows it — so a bare `Counter` is typed `Counter`, not `Pid[state]`.

  **Live probes (confirmed this task):**
  - `let p = if true do spawn(Counter) else spawn(Named) end` typechecks CLEAN with `Counter`'s state `{count:Int}` and `Named`'s state `{name:String}` — the two `if`-branches unify only because each `spawn` produced an independent free var; were the parameter the state type, T-If would reject them (`Pid({count:Int})` vs `Pid({name:String})`).
  - `is_alive(Counter)` (with `is_alive : Pid(a) -> Bool`) is REJECTED `` expected `Pid(r3)` but got `Counter` `` — the bare name resolves to the nullary ctor's `Counter` type, not the `:6821` `Pid[state]` value binding.

  **Compounding gap:** no surface annotation can pin the parameter either. The built-in `Pid` is registered at arity 1 (`typecheck.ml:1849`), but stdlib `GlobalPid` declares a 0-arity `type Pid = { … }` record (`stdlib/global_pid.march:11`); because March has a single global type namespace with no per-module type identity (`core-march-types.md` §2.5), that bare `Pid` shadows the built-in in `env.types`, so `Pid(T)` annotations reject `` `Pid` expects 0 type argument(s) but got 1. `` (arity check, `typecheck.ml:2348–2351`).

  **Net effect:** the state type IS checked inside the actor decl (`init` + each handler body checked to return the state record, `typecheck.ml:6785–6820`), but it does not propagate to any observable `Pid` at a `spawn` site — the `Pid` parameter stays a free var that unifies opportunistically with whatever surrounding builtins demand (`is_alive`, `kill`, `send`, …). Tightening `spawn`'s result to `Pid[state]` (or making the actor name resolve to its `:6821` binding rather than the `:6769` nullary ctor) is a compiler change, out of scope for this documentation-only slice. Deliberately NOT added to `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/` as a `reject/` program — there is no *incorrect* program to pin (the current fresh-var behavior is what it is, and `accept/t39_actor_spawn_pid` witnesses it); this is a faithfulness/expectation-gap note, not a rejectable typo.

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — actors: message payload typing + actor affinity, Task 3, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **`send(target, Msg(args))` does NOT check the message against the target actor's accepted-message set — a wrong-actor send typechecks (exit 0), then is silently DROPPED interpreted but MISROUTED (memory-unsafe) compiled.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.6.4 and §4.1 finding 19. **Distinct from finding 18** (the `Pid`-is-unparameterized-after-`spawn` gap above): finding 18 is about the *type the Pid carries*; THIS is about the `send` typing arm ignoring the target regardless of what the Pid carries. The `ESend` arm (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml:4177`) infers the target Pid's type and immediately discards it — `ignore (infer_expr env cap)` (`:4178`) — so `send` never consults *which actor* `cap` is. The only send-side check is `check_sendable` (`typecheck.ml:3335`), which walks the message type and errors solely on a `RingBuf`-family constructor (`let non_sendable_types = ["RingBuf"]`, `:3331`) — NOT a message-acceptance check. The message payload *shape* IS checked (ordinary `ECon` constructor typing via `let msg_ty = infer_expr env msg`, `:4179`, witnessed by `reject/t29_actor_send_wrong_payload`: `send(counter, Inc("x"))` where `Inc` expects `Int` rejects `` expected `Int` but got `String`. ``), but *which handler the target actually has* is not.

  **Live repro (confirmed this task, BOTH backends).** `Counter` handles only `Inc(Int)`; `Logger` handles `Log(String)`. Send `Log("stray")` — a `Logger` message — to the `Counter` Pid:
  ```
  mod Main do
    actor Counter do
      state { count : Int }
      init  { count: 0 }
      on Inc(x : Int) do
        println("Counter.Inc -> " ++ int_to_string(state.count + x))
        { count: state.count + x }
      end
    end
    actor Logger do
      state { seen : Int }
      init  { seen: 0 }
      on Log(m : String) do
        println("Logger.Log -> " ++ m)
        { seen: state.seen + 1 }
      end
    end
    fn main() do
      let counter = spawn(Counter)
      send(counter, Inc(3))
      send(counter, Log("stray"))   -- Log is a Logger message, not Counter's
      run_until_idle()
      println("done")
    end
  end
  ```
  - `--check` exits **0** (accepts).
  - **Interpreted** (`main.exe file.march`) prints `Counter.Inc -> 3` then `done` — the stray `Log` matched no `Counter` handler NAME (`h.ah_msg.txt = msg_tag` string equality, `lib/eval/eval.ml:7545`) and was **silently dropped** (`None -> ()`, `eval.ml:7547-7549`).
  - **Compiled** (`--compile --opt 2`) prints `Counter.Inc -> 3`, then `Counter.Inc -> <garbage int, e.g. 2796564219>`, then `done` — the stray message was **MISROUTED** into `Counter`'s first handler slot. The compiled dispatch lowers each message to a per-actor `<Actor>_Msg` variant in handler-declaration order (`lib/tir/lower_actor.ml:17`) and dispatches by an `ECase` on that variant's discriminant with **no default arm** (`ECase(msg, branches, None)`, `lower_actor.ml:256`); a foreign message's discriminant indexes into the *target* actor's branch table, so `Log`'s slot hits `Counter.Inc`, whose body reinterprets the `String` payload pointer as an `Int` (non-deterministic, memory-unsafe).

  **Why filed separately from finding 18:** finding 18 = "the Pid is unparameterized / a `Pid(T)` annotation is impossible." This finding = "`send` does not gate the message by the target actor" — and it would remain true EVEN IF the Pid carried its state type, because `send` discards `τ_cap` entirely (`:4178`). Fixing it (typing Pids by their accepted-message set so `send` can gate the message) is a significant type-system design decision — deferred, out of scope for this docs-only slice. Deliberately NOT a `reject/` corpus program (it typechecks; a `reject/` witness would codify the WRONG behavior); the correctly-typed accept path is `accept/t40_actor_send_typed_payload`. The message-name flat global namespace that underlies the compiled misroute is a DESIGN POINT (analogous to `core-march-types.md` §2.5's no-per-module-type-namespace point), documented in §2.6.4 — NOT filed as a gap.

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — actors: lifecycle + epoch capabilities, Task 4, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **The capability / dead-actor `send` plane is NOT byte-identical interp vs compiled — the compiled epoch-`Cap` validation is non-functional.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.10.6 (the lifecycle + epoch-capability widening). The **live-actor message plane** (`spawn`/`send`/`receive`/`run_until_idle`/`is_alive`) agrees between backends (witnessed by golden `g35`–`g37`), but the dead-actor and capability paths diverge — verified live on both backends:
  - `send(dead_pid, Msg)` → interp `None` (drop) vs compiled `Some` (compiled does not implement the dead-actor drop).
  - `get_cap(dead_pid)` → interp `None` vs compiled `Some` (compiled `get_cap` does not gate on liveness).
  - `send_checked(cap, Msg)` → interp returns `:ok` for a valid live cap / `:error` for a dead/stale/revoked cap (`eval.ml:3137`); **compiled performs no epoch validation at all and returns an uninterned/garbage atom** (compares equal to neither `:ok` nor `:error`, `show`s as `:<atom>`) for EVERY cap, live or dead. So the entire epoch-`Cap` security mechanism (`VCap of int*int` `eval.ml:48`, `send_checked` `:3137`, `revoke_cap` `:3164`, epoch-staleness `:384–388`/`increment_epoch:1100`/restart-inherit `:1513–1517`) is documented for the **interpreter** only; it is not usable compiled. Because a divergent program cannot be a golden `MATCH`, no cap witness was added to `specs/lang/golden/`; the mechanism is documented in §4.10.6 prose + citations with the divergence table. Fixing it is compiled-runtime work (implement the dead-actor drop, liveness gate, and epoch validation in the compiled scheduler/runtime), out of scope for this documentation-only slice.

- ✅ **(FIXED, 2026-07-07) — `revoke_cap`/`is_cap_valid` are now typecheck-registered.** Fix locus: `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` builtin table now carries `revoke_cap : Cap(a) -> Atom` (returns the `:ok` atom) and `is_cap_valid : Cap(a) -> Bool`, next to `get_cap`/`send_checked`, in the `poly1` form. Witness: `specs/lang/types/accept/t58_revoke_cap_typechecks.march` (spawns an actor, `get_cap`s it, exercises both new builtins on the `Cap(a)` payload) — flips `` I cannot find `revoke_cap` `` → exit 0. NOTE: this is TYPECHECK registration only; the compiled-runtime cap-plane divergence documented directly above (compiled `send_checked`/`get_cap` non-functional) remains OPEN and out of scope. Original finding below.

  **[ORIGINAL FINDING]** `revoke_cap` (`lib/eval/eval.ml:3164`) and `is_cap_valid` (`:3172`) have no entry in the typecheck builtin table (only `get_cap`/`send_checked` are present, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml:1473–1474`), so a surface program calling `revoke_cap(cap)` is rejected `` I cannot find `revoke_cap`. `` This is a second, independent reason (on top of the compiled-runtime divergence above) that a `revoke_cap`-based golden witness is impossible today. Documented in §4.10.6. Fixing it is a small compiler change (add the two builtins to the typecheck table with `Cap(a) -> Atom` / `Cap(a) -> Bool` signatures), out of scope here.

### Compiler / runtime (found during Core March widening slice — actors: supervision, Task 5, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **`get_actor_field`/`pid_of_int` CRASH the compiled backend — external actor-state inspection is interp-only, and this BLOCKS compiled observation of supervised children.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.10.7 (the supervision widening). The compiled runtime's `march_get_actor_field` (`runtime/march_runtime.c:3329`) is a hard stub that ignores both arguments and returns `None` unconditionally (`(void)pid; (void)name; … tag 0 = None`), and `march_pid_of_int` (`:3324`) does an unsafe untagged int→pointer cast (`return (void *)(intptr_t)n;`). Verified live this task: `examples/supervision_strategies.march` typechecks and **interprets cleanly (exit 0)**, showing the `one_for_one`/`one_for_all`/`rest_for_one` restarts, but the **compiled** binary prints a garbage pid (`wa=2599429144`, from `get_actor_field` yielding `None` and `pid_of_int` casting it) and then **exits 139 / SIGSEGV** on the subsequent deref. Because the ONLY surface way to reach a supervised child is to read its pid out of the supervisor state via `get_actor_field(sup, "wa")` + `pid_of_int(...)` (to `send`/`kill`/`is_alive` it), sending to, killing, or checking a supervised child is impossible compiled — which is exactly why NO `one_for_one` restart golden could be added (a divergent/crashing program cannot be a golden `MATCH`; the restart semantics are documented in §4.10.7 prose + `eval.ml` citations instead, the same class as the §4.10.6 capability/dead-`send` divergence). The external-inspection idiom is therefore interp-only and the compiled backend diverges (crash vs `None`). Fixing it is compiled-runtime work (implement a real `march_get_actor_field` reading the actor's state record, and a safe tagged `march_pid_of_int`), out of scope for this documentation-only slice.

  **Related, narrower divergence found the same task (same non-golden verdict):** the compiled supervisor does **not** run its declared children's `init` bodies at `spawn(Sup)` time. Verified live: a `Sup` declaring two `Worker` children whose `Worker.init` has a `println("worker init")` body — interp fires `worker init` once per child at `spawn(Sup)`; compiled fires **none** (prints only the following `done`). A *directly*-spawned actor's printing `init` DOES run byte-identically on both backends, so the gap is specifically the supervisor-child-spawn plane (`ESpawn` supervisor arm, `eval.ml:7207–7254`, evaluates each child `init` at `:7220`), not `init` bodies in general. This is why even the mildest "a supervisor spawns its declared children" witness (observed purely via a printing `init`, with NO `get_actor_field`/`pid_of_int`) diverges compiled and could not be a golden. Documented in the §4.10.7 divergence table; fixing it is compiled-runtime scheduler work, out of scope here.

- [ ] **`Actor.call`'s `timeout_ms` is UNENFORCED in the compiled runtime — a synchronous call cannot actually time out.** `march_actor_call(void *actor, void *inner_msg, int64_t timeout_ms)` (`runtime/march_runtime.c:1809`) explicitly discards the timeout: `(void)timeout_ms; /* timeout not yet enforced; accepted for API compat */` (`:1810`). The parameter is accepted for ABI/API compatibility but has no effect — a `call` that would block indefinitely will not be interrupted by its stated timeout. Noted here for reference-completeness alongside the supervision widening (§4.10.7); it is not exercised by any golden (the actor-`call` path is not part of the operational core fragment). Fixing it is runtime work (thread the timeout through the compiled scheduler's blocking-receive so the call unblocks with a timeout result), deferred, out of scope for this documentation-only slice.

- [ ] **`Actor.call`'s handler-dispatch FORM diverges interpreted vs compiled — no single example works in both backends.** The interpreter (`lib/eval/eval.ml:6455`) wraps the call as `Call(ref, msg)` (`VCon ("Call", [VInt ref_id; inner_msg])`) and delivers it to a **two-argument** handler matching `on Call(ref, msg)`; any message value works, and dispatch is to a `Call`-named/first handler. The compiled runtime (`march_runtime.c:1809–1849`) instead reads the **tag** of a **zero-arg sentinel** `inner_msg`, builds an augmented message `{tag, field0 = caller}`, and routes it to the **handler at that tag**, binding the caller as its **single** argument (e.g. `on GetCount(reply_to)`). Consequences (verified live 2026-07-06, both forms × both backends): the interpreter form (`on Call(ref, msg)` + `Actor.call(pid, Inc(), …)`) works interpreted (`count = 2`) but **DEADLOCKS compiled** — the tag routes to a non-replying handler and the unenforced timeout (finding above) never fires; the compiled form (zero-arg sentinel + one-arg handler) works compiled (`count = 1`) but returns `Err("no reply …")` interpreted. `specs/lang/actors.md` documents the **compiled** form as canonical (maintainer decision) with an interpreter-divergence note; the `stdlib/actor.march` and `share/march/actor.march` docstrings likewise show the compiled form. Fixing it is runtime/interpreter work — reconcile the two protocols, most likely by making the interpreter tag-route and inject a single caller field to match compiled — out of scope for the doc reconciliation.

### Compiler (found during Core March widening slice — interfaces/impls declaration checking, Task 6 closeout / `interfaces.md` reconciliation, 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **A user-interface default method whose body calls a sibling interface method compiled ALWAYS returns the same result, regardless of the actual call arguments — a real, previously-undocumented compiled-only wrong-answer bug (NOT a hang).** Found while reproducing `specs/lang/interfaces.md`'s stale "Known issue" callout (which claimed a default `neq` calling `eq` "hangs/stack-overflows at runtime"). That claim is FALSE — reproduced live, interpreted AND compiled, and neither backend hangs or overflows the stack; the interpreted backend is fully correct. But the compiled backend has a different, real bug in the same area: the default method's body (compiled to a lambda closure over the interface's own type parameter, evaluated at `impl`-application time) does not correctly re-evaluate per call — it appears to always take the same branch/answer as whichever call is compiled/inlined first, ignoring the actual arguments on subsequent (or all) calls.

  **Minimal repro** (confirmed live this task, interpreted vs `--compile --opt 2`, deterministic across reruns):
  ```march
  mod M do
    interface MyOrd(a) do
      fn mycmp : a -> a -> Int
      fn mylt  : a -> a -> Bool do fn (x, y) -> mycmp(x, y) < 0 end
    end

    type AppColor = Red | Green | Blue

    fn color_rank(c) do
      match c do
        Red -> 0
        Green -> 1
        Blue -> 2
      end
    end

    impl MyOrd(AppColor) do
      fn mycmp(a, b) do
        color_rank(a) - color_rank(b)
      end
    end

    fn main() do
      println(bool_to_string(mylt(Red, Green)))   -- expect true  (0-1 = -1 < 0)
      println(bool_to_string(mylt(Green, Red)))   -- expect false (1-0 = 1, not < 0)
    end
  end
  ```
  ```
  $ march file.march                                          # interpreted: true / false  (correct)
  $ march --compile --opt 2 -o /tmp/b file.march && /tmp/b     # compiled:    true / true   (WRONG — 2nd call should be false)
  ```
  Calling `mycmp` directly (not through the `mylt` default) is correct compiled in isolation (confirmed: printing `mycmp(Red, Green)` compiled gives `-1`, matching interpreted) — the bug is specific to the DEFAULT method's own lambda body, not to `mycmp`'s dispatch or to `<`'s codegen. Also reproduces identically for `Eq`-shaped defaults (`neq` calling `eq` via `fn (x, y) -> !eq(x, y)`) and is NOT specific to colliding with the built-in `Eq`/`Ord` interface names — renaming to a user-only `MyEq`/`myeq`/`myneq` (no built-in name collision at all) reproduces byte-for-byte identically, ruling out the `impl_tbl` dispatch-key-collision mechanism the stale doc callout blamed (that mechanism was a real, separate, already-fixed bug — see `core-march.md` §4.4.2's "context, not a rule" note on the `is_type_dispatched_iface` guard). Also confirmed the doc's claim that "`Ord`'s `cmp`-calling defaults are unaffected" is ALSO wrong — `mylt`/`mygt` derived from `mycmp` show the identical always-same-answer symptom.

  A second, textually-adjacent shape (`impl` methods with constructor-pattern params, e.g. `fn mycmp(N(a), N(b)) do … end`, as opposed to `fn mycmp(a, b) do match (a,b) do … end end`) instead hits a DIFFERENT, pre-existing ICE (`` LLVM Llvm_ctx.emit: constructor pattern $Tuple2(...) destructures a non-pointer scrutinee ... type-incorrect TIR reached codegen ``) rather than the wrong-answer symptom above — noted here because it was surfaced by the same investigation, but it is a distinct failure mode (compile-time ICE, not a silent runtime wrong-answer) and not otherwise probed or filed by this task.

  Root cause not pinned down (docs-only reconciliation task, `lib/tir`/`lib/eval` not modified) — plausible candidate, per `core-march-types.md` §2.3's default-method mechanics (`inject_defaults`, `lib/desugar/desugar.ml:897`–`931`, splices a synthesized zero-param clause wrapping the default expression verbatim before typecheck ever sees the impl) interacting with the compiled backend's closure/TCO lowering for that synthesized clause, but this was not investigated further. Filed here (not fixed) because it directly falsifies both halves of `interfaces.md`'s removed "Known issue" callout (see that file's reconciliation, this same commit) and is a real, reproducible, currently-undocumented compiled-backend correctness bug distinct from every other interface/impl finding filed by this widening slice (impl-coherence overlap, above, and derive-unknown-type no-op, above — neither of which is about default-method lambda bodies specifically). Deliberately NOT added to `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/` — that corpus is `--check`-only (typecheck-time accept/reject) and this is a RUN-time, compiled-only wrong-answer, exactly the same harness-shape limitation documented for the impl-coherence divergence above.

### Compiler (found during Differential Oracle Task 4 generator scoping, 2026-07-04)

- [ ] **Compiled and interpreted `hash()` use different, backend-specific algorithms with no cross-backend value equality for RECORD types — expected, but undocumented.** For `TDRecord`, derived `Hash`'s body (`expand_derive`, `lib/desugar/desugar.ml` "Hash" case) calls the polymorphic `hash()` builtin per field and combines the results. That builtin is genuinely backend-specific: interpreter uses OCaml's `Hashtbl.hash` (`lib/eval/eval.ml`); compiled uses a custom splitmix-style hash (`march_hash_int`/`march_hash_string`/etc., `runtime/march_runtime.c`). Confirmed by hand: `hash({x:1,y:2})` prints `28043382405` interpreted vs `6305855436935449413` compiled for the identical value — not a bug, just two independent hash implementations that were never meant to agree. (Derived `Hash` on a VARIANT type is NOT affected — its body is the bare constructor index as an integer literal, `ELit (LitInt i)`, no call to the `hash()` builtin at all, so it's trivially portable.) `syntax_reference.md`'s derived-Ord/Hash section documents the payload-ignoring semantics but doesn't mention the record-Hash cross-backend non-portability; worth a one-line addition so a future test author doesn't file it as a divergence. Differential-oracle Task 4's record derived-method generator (`gen_derived_method_record_module`, `test/test_properties.ml`) deliberately never prints `hash()`'s result for this reason (only `==`/`eq`/`compare`, all pure Int-field comparisons); the Newtype/Boxed/enum generators DO print `hash()` since those are all variant types (portable by construction, not by luck — verified by hand).

- [ ] **Tuples have no `Show` impl — compiled `println`/`to_string` on any tuple is broken (link failure or garbage output).** stdlib/prelude.march defines `impl Show(List(a))`, `impl Show(Option(a))`, `impl Show(Result(a,e))` but no `impl Show` for tuples. `println((1, 2))` fails to even LINK compiled (`Undefined symbols for architecture arm64: "_show"` — the bare `show` call inside `println`'s prelude body never resolves for a tuple argument type). `to_string((1, 2))` compiles and runs but prints garbage: `#<tag:0>` instead of `(1, 2)`. Interpreted mode is correct in both cases (`value_to_string`'s `VTuple` case, `lib/eval/eval.ml`, produces `"(1, 2)"` exactly — confirmed by an existing test, `test/test_eval.ml:330`). Repro:
  ```march
  mod Main do
    fn main() do
      println((1, 2))   -- compiled: link error "Undefined symbols: _show"
    end
  end
  ```
  and, compiling successfully but wrong:
  ```march
  mod Main do
    fn main() do
      print(to_string((1, 2)))  -- interp: "(1, 2)"   compiled: "#<tag:0>"
      print("\n")
    end
  end
  ```
  Fix needs either a dedicated `impl Show` for tuples (stdlib, if tuple types can be named in an `impl` head) or, more likely, a compiler-level special case in the `to_string`/`show` dispatch (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s builtin `to_string` dispatch and/or mono's interface-impl resolution) that renders tuple element-wise like the interpreter does. Differential-oracle Task 3 (`test/test_properties.ml`) deliberately excludes tuple-container generators from `gen_well_typed_module` to avoid reddening the oracle on this pre-existing bug; a tuple generator can be added once this is fixed.
- [ ] **`to_string` on any non-primitive type (`List`, `Option`, `Result`, tuples, custom ADTs) is broken compiled — bypasses `show` entirely, unlike `println`.** `println(x)` (prelude: `print(show(x)); print("\n")`) correctly dispatches through the `Show` interface and IS fixed for `List`/`Option` (the `ffe6fba8` fix). But `to_string(x)` used directly — not via `println` — is a SEPARATE builtin (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s `to_string` dispatch handles `Int`/`Float`/`Bool`/`String` with dedicated C helpers; everything else, including `List`/`Option`/tuples, falls through to the generic runtime `march_value_to_string(ptr)`, which only recognizes tagged-int immediates, actor Pids, and raw string cells — any other heap object hits the fallback `"#<tag:%d>"` in `runtime/march_runtime.c`). Confirmed compiled-only divergences (interpreter is correct in all cases): `to_string([1,2,3])` → `"#<tag:1>"` (interp: `"[1, 2, 3]"`); `to_string(Some(9))` → `"9"` (interp: `"Some(9)"`); `to_string(None : Option(Int))` → `"nil"` (interp: `"None"`). This was already noted in passing as a "deferred discovery" under the `Check.all` property-test fix (this file, `Done` section) but never had its own tracked entry or a minimal repro. Differential-oracle Task 3's generators route every container print through `println` directly (never `to_string`) specifically to avoid this bug; a follow-up widening those generators to also cover `to_string(container)` should wait for this fix.
- [ ] **A bare/unpinned `None` (no `Some(_)` of the same element type at the SAME call site) fails to LINK compiled — even with an explicit type annotation.** `println(None)` fails compiled with `Undefined symbols: _show` regardless of whether `None` carries an explicit `Option(Int)` annotation (`let x : Option(Int) = None; println(x)` fails identically). This is NOT fixed by a `Some(_)` of the same type appearing elsewhere in the program (even in an immediately preceding statement) — mono resolves each `println`/`show` call site's `Show$Option` specialization independently from its own static argument type, not globally across the module, so a sibling `Some` does not "pin" the type for a separate `None` call. `None` DOES compile correctly when it appears inside a list literal alongside a `Some(_)` in the SAME call site, e.g. `println([Some(1), None, Some(3)])` (this is the exact repro `ffe6fba8`'s own regression test uses and IS green). Repro (fails):
  ```march
  mod Main do
    fn main() do
      let x : Option(Int) = None
      println(x)   -- link error: Undefined symbols: _show
    end
  end
  ```
  Likely same root cause as the two entries above (mono's interface-impl specialization needs a concrete element type to resolve `Show$Option.show`, and a lone `None` carries none at the call site — even a static annotation on the binding doesn't flow into the specialization). `test/test_properties.ml`'s new generic-container generators (Differential Oracle Task 3) deliberately never emit a bare `None` for this reason — every `Option` print is `Some(n)`, and every `None` appears only inside a mixed `[Some(...), None, ...]` list literal.
- [ ] **TEST HARNESS GAP (not a compiler bug): `prop_generated_programs_are_well_typed` (`test/test_properties.ml`) reddens on Task 3's own `gen_println_int_list_module`/`gen_println_string_list_module`/etc. because its typecheck pipeline never loads the stdlib.** Found during Task 4 verification (full-suite run), root-caused, confirmed to reproduce identically on the unmodified pre-Task-4 file (a byte-for-byte copy of `git show HEAD:test/test_properties.ml` with the same probe hook reproduces it), so this is NOT a Task 4 regression and NOT caused by the derived-method generators. `pipeline_up_to_typecheck` (`test_properties.ml:32`) calls `March_typecheck.Typecheck.check_module` directly on the bare parsed+desugared module — no stdlib `.march` files are ever loaded into the typecheck env (unlike the `--check`/`--compile` CLI paths, which auto-discover and link stdlib's `prelude.march` `impl Show(List(a))` etc. via `MARCH_LIB_PATH`/default stdlib discovery). So `println([0])` — a QCheck-shrunk minimal case from `gen_println_int_list_module` — fails typecheck in-process with `expected String but got List(Int)` (the typechecker's builtin fallback signature for `println`, used only when no `Show` impl is resolvable) even though the identical program compiles and runs correctly via `--check`/`--compile`/`--` interpretation (confirmed: `main.exe --check` on the identical source exits 0). Since `prop_generated_programs_are_well_typed` runs at `~count:500` over the full `gen_well_typed_module` pool, this was latent since Task 3 landed the `println(<container>)` generators — the random draw simply didn't hit this specific interaction until now (QCheck shrinking easily finds it once triggered: shrunk to `println([0])` in 4 steps). Fix options: (a) give `pipeline_up_to_typecheck` a pre-built stdlib-loaded env (there is a `check_module_with_env` entry point in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` for exactly this) and thread it through the no-crash/well-typed properties that use `gen_well_typed_module`, or (b) scope `prop_generated_programs_are_well_typed` to only the generator subset that doesn't call interface-dispatched builtins (`println`/`to_string` on non-primitive types), mirroring how the dedicated `prop_oracle_*` properties already avoid this by going through the full CLI (`oracle_check`) which DOES load stdlib. Not fixed as part of Task 4 (out of scope — Task 4 touches only the derived-method generators, all of which print via `bool_to_string`/`int_to_string` first and never hit this path).

### Differential-oracle coverage follow-ups (Task 6 / Phase 3, 2026-07-04)

- [ ] **Widen the oracle opt-matrix with a `--no-opt` (TIR-optimizer-off) axis.** Task 6's `prop_oracle_opt_matrix` (`test/test_properties.ml`) diffs the interpreter against the compiled binary at `--opt 0` and `--opt 2` — but `--opt N` is the *clang* codegen level (`opt_level`, `bin/main.ml`), NOT the March/TIR optimization passes. The TIR-level optimizer-miscompile family (the sort_by/cprop class the plan cites) is gated by the SEPARATE `--no-opt` flag (`opt_enabled`, which toggles `Fusion.run`/`Opt.run` etc.). That family is already caught *as a flat mismatch* by the existing default-O2-vs-interpreter comparison (the default has TIR passes on), so this is coverage-broadening, not a gap: adding a third matrix configuration that compiles with `--no-opt` and diffs TIR-off-vs-interpreter (and, for attribution, TIR-off-vs-TIR-on) would (a) exercise the less-tested base-codegen path and (b) let a TIR-optimizer miscompile be *attributed* (agrees at `--no-opt`, diverges with passes on) rather than only flagged. Deferred from Task 6 to bound wall-clock and redden-risk (the `--no-opt` path is less exercised; a base-codegen divergence there would need filing + carving before it can land green). When added, reuse the existing `oracle_check ~opt` plumbing — extend it to also thread `--no-opt`, or add a parallel `?tir_opt` flag.
- [ ] **Extend the conformance-sweep corpus beyond `bench/`+`examples/` (Task 7 / Phase 4, 2026-07-04).** `test/test_oracle.ml`'s full-corpus sweep deliberately covers only `bench/`+`examples/` top-level `.march` files. Extend it to (a) a triaged subset of `test/`'s 189+ fixture programs that are actually meant to run standalone and print deterministic output (most are single-feature snippets or deliberately-erroring negative cases and are out of scope), and (b) a dedicated stdlib-doctest extractor that pulls `march>` REPL-transcript comments out of `stdlib/*.march` module doc comments and runs them both-ways. See the corpus-scope comment at the top of `test/test_oracle.ml` for the full rationale. (Moved here from P0 — this is test-infra coverage-broadening, not a blocking compiler bug.)

### Compiler (found during Wave 4 Task 5 semantics-notes verification, 2026-07-03)

- [ ] **Top-level default-arg functions are unreachable by name from March source — typecheck resolution gap (any arity, both backends).** A source-level call to a default-arg function's own name fails typecheck with ``I cannot find `add`.`` under `--check`, `--compile`, and plain interpretation alike — at every arity including full:
  ```march
  mod Main do
    fn add(x, y \\ 10, z \\ 20) do x + y + z end
    fn main() do
      println(int_to_string(add(1, 5, 6)))   -- I cannot find `add`.  (exit 1)
    end
  end
  ```
  Root cause: `expand_defaults_decl` (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`) deliberately emits ONLY mangled `add$1`/`add$2`/`add$3` decls — no bare-name dispatcher decl (its doc comment: the interpreter's env-builder auto-creates `VMultiarity` entries for the base name from the `foo$N` pattern, and the TIR lowering rewrites call sites via `_default_dispatch` in `lib/tir/lower.ml`). But the TYPECHECKER runs between desugar and both of those consumers, binds each `DFn` under its literal post-desugar name only (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`), and has no equivalent arity-dispatch reconstruction — so bare `add` is never in scope and every source-level call dies before eval or TIR ever run. **Test gap that hid this:** every existing default-arg test (`test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`: `test_default_arg_used`, `test_default_arg_overridden`, `test_default_multiple_defaults`) invokes the function from the OCaml harness via `call_fn env "greet" [...]`, which bypasses source-level name resolution entirely; `test_default_arg_typechecks` only checks the *definition* typechecks, not a call site. No test in the tree contains a March-source call to a default-arg function. (The `greet("World")` example in `syntax_reference.md` § Functions does not compile today.) Fix should add a source-level call-site test at every arity, top-level AND nested. Related, distinct: nested-module default-arg fns additionally DROP their default values entirely (`desugar_fn_def`'s `FPDefault` fast path — "nested default-arg dispatch was never wired up"), so even after the typecheck gap is fixed, nested reduced-arity calls will still fail. Doc note: `syntax_reference.md` § "Semantics notes → Nested-module default-arg functions".

### Web Framework: Islands Library

- ✅ **Islands library (`islands/`)** — `forge new islands --lib`. Core: `Islands.wrap`, `Islands.wrap_eager`, `Islands.client_only`, `Islands.bootstrap_script`, `Islands.preload_hint`, `Islands.Registry`. `HydrateOn` type (`Eager | Lazy | OnIdle | OnInteraction`). `interface Island(s)` typeclass. `islands/runtime/march_islands.js` — actor-per-island JS runtime with configurable hydration strategies (eager, lazy, idle, interaction), event delegation (`data-on-click`, `data-on-input`, `data-on-change`, `data-on-submit`), cross-island messaging API. 34 tests in `islands/test/islands_test.march`. Specs: `specs/bastion/architecture.md`, `specs/bastion/wasm-islands.md`, `specs/bastion/templates.md`. WASM loading stub plugs in when Tier 4 browser target lands.
- ✅ **Bastion scoped island CSS and static file serving** — `Static` module (bastion): ETag (mtime+size), smart cache-control (immutable for 8-char-hash fingerprinted files, 1h otherwise), `Static.path/1` (manifest lookup with `/static/` fallback), `Static.css_tag/1`, `Static.js_tag/1`, `Static.mime_type/1` (20 MIME types), `Static.looks_fingerprinted/1`. New `IslandCss` module (bastion): `scope_css/2` (CSS selector prefixing with island data-attribute), `attribute_for/1`, `attr_name/1`, `to_kebab/1` (PascalCase→kebab-case). `Islands.wrap_with_css/5`, `Islands.wrap_eager_with_css/4` in stdlib (embeds scoped CSS in `data-march-island-css` attribute). `IslandAssets` enhanced: manifest.json endpoint, content-hash WASM cache (immutable/60s), `wasm_url/2`. `march-islands.js` updated: `_injectScopedCss` injects per-island `<style>` tags on hydration (deduplicated by module name). 48 tests in `bastion/test/bastion_test.march`.
- ✅ **WASM Tier 4 (browser target) — pipeline validated** — `wasm32-unknown-unknown` codegen produces working WASM binaries. Validated end-to-end: `march_island_render`, `march_island_update`, `march_island_init`, `march_alloc_export`, `march_dealloc`, `march_island_render_html`, `march_island_string_length`, `march_island_string_data` all export correctly and pass JS `WebAssembly.instantiate` tests. Fixed 4 compiler bugs: (1) `find_fn` island export matching was catching `Vault.update` instead of user's `update` — narrowed to user module prefix; (2) vault builtins (`vault_new/get/set/update/etc.`) missing from `is_builtin_fn`/`mangle_extern`/preamble — causing broken `%vault_update.addr` IR; (3) `ECallPtr` to known builtins wasn't short-circuited to direct calls; (4) `build_wasm.sh` pointed to native `march_runtime.c` instead of `march_runtime_wasm.c`. Added `memset/memcpy/memmove` stubs and vault WASM stubs (panic) to `march_runtime_wasm.c`. **Remaining**: Integrate into `Islands.loadWasmModule` stub in `march_islands.js`; `march_island_render_html` only works for IOList-returning renders (String-returning renders need `march_island_render` + `march_island_string_*`).
- ✅ **Bastion.Cache + Bastion.Depot** — HTTP middleware layer in `stdlib/bastion.march`. `Bastion.Cache`: `etag/1` (FNV-1a ETag + 304 Not Modified short-circuit), `cached/4` (full-response cache via Vault with TTL), `fragment/3` (IOList fragment cache with TTL), `invalidate/2`, `invalidate_prefix/2` (O(n) prefix scan via `Vault.keys`), `cache_control/2`, `no_cache/1`, `public_cache/2`. `Bastion.Depot`: `with_pool/2` (attaches DB pool to conn assigns under `"db"`). Also added `Vault.keys/1` and `HttpServer.get_resp_header/2`. 16 tests.
- ✅ **Bastion web framework layer** — Full framework layer on top of `HttpServer`. Router: `Router.new`, `Router.get/post/put/delete/patch`, `Router.action` (ActionResult-based), `Router.scope`, `Router.fallback`, `Router.dispatch`; pattern params via `:id`. Controller: `ActionResult = Ok(Conn) | Error(Int, String)`, `Controller.action`, `Controller.render`, `Controller.render_iolist`, `Controller.json`, `Controller.text`, `Controller.redirect`. FallbackController: `call`, `call_json`, `new`. Middleware: `compose`, `pipeline`, `logger`, `request_id`, `cors`, `content_type`, `body_parser`. TypedMiddleware: session (cookie-backed, signed HMAC-SHA256), JWT auth, typed assigns, `require_auth`. Request/Response/ConnStates helpers. ErrorView. Static file server with ETag, fingerprint caching, manifest. IslandCss: selector scoping, kebab-case transform. IslandAssets: island runtime JS/WASM serving, manifest, cache control. Islands stdlib additions: `wrap_with_css`, `wrap_eager_with_css`. 143 tests in `bastion/test/bastion_test.march`.
- ✅ **Bastion configuration system** — `Env` stdlib module (`Env.get`, `Env.require`, `Env.get_int`, `Env.get_bool`, `Env.is_set`, `Env.require_int`). `Config` stdlib module backed by a global Vault table: 2-level `Config.get`/`Config.put`, 3-level `Config.get_in`/`Config.put_in`, `Config.from_env`/`Config.from_env_int`/`Config.from_env_bool` for env-var loading, `Config.validate`/`Config.validate_in` with predicate, `Config.env`/`Config.is_dev`/`Config.is_test`/`Config.is_prod` (reads `MARCH_ENV`), `Config.put_endpoint`/`Config.endpoint_port`/`Config.endpoint_host`/`Config.secret_key_base` for web server config, `Config.new_store` for isolated named stores. Spec: `specs/bastion/configuration.md`. 28 tests in `test/stdlib/test_env.march` + `test/stdlib/test_config.march`.
- ✅ **`BastionDev` stdlib module** — Live-reload SSE injection, request timing middleware, Phoenix LiveDashboard-inspired dev dashboard at `/_bastion`, and rich error overlay. `BastionDev.request_timer/finish_timer/server_timing/conn_inspector` middleware plugs. `BastionDev.inject_live_reload` injects SSE script before `</body>`. `BastionDev.live_reload_handler` serves `/_bastion/live_reload`. `BastionDev.dashboard_html/dashboard_handler` renders full metrics dashboard (total requests, error count, avg duration, WS connections, vault table sizes, color-coded request log, error log with expandable traces, route registry). `BastionDev.error_overlay_html/error_overlay` renders Phoenix-style rich 500 pages with stack trace and conn state. Stat types: `RequestEntry`, `ErrorEntry`, `RouteEntry`, `VaultStat`, `DevStats`. 66 tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_dev.march`.
- ✅ **Signed/encrypted cookies with key rotation** — `BastionCookies.put_signed/get_signed` and `put_encrypted/get_encrypted`. Keys derived via PBKDF2-SHA256 from `secret_key_base` with cookie-name context. HMAC-SHA256 signing; authenticated encryption with integrity verification. `delete/2` sets Max-Age=0. Full opts support (max_age, secure, http_only, same_site, path). `stdlib/bastion_cookies.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_cookies.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/cookies.md`.
- ✅ **Reversible routing / compile-time route verification** — `BastionRoutes` registry backed by Vault. `register/4` (method, pattern, helper_name, arity), `all/0`, `find_by_name/1`, `path/3` (fills `:param` slots, appends `?k=v` query strings with percent-encoding), `static_path/1`, `path1/2`, `path2/3`. `stdlib/bastion_routes.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_routes.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/route-verification.md`.
- ✅ **Request/response lifecycle hooks with structured telemetry** — `BastionTelemetry.attach/3`, `detach/1`, `execute/3`, `recent_events/1`, `handler_count/0`, `subscribed_events/1`. `TelemetryEvent` type with name/measurements/metadata. Vault-backed ring buffer (1000-entry cap). `Aggregator` sub-module: `start/0`, `record/1`, `recent_requests/1`, `counters/0`. `register_before_send/2` and `register_after_send/2` hooks on Conn via assigns. `stdlib/bastion_telemetry.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_telemetry.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/telemetry.md`.
- ✅ **Connection draining on hot deploy** — `BastionHotDeploy`: `DrainStatus = Serving | Draining | Starting | Stopped`, `DrainConfig`, `default_config/0`. State in Vault: `start_drain/0`, `stop_drain/0`, `mark_starting/0`, `mark_serving/0`. In-flight counter: `increment/decrement/in_flight_count`. `health_status/1` → `(Int, String)` JSON; `drain_response/1`; `is_draining/0`; `should_drain_request/1`. `stdlib/bastion_hot_deploy.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_hot_deploy.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/hot-deploy.md`.
- ✅ **CSP nonce injection** — `BastionCSP.assign_nonce/1` (16 random bytes, base64-encoded, stored in `csp_nonce` assign), `nonce/1`, `get_nonce/1`, `set_header/1`, `protect/1`, `protect_with_overrides/2`, `disable/1`, `report_only/2`. Strict default policy (script-src, style-src with nonce, img-src, font-src, connect-src wss:, worker-src blob:, frame-ancestors, base-uri, form-action). `stdlib/bastion_csp.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_csp.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/csp.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed pub/sub on the actor system** — `BastionPubSub`: named instances, `Adapter = Local | Redis(String) | Cluster`. `subscribe/2`, `unsubscribe/2`, `broadcast/3`, `broadcast_from/3`, `local_broadcast_sync/3`, `subscribers/2`, `recent_broadcasts/2`. Wildcard topic matching (`*`). Vault-backed subscription and broadcast log. `stdlib/bastion_pubsub.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_pubsub.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/pubsub.md`.
- ✅ **Request sandboxing for tests** — `BastionTestSandbox`: `SandboxEnv` type (id, vault_namespace, pubsub_name). `checkout/1`, `release/1`, `checkout_tracked/1`, `release_tracked/1`, `is_active/1`, `active_count/0`. Isolated Vault namespace per sandbox (`vault_put/3`, `vault_get/2`, `vault_delete/2`). `build_conn/3`, `build_conn_full/5`. Global registry in Vault tracks active sandboxes. `stdlib/bastion_test_sandbox.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_test_sandbox.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/test-sandbox.md`.
- ✅ **Idempotency keys as middleware** — `BastionIdempotency`: `IdempotencyState = InProgress(Int) | Completed(Int, List, String, Int)`. `get_key/1` (reads `Idempotency-Key` header), `lookup/3`, `mark_in_progress/3`, `complete_entry/6`, `replay_response/2`, `invalidate/3`, `protect/3` (handler fn wrapper). Cache hit replays stored response; InProgress returns 409 with `Retry-After: 5`; 5xx not cached. `X-Idempotent-Replayed` header. `stdlib/bastion_idempotency.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_idempotency.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/idempotency.md`.
- ✅ **Structured logging with request correlation IDs** — `CorrelationId.assign/1` middleware reads or generates a UUID v4 as the request correlation ID, stores it in `conn.assigns["request_id"]`, echoes it in the `X-Request-ID` response header, and injects it into the Logger context via `Logger.with_context` so all subsequent log calls in the request process include `request_id` automatically — no manual threading. `CorrelationId.request_id/1` accessor, `CorrelationId.clear/0` to reset Logger context. `uuid_v4` builtin added to eval (RFC 4122 v4, version nibble=4, variant=10xx). 15 tests in `test/stdlib/test_correlation.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/logging.md`.
- ✅ **`Html` stdlib module** — `Html.escape`, `Html.escape_attr`, `Html.Safe` type, `Html.raw`, `Html.safe_to_string`, `Html.tag`, `Html.render_partial`, `Html.render_collection`, `Html.layout`, `Html.content_hash`, `Html.list`, `Html.join`. Auto-escaping `~H` sigil via `html_auto_escape` builtin (handles `Html.Safe` and IOList without double-escaping). `IOList.hash` (FNV-1a 64-bit, for ETag generation). `IOList` is the canonical rendered HTML fragment type: cacheable in Vault, re-embeddable in `~H` without escaping. 52 tests in `test/stdlib/test_html.march`, 19 tests in `test/stdlib/test_sigil.march`. Example: `examples/templates.march`.
- ✅ **`CSRF` stdlib middleware module** — `stdlib/csrf.march`. `CSRF.generate_token()` (CSPRNG, 32 bytes, base64); `CSRF.ensure_token(conn)` idempotent per-session token assignment; `CSRF.token(conn)` accessor; `CSRF.tag(conn)` IOList hidden input; `CSRF.tag_string(conn)` String variant; `CSRF.validate(conn)` form-body token comparison; `CSRF.protect(conn)` middleware (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS + JSON pass, POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE validated, 403 on mismatch); `CSRF.skip(conn)` webhook bypass. `~H` desugar: `inject_csrf_tokens` in `lib/desugar/desugar.ml` splits `<form method="post/put/patch/delete">` string literals at the form's `>` and injects `CSRF.tag_string(conn)` — automatic hidden-field injection in all `~H` templates. 32 tests in `test/stdlib/test_csrf.march`.
- ✅ **`Session` stdlib middleware module** — `stdlib/session.march`: cookie-backed (`:cookie`) and Vault-backed (`:vault`) session middleware. `:cookie` stores full session data in a signed browser cookie; `:vault` stores only session ID in cookie with data in `Vault`. Signing: HMAC-SHA256 via `pbkdf2_sha256` key derivation from `Config.secret_key_base` + per-cookie-name salt; cookie format `base64(payload).base64(hmac)`; tampered cookies silently start fresh sessions. Public API: `Session.load(conn, backend)` middleware plug; `Session.save(conn)` / `Session.save_with_opts(conn, opts)` (supports `max_age`, `secure`, `same_site`, `path`, `HttpOnly`); `Session.get/put/delete/clear/all`; `Session.put_flash/get_flash` one-request flash messages (sweep on `save`, unread flash survives). Session data serialised as `key\x1Fval\x1Ekey\x1Fval` strings for storage in Conn string assigns. 49 tests in `test/stdlib/test_session.march`.

### Tooling: Forge Build Tool

- ✅ **Shell completions + external subcommands** — `forge completions bash|zsh|fish` prints a ready-to-source completion script; `forge-<cmd>` binaries on PATH are exec'd when `<cmd>` is not a built-in (built-ins always win). `forge/lib/cli_ext.ml` (pure `completion_script`/`external_subcommand`, 6 unit tests). Item #5 of `specs/forge_tooling_roadmap.md`.
- ✅ **`forge watch`** — reruns build/test/run on source changes (polls `lib/ src/ test/ config/` + `forge.toml`); never exits on failure. `forge/lib/watcher.ml` (pure `changed`, 5 unit tests) + `cmd_watch.ml`. Item #1 of `specs/forge_tooling_roadmap.md`.
- ✅ **`forge build --target js` / `forge run --compiled --target js` (2026-06-22)** — `forge build --target js` compiles the project entry to an ES module (`.mjs`) under `.march/build/{debug,release}/<name>.mjs`; the compiler auto-copies `march_runtime.mjs` + `march_dom.mjs` alongside it. `forge run --compiled --target js` builds the `.mjs` and runs it with `node`. All other targets (`native`, `wasm*`) unchanged — `?target` defaults to `None` so existing call-sites (`forge test`, `forge release`, `forge watch`) are unaffected. `forge/bin/main.ml` + `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml` + `forge/lib/cmd_run.ml`.
- ✅ **`forge bench`** — compiles + runs `bench/*.march` at `--opt 2`, reports wall-clock times (table or `--json`), name-substring filter. `forge/lib/cmd_bench.ml` (pure `bench_name`/`matches`/`format_results`, 4 unit tests). Item #2 of the tooling roadmap.
- ✅ **`forge version` / `forge release`** — bump `[package].version` (patch/minor/major or explicit), `--tag` commits + tags `vX.Y.Z`; `release` gates clean→build→test→bump+tag. `forge/lib/versioning.ml` (pure `bump`/`rewrite_version_line`, 5 unit tests) + `cmd_version.ml`/`cmd_release.ml`. Item #3 of the tooling roadmap (fmt --check + lint CI already existed).
- ✅ **`forge licenses` + `--frozen`** — `forge licenses [--json] [--strict]` lists deps + declared license; `forge build --frozen/--locked` errors on lockfile drift instead of re-resolving. `forge/lib/cmd_licenses.ml` (pure `format`/`any_missing`/`frozen_error`, 3 unit tests). Item #4 of the tooling roadmap.

- ✅ **Implement `forge`** — Implemented as `forge/` package. Commands: `forge new`, `forge build`, `forge run`, `forge test`, `forge format`, `forge interactive`/`i`, `forge deps`, `forge clean`, `forge search`. Template scaffolding generates valid March code (PascalCase module names, `do/end` fn bodies, `println` builtin). 15 tests in `forge/test/test_forge.ml`.
- ✅ **`forge search` — Hoogle-style search** — `lib/search/search.ml` (new `march_search` library): Levenshtein fuzzy name search, type-signature component matching, doc-keyword search, combined search (AND-semantics). JSON index cache at `.march/search-index.json`. `forge/lib/cmd_search.ml` wires up cmdliner with `--type`, `--doc`, `--limit`, `--json`, `--rebuild` flags. 25 tests in `test/test_search.ml` (levenshtein, name search, type search, doc search, combined, JSON roundtrip, stdlib integration).
- ✅ **`forge bastion` command group + scaffold fixes** — *(Moved to bastion repo)* `forge bastion new/server/routes/gen` commands (`cmd_bastion_new.ml`, `cmd_bastion_server.ml`, `cmd_bastion_routes.ml`, `cmd_bastion_gen.ml`, `gen_auth.ml`, `scaffold_bastion.ml`, `cmd_assets.ml`) removed from `forge/` and ported to the bastion repo. `forge bastion` subcommands now ship alongside the framework they scaffold.
- ✅ **Bastion adversarial test suite** — 346 adversarial tests across 7 files in `examples/bastion_tests/`: routing (42 tests — path params, method dispatch, scope prefixes, fallbacks, edge cases), caching (37 — ETags, Cache-Control, Vault fragment cache, CDN headers), sessions (35 — cookie/vault backends, flash, TTL, concurrent), CSRF (44 — token generation, form injection, bypass, replay), dev-dashboard (64 — BastionDev middleware, SSE live-reload, System stats, error overlay), HTML (54 — escaping, sigil injection, IOList, partial rendering), islands (70 — WASM loading, hydration strategies, CSS scoping, cross-island messaging). Fixed `stdlib/islands.march` bug: `_island_runtime_script` embedded the full JS blob instead of serving from `/_bastion/islands.js`. `RESULTS.md` documents all 346 findings.
- ✅ **Dotted import paths** — `import Mod.Sub`, `import Mod.Sub.{foo, bar}`, `import Mod.Sub, only: [foo]`, `import Mod.Sub, except: [foo]` all supported. `import_decl` in `lib/parser/parser.mly` refactored from 4 flat rules using `upper_name` to a single rule with a new right-recursive `import_path_tail` nonterminal (modeled after `use_path_tail`). Conflict-free LALR(1): DOT+LBRACE→brace selector, DOT+UPPER_IDENT→recurse, COMMA→only/except filter, empty→UseAll. 3 new parse tests.
- ✅ **Dotted names in `impl` and `when` constraints** — `impl Conduit.Storage(T)` and `when Conduit.Storage(s)` now parse: both `impl_decl` and `constraint_expr` in the parser accept `upper_dot_path`, joining segments with "." into the `impl_iface.txt`. Typecheck: `prebind_mod_members` now also pre-registers nested module interfaces as `"Mod.Iface"` qualified names; `DUse UseAll` handler now imports interface names (not just vars) from the module prefix into scope, enabling `import Conduit` + `impl Storage(T)` to resolve correctly. 1 new parse test (1311 total).
- ✅ **Multi-file / project-aware compilation** — Scaffolded Bastion apps now compile end-to-end with `forge build`. Three fixes in `bin/main.ml`: (1) `module_name_to_filename` extended to handle dotted names — `MyApp.Router` → `my_app/router.march`, `MyApp.Templates.Layout` → `my_app/templates/layout.march`; (2) `resolve_imports` now tracks loaded file paths (`loaded_paths` set) to prevent duplicate loads when explicit `use` and auto-discovery overlap; (3) auto-discovery: after resolving explicit imports, all `.march` files in `MARCH_LIB_PATH` dirs are loaded — sorted by module-name dot-depth (more dots = deeper namespace = fewer dependents = load first) so the evaluator sees dependencies before their users (`Templates.Page.Index` → `Templates.Layout` → `PageController` → `Router` → app root). Validated: full `Router.dispatch → PageController.index → Layout.wrap → Page.Index.render` chain works from a scaffolded `test_app_01` with `MARCH_LIB_PATH` set.
- ✅ **Forge dependency resolution and versioning system** — Full plan at `specs/plans/forge-dependency-resolution-plan.md`. Six implementation phases (Phases 1–6 complete): (1) Semver `~>` / `>=` / `<=` / `>` / `<` / exact / `AND` combinations (`resolver_version.ml`, `resolver_constraint.ml`); five dep source types (`RegistryDep`, `GitTagDep`, `GitBranchDep`, `GitRevDep`, `PathDep`); `forge.lock` TOML with manifest hash drift detection (`resolver_lockfile.ml`). (2) In-memory registry index; PubGrub greedy solver with FIFO work queue, conflict-driven incompatibility tracking, human-readable error messages (`resolver_pubgrub.ml`, `resolver_registry.ml`). (3) SHA-256 content-addressed package store — sorted canonical archive (`.git`/`.march` excluded, no timestamps), store/lookup/verify with tampering detection (`resolver_cas_package.ml`). (4) `[patch]` fork overrides — `GitBranchDep`/`PathDep` substitution before solver; extra patches for transitive deps; `forge deps` and `forge deps update`. (5) Text-based `.march` source API surface extraction (`pub fn`/`pub type`), diff (Added/Removed/Changed), MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH classification, semver enforcement in `forge publish --old-source` (`resolver_api_surface.ml`, `cmd_publish.ml`). (6) Property-based tests (QCheck, 200 cases × 3 properties), regression tests for Cargo/Hex/npm known bugs, solver performance benchmarks (chain-100: <100ms, chain-500: <1s, diamond-20×20: <500ms). Total: 132 forge tests across 6 suites.
- ✅ **Forge toolchain version manager (first slice)** — `forge toolchain install [<version>|nightly]` / `use` / `list` / `uninstall` over the shared `~/.march/{versions,current,bin}` layout (`forge/lib/toolchain.ml`), plus the `install.sh` curl installer. See `specs/forge_version_manager.md § Implementation Status`.

#### Forge version & package management — remaining gaps

_Full prioritised list and rationale in `specs/forge_version_manager.md § Gaps & Roadmap (2026-06-13)`._

- ✅ **Per-project toolchain pinning** — `.march-version` (walking up parents) + Version Resolution Order (`Toolchain.find_pin`/`resolve_version`/`march_command`/`path_prefix`, 12 unit tests), wired into `forge build`/`run`/`check`/`test` via the shared `lib_path_env` PATH prefix; `build`/`run` hard-error on a missing pin. New `forge toolchain pin`/`which` commands. See `forge_version_manager.md § Implementation Status`.
- ✅ **Toolchain version in `forge.lock`** — Option B (record + drift-warn, never override): `forge deps` writes a `[toolchain]` section; `forge build` warns on drift (`Resolver_lockfile.read_toolchain`/`?toolchain`, `Toolchain.toolchain_drift`, 5 unit tests). `.march-version` stays authoritative. `--frozen` enforce mode is future work.
- ✅ **`forge.toml` `march` constraint enforcement** — `forge build` blocks when the resolved toolchain doesn't satisfy an optional `march = "~> X.Y"` under `[package]` (`Toolchain.check_constraint`, 4 unit tests; non-semver tags allowed through). `project.ml` gains a `march_req` field.
- ✅ **Auto-install a missing pinned toolchain** — `forge build`/`run` download the resolved pin/global toolchain on demand (`Toolchain.ensure_installed`, rustup-style) instead of erroring; constraint checked before download. 2 unit tests + live e2e.
- ✅ **`forge upgrade`** — installs the latest March (newest stable, else newest nightly) and makes it active; no-op if already latest (`Toolchain.upgrade`, verified e2e).
- ✅ **`forge toolchain list --remote`** — lists installable versions from the GitHub releases API, grouped stable/nightly, marking installed ones (`Toolchain.classify_remote`/`list_remote`, 1 unit test).
- ✅ **`forge tree` / `forge why <pkg>`** — dependency-graph introspection (`deptree.ml` pure tree/path logic, 4 unit tests; `cmd_tree.ml` rebuilds edges from each installed dep's forge.toml since the lockfile has none). Diamonds and cycles handled.
- [ ] **`forge outdated`** — show deps with newer compatible versions (needs the registry to know what's newer).
- ✅ **`license`/`repository`/`homepage` in `forge.toml`** — parsed into the project record (`project.ml`); `forge new` scaffolds a `license` placeholder; `forge publish` nudges when unset (2 unit tests). `keywords` (array) now feasible — TOML parser gained `Array` support.
- ✅ **Registry network publish/fetch** — `forge publish` / `forge retire` now submit to a real forgepm registry over native HTTP/TLS (`forge/tasks/forge_registry.march`, embedded in the forge binary); see `forgepm/specs/forge-publish-client.md` for the client-registry contract. `forge login`/`forge yank` remain future work.
- ✅ **Workspaces / monorepo — Phase 1** — `[workspace].members` in `forge.toml`; `forge build`/`test`/`lint` iterate all members at the workspace root (dep order TBD — currently declaration order); `-p <name>` selector for a single member; `Workspace.find_root` walks up parents; `Workspace.parse_members` (TOML array) pure + 5 unit tests. TOML parser now supports arrays (`Array` variant + `get_string_list`). Phase 2 (unified `forge.lock`) and Phase 3 (topological order, `--changed`) are future work.
- [ ] **Vendoring / explicit offline mode** (`forge vendor`, `--offline`) — partly mitigated by the CAS cache; no explicit story.
- [ ] **Optional dependencies / feature flags** — conditional deps and compile-time features (a language-design question).
- [ ] **Full AST-based semver-compat checking on publish** — `Resolver_api_surface` is text-heuristic; linearity/generic diffing needs compiler integration.
