### Compiler: Capabilities/effects (found during Core March widening slice 5)

- [ ] **F1 — body-scanned IO caps are WARNING-only, not error; "absence of `needs` = machine-verified purity" holds only when authors thread `Cap(X)` through signatures.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.6 (capabilities widening, Task 2, 2026-07-07). Check 1b (`typecheck.ml:5588-5605`, `Err.warning_with_fix` at `:5596`) — a function BODY calling a `builtin_cap_table` builtin (`file_read`, `tls_connect`, `vault_set`, …) without a covering `needs` — is WARNING-only, not an error: `--check` exits 0. Contrast Check 1 (signature `Cap(X)`), Check 4 (transitive `use`), and Check 5 (extern `Cap(X)`), which are all true ERRORs, exit 1. So a module that calls IO builtins directly in a function body, never threading `Cap(X)` through any signature, is *warned*, not *rejected* — the guarantee "no `needs` declared ⇒ the function is pure" only holds at the ERROR-level signature/transitive/extern-cap surface, not at the body-call surface. `specs/lang/capabilities.md` overclaims this: `:69` ("The compiler enforces this transitively... the build fails"), and the prose framing around `:142`/`:312` ("There are no false positives"; "the type checker already enforces `needs` as an error") describes the body-call case as effectively error-level when it is warning-level in the actual implementation (the example text in those sections does correctly say "Warning"/"hint", but the surrounding claims overstate the guarantee). Verified live, both sides reproduced side by side: a `Cap(IO.FileRead)` signature with no `needs` rejects (`` `Cap(IO.FileRead)` used in module `Reader` but `IO.FileRead` is not declared in `needs`. ``, exit 1); a body call to `file_read` with no `needs` only emits a HINT + WARNING (`` function body calls a builtin that requires `Cap(IO.FileRead)` but `Reader` does not declare `needs IO.FileRead`. ``, exit 0). This is a design/enforcement gap, not a mechanical bug — Checks 1/4/5 themselves are sound and precise over the `builtin_cap_table` domain. Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice); reconciling `capabilities.md`'s wording against this reality is deferred to Task 7 of the widening plan. See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.6 for the full three-tier table and both live transcripts.
- ✅ **F6 (FIXED, 2026-07-07) — all 18 capability-hierarchy entries are now registered as valid `Cap(X)` type ARGUMENTS.** Fix locus: `builtin_types` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`) now registers the 8 previously-missing 0-arity type names (`IO.Random`, `IO.Database`, `IO.Spawn`, `IO.Mut`, `IO.Telemetry`, `IO.Foreign`, `IO.Foreign.Blocking`, `IO.NetConnect.TLS`) alongside the original 10, matching the full 18-entry hierarchy in `lib/caps/cap_lattice.ml:15-33`. Witness: `specs/lang/types/accept/t57_cap_all_hierarchy_args.march` (four of the newly-registered roots typed as `Cap(X)` params + matching `needs`) — flips `` Unknown module `IO` `` → exit 0. Original finding below.

  **[ORIGINAL FINDING]** Found during capabilities widening Task 1 (2026-07-07). `builtin_types` (`typecheck.ml:1858-1861`) lists exactly `IO`, `IO.Console`, `IO.FileSystem`, `IO.FileRead`, `IO.FileWrite`, `IO.Network`, `IO.NetConnect`, `IO.NetListen`, `IO.Process`, `IO.Clock` as registered 0-arity type names — the other 8 hierarchy entries (`IO.Random`, `IO.Database`, `IO.Spawn`, `IO.Mut`, `IO.Telemetry`, `IO.Foreign`, `IO.Foreign.Blocking`, `IO.NetConnect.TLS`) are valid `needs` targets (an all-string manifest, so e.g. `needs IO.Random` alone typechecks fine) but reject when written as a `Cap(X)` type annotation: `fn f(c : Cap(IO.Random)) : Int do 1 end` rejects with `` Unknown module `IO`. Did you mean `Dir`? `` — verified live (`Cap(IO.Random)` + `needs IO.Random` → `Unknown module IO`, while the structurally identical `Cap(IO.Network)` + `needs IO.Network` accepts). Cause: `cap_paths_in_surface_ty` only extracts a `Cap(X)` path if `X` first survives `surface_ty`'s ordinary type-name resolution; for the 8 unregistered names, `surface_ty`'s qualified-type fallback (`resolve_qualified_type`, `typecheck.ml:729-737`) tries to load an actual module named `IO` (`stdlib/io.march`, which declares no types), fails to find `IO.Random` among its exports, and falls through to the generic qualified-name-not-found diagnostic. A precise, narrow, mechanical registration gap — fix candidate: add the 8 missing paths to `builtin_types` as 0-arity type names, mirroring the existing 10. Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice); documented in `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.3.
- [ ] **The flaky narrowing HINT — Check 3's `Cap(IO)`-narrowing HINT fires nondeterministically (~1-in-10) on the byte-identical binary + input.** Found during capabilities widening Task 3 (2026-07-07), filed now. (Labeled distinctly from the sessions-slice "F7" linearity finding to avoid a shared-doc label collision.) The Check 3 narrowing HINT (emitted when a `Cap(IO)` root capability is used where a narrower sub-cap would suffice) is INCONSISTENT across repeated `--check` runs of the SAME binary on the SAME file: on `accept/t51`'s shape (a two-function `cap_narrow`-threading module) it appeared ~1 of 10 runs; a reduced repro (a single-function module with a bare unused `Cap(IO)` parameter, no `cap_narrow`) reproduces the same flakiness, so it is not `cap_narrow`-specific. Independently reproduced by the Task-3 reviewer (t51 HINT 0/80 runs vs `reject/t37` HINT 80/80 — the exact inconsistency). **The `--check` EXIT CODE is invariant** (accepts exit 0, rejects exit 1 with stable ERROR text every run), so NO corpus program depends on it and conformance is unaffected — but it is a real, previously-undocumented compiler nondeterminism (likely a hash/traversal-order dependence in the HINT-emission path). A determinism-quality follow-up. Documented in `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.10 + `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md` (t51 row). Not fixed here; the trigger was not isolated further.
- ✅ **F2 (FIXED, 2026-07-07) — `cap pure`/`cap deterministic` banned builtin sets are now DERIVED from `builtin_cap_table` instead of hand-guessed name lists that referenced NONEXISTENT builtins and missed the REAL effectful ones.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 (capabilities widening, Task 4, 2026-07-07); fixed in Task 5 of the same slice. **The bug:** `pure_banned` (`typecheck.ml:6570-6576`) listed `write_file`/`read_file`/`append_file`/`delete_file`/`random_int`/`random_float`/`random_bool`/`now_ms` — **none of which exist as builtins** — while missing the real ones (`file_write`/`file_read` `IO.FileWrite`/`IO.FileRead`, `random_bytes` `IO.Random`, `unix_time_ms` `IO.Clock`, `vault_set` `IO.Mut`, …); `deterministic_banned` (`:6632-6635`) listed `now_ms`/`now_ns`/`monotonic_ms` (none exist) and omitted the real `unix_time_ms`. Consequence (live-verified before the fix): `cap pure` + `file_write`/`file_read`/`random_bytes` and `cap deterministic` + `unix_time_ms(())` all typechecked with `--check` **exit 0** — only F1's UNRELATED body-scan HINT+WARNING fired, never the cap's own `` has side effects `` / `` non-deterministic `` ERROR. A genuine soundness hole. **The fix (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`):** both sets are now derived from the authoritative effect map `builtin_cap_table` (`:999`, the same map Check 1b and `lib/refinecheck/cap_infer.ml` trust). `pure_banned` = every builtin in the table (all ≈90 are effectful) ∪ the incidental non-table impure names `{spawn, send, exit}`. `deterministic_banned` = only the builtins the table maps to a NONDETERMINISM cap — `IO.Clock`/`IO.Random`, decided by the new `is_nondeterministic_cap` helper (`:6572`) — so `cap deterministic` stays correctly WEAKER than `cap pure`: it bans clock/RNG but still PERMITS ordinary deterministic-ish IO such as a `file_read` (`IO.FileRead`), matching the documented semantics in `capabilities.md`/§2.8.11. `check_pure_module`/`check_deterministic_module`'s enforcement LOGIC is unchanged — only the banned SETS they consult. **Verified (both directions):** after the fix, `cap pure` + `file_write`(type-correct `Result(Unit, String)`)/`file_read`/`random_bytes` and `cap deterministic` + `unix_time_ms(())` all REJECT (exit 1) with the cap's own error (were exit 0); a genuinely-pure arithmetic `cap pure` module (`accept/t54`) and `cap deterministic` + `file_read` both STILL ACCEPT (no over-rejection). **Tests:** 5 new NON-VACUOUS regression cases in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`cap_pure_no_extern_det` group) using REAL type-correct builtins so `has_errors` — which counts only `severity = Error`, never the F1 body-scan WARNING — is true SOLELY because the cap check fires (RED pre-fix, GREEN after): `cap pure` + `file_write`/`file_read`/`random_bytes`, `cap deterministic` + `unix_time_ms`, and a positive `cap deterministic` + `file_read` → no error. (The 4 pre-existing `now_ms`/`random_int` cases pass on the pre-fix compiler too, but for the WRONG reason — those names are unbound, so an "I cannot find" ERROR satisfies `has_errors` regardless of the cap ban; left in place, noted.) **Corpus:** `specs/lang/types/reject/{t45_cap_pure_file_write, t46_cap_pure_random_bytes, t47_cap_deterministic_unix_time_ms}.march` — each type-correct so the cap ban is the sole rejection, each IMPOSSIBLE pre-fix (they accepted). `check_types.sh` 100 → **103** (56 accept, 44 → **47** reject). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 and `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`.
- ✅ **F3 (FIXED, 2026-07-07) — `cap no_panic` now consumes the exhaustiveness checker's verdict, so a non-exhaustive `match` inside a `cap no_panic` module is an ERROR (it panics at runtime) instead of typechecking clean.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 (capabilities widening, Task 4, 2026-07-07); fixed in Task 6 of the same slice. **The bug:** `check_no_panic_module` worked purely from the syntactic `calls_in_expr` call-collector (a name/span list of CALLS) and had no notion of `EMatch` coverage — it only caught NAMED partial functions (`panic`, `unwrap`, `head`, …). A `cap no_panic` module with `fn get(opt : Option(Int)) : Int do match opt do Some(x) -> x end end` (missing `None`) typechecked with `--check` **exit 0**, emitting only the generic, module-agnostic `` Non-exhaustive pattern match — missing case: None `` **Warning**; calling it at runtime aborts with a "no matching clause" panic — exactly the failure class `cap no_panic` exists to rule out. **The fix (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, option (a) — a recorded-span side-table):** `check_exhaustiveness` already finds every non-exhaustive match and emits the Warning; it now ALSO records the offending `match`'s span into a new shared `env` field `nonexhaustive_match_spans : Ast.span list ref` (a mutable ref threaded through every env copy, like `import_tracker`; recording is cheap and never itself an error). `check_no_panic_module` — which runs ONLY for modules that declared `cap no_panic` — reads that side-table and, for any recorded span nested (by the new `span_within` source-position containment helper) inside one of THIS module's own function bodies, reports an ERROR: `` `get` in `mod Guarded` (declared `cap no_panic`) contains a non-exhaustive `match`, which panics at runtime when no clause matches. ``. Exhaustiveness itself is NOT re-implemented — the fix only plumbs `find_missing_mc`'s existing verdict to `no_panic`. **Key regression guard (I2):** because the read is gated inside `check_no_panic_module`, a non-exhaustive match in a PLAIN (non-cap) module is NEVER promoted — it stays a non-blocking Warning (exit 0). `accept/t14_nonexhaustive_match_still_typechecks.march` pins this and STILL accepts. **Verified (live, before/after):** `cap no_panic` + non-exhaustive match → REJECT exit 1 (was exit 0 + Warning only); `cap no_panic` + exhaustive match / `_ -> ...` catch-all → STILL ACCEPT exit 0; existing `cap no_panic` rejections (explicit `panic` `reject/t42`, unsafe division) → STILL fire; `accept/t14` plain non-exhaustive → STILL ACCEPT exit 0. **Tests:** 4 new NON-VACUOUS regression cases in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`cap_no_panic` group) — `cap no_panic` + non-exhaustive match (RED pre-fix / GREEN after, since `has_errors` counts only `severity = Error` not the Warning), `cap no_panic` + exhaustive match, `cap no_panic` + wildcard catch-all, and the plain-module non-exhaustive guard (all three latter accept). **Corpus:** `specs/lang/types/reject/t48_cap_no_panic_nonexhaustive_match.march` — type-correct, IMPOSSIBLE to reject pre-fix (accepted exit 0). `check_types.sh` 103 → **104** (56 accept, 47 → **48** reject). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 and `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`.
- [ ] **F5 (cosmetic) — `println`/`print` produce NO Check-1b body-scan diagnostic at all, despite being registered in `builtin_cap_table` under `IO.Console` and counting as a capability use for Check 2's unused-`needs` accounting.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 (capabilities widening, Task 4, 2026-07-07; first observed as a probe result during the capabilities survey, §3.3/F5). A bare `println("hi")` call with no covering `needs IO.Console` triggers **neither** Check 1b's warning-with-fix (`typecheck.ml:5588-5605`) **nor** the softer `lib/refinecheck/cap_infer.ml` HINT, while an otherwise-identical `file_read(...)` call in the same function body triggers both — no explicit `IO.Console`-specific filter is visible in either code path, so the two builtins appear to be reaching (or failing to reach) the warning path via some parse/desugar difference in how a bare `println`/`print` call resolves versus other builtins, not investigated further here. This is UNRELATED to F2 above — `println`/`print` ARE correctly present in `pure_banned` (`typecheck.ml:6571`) and `cap pure` DOES correctly reject a bare `println` call (verified: `` has side effects `` ERROR, exit 1) — F5 is purely a Check-1b/cap_infer body-scan COVERAGE gap that makes `IO.Console` the de-facto "free" capability at that specific (warning-only, already low-stakes per F1) layer. Low priority; not investigated further in this docs-only task. See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 for the note.
- [ ] **The proof-cap mint mismatch — the proof-cap "mint" idiom `capabilities.md` documents does not typecheck as written; the mechanism that DOES work (`cap_narrow`) is unrestricted and can mint an arbitrary non-IO proof capability from any ordinary `Cap(IO)`, without a covering `needs`.** Found during capabilities widening Task 7 (closeout, 2026-07-07), reconciling `capabilities.md`'s proof-caps section against live `--check` behavior. (Deliberately given a descriptive name, not a letter — every one of `F1`–`F8` is already in use elsewhere in this document: `F1`–`F6` by this slice's own other capabilities findings, `F7`/`F8` by the UNRELATED session-types-slice linearity and diagnostic-noise findings; and the capabilities-survey's own internal working label for this finding was "F4," which would ALSO collide with the already-FIXED session-types "F4" — MPST-merge-leaks-into-binary-duality — elsewhere in this same document. Follows the same collision-avoidance precedent as "the flaky narrowing HINT" finding, below.) Deferred by the widening plan itself (`specs/plans/2026-07-07-widening-capabilities-plan.md`, "Proof caps (F4) are DEFERRED to a later slice") — filed formally here since it had not yet been given its own `specs/todos.md` entry. **The mismatch, live-verified:** `capabilities.md`'s worked example shows `mod Db` minting `Cap(Db.Migrated)` via `execute_pending_migrations(raw); ()` — the literal `()` at the end of a `Cap(Db.Migrated)`-returning function body. That does NOT typecheck: `--check` rejects with `` expected `Cap(Db.Migrated)` but got `()` ``, exit 1. The mechanism that DOES satisfy Check 6's return-type rule is `cap_narrow` — a call to `cap_narrow(cap)` where `cap : Cap(IO)`, at a call site typed `Cap(Db.Migrated)`, typechecks cleanly and mints the proof cap (`typecheck.ml`'s `cap_narrow` builtin is declared with a **polymorphic** return type, `Cap(IO) -> Cap(a)`, documented in `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.8 as "also the mechanism a proof-cap mint... uses to satisfy Check 6's return-type rule"). But `cap_narrow` is the SAME builtin used for ordinary least-privilege IO-cap narrowing (`Cap(IO) -> Cap(IO.Network)`, etc.) — nothing distinguishes "narrowing to a real IO sub-cap" from "minting an unrelated proof capability" at the type level, so the unforgeability properties `capabilities.md` documents ("only public functions of `mod Db` can mint it," "the runtime-provided `Cap(IO)` cannot produce it") do not actually hold: **live-verified**, a one-line `fn run_migrations(cap : Cap(IO)) : Cap(Db.Migrated) do cap_narrow(cap) end` — outside `mod Db`, given only an ordinary `needs IO` `Cap(IO)`, with **no `needs Db.Migrated` declared** — typechecks with `--check` exit 0 (only the unrelated Check-3 narrowing HINT fires, suggesting a narrower IO sub-cap; no error). So any code holding a plain `Cap(IO)` can mint any nominal proof capability by name, with zero declaration or provenance requirement, contradicting the tutorial's "unforgeable" claim. A genuine, narrow gap in the proof-cap mechanism (Check 6), not a doc bug — `capabilities.md` is reconciled (Task 7) to flag the documented idiom as a known mismatch pending a later proof-caps-focused slice; not fixed here (out of this docs-only slice's scope, and out of F2/F3's fix scope). See `specs/lang/capabilities.md`'s "Proof caps" section for the reader-facing note and `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.8 for the compiler-facing citation.
- [ ] **A `match` with a pattern GUARD short-circuits the exhaustiveness checker entirely, so a guarded, genuinely non-exhaustive match inside a `cap no_panic` module is neither recorded nor rejected — a residual soundness gap in `cap no_panic`'s guarantee that predates and is out of scope for F3's fix.** Found during capabilities widening Task 7 (closeout, 2026-07-07) while reviewing F3's fix for completeness. `check_exhaustiveness` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml:3292-3297`) begins with `` let has_guards = List.exists (fun (br : Ast.branch) -> br.branch_guard <> None) branches in if has_guards then () else ... `` — if ANY arm of the match carries a `when` guard, the function returns immediately, before ever computing `find_missing_mc`'s coverage verdict, before emitting even the ordinary (non-cap, non-blocking) `` Non-exhaustive pattern match `` Warning a guard-free non-exhaustive match would get, and — critically for F3's mechanism — **before ever recording the match's span into `env.nonexhaustive_match_spans`**, the side-table F3's fix added for `check_no_panic_module` to consult. So a `cap no_panic` module containing a guarded, non-exhaustive match is invisible to BOTH the ordinary warning AND F3's new error path: `--check` exits 0 with **zero diagnostics of any kind**. Live-verified: `` mod GuardGap do cap no_panic; fn classify(n : Int) : Int do match n do x when x > 0 -> 1 end end end `` — a match with only a guarded arm, no catch-all, clearly missing coverage for `n <= 0` — typechecks with `--check` exit 0, no warning, no error; calling `classify(-5)` at runtime (interpreted) aborts with `` panic: match failure — Non-exhaustive pattern match: no branch matched the value -5. ``, exit 1 — exactly the failure class `cap no_panic` exists to rule out at compile time, slipping through uncaught. **Not a regression and not in F3's scope** — `check_exhaustiveness`'s guard short-circuit is pre-existing behavior (guard coverage is undecidable in general without a guard-aware SMT-backed analysis, per the function's own doc comment, `:3291`: "Skips the check when any branch has a guard (coverage becomes undecidable)"); F3's fix correctly wires into whatever verdict `check_exhaustiveness` already computes, and inherits this pre-existing blind spot rather than introducing a new one. Filed here as a residual, open gap in `cap no_panic`'s soundness guarantee — fixing it would need a guard-aware (likely Z3-backed, similar to `division_safety.ml`) exhaustiveness analysis, a substantially larger undertaking than F3's span-plumbing fix; out of scope for this docs-only closeout task. See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.11 for the cross-reference.

### Compiler: Session types (protocols/channels)

- ✅ **F4 — the MPST merge rule leaked into binary duality: a `choose` with two structurally-identical-type branches was wrongly rejected as "not duals of each other." — FIXED 2026-07-07 (fix-campaign pilot, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`).** The `ProtoChoice` merge-rule branch is now gated on `multiparty` (one added conjunct): a binary protocol's non-chooser always projects to `SOffer{…}` (it observes the choice); only a true MPST (>2-role) bystander merges. Verified live — identical-`Int`-branch binary protocol now `--check`s exit 0 (was "not duals"); MPST 3-role bystander merge byte-identically preserved; types corpus 78/78 (+`accept/t44_binary_choice_identical_branches`); regression tests `test_session_binary_choice_identical_branches` + `test_session_mpst_bystander_still_merges`. Original analysis retained below for history. Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.7.5 and §4.1 finding 20 (session-types widening, Task 2, 2026-07-06). Cause: `project_steps`'s `ProtoChoice` arm (`typecheck.ml:5906–5919`) applies the standard MPST mergeability rule — "if all branches project to the exact same local type for a non-chooser role, collapse them into that one type (the role need not observe the choice)" — UNCONDITIONALLY, including when `multiparty = false` (an ordinary 2-role binary protocol). For a binary protocol the "non-chooser" role is the chooser's ONLY peer, not a bystander the merge rule was designed to exempt. When both `choose` branches happen to carry an identical payload type (`session_ty_exact_equal`, `typecheck.ml:2058`), the peer's projection collapses from `SOffer {...}` to that single shared type, which is then no longer the dual of the chooser's `SChoose {...}` — so `project_protocol`'s binary duality check (`:5972–5986`) rejects a perfectly legal protocol. Minimal repro (verified live):
  ```march
  protocol Decision do
    choose by Client:
      ok  -> Server -> Client : Int
      err -> Server -> Client : Int
    end
  end
  ```
  `march --check` on this exits **1**:
  ```
  Protocol `Decision`: the projection onto `Client` and the projection onto `Server` are not duals of each other.
  dual(Client) = Offer{ok: Send(Int, End), err: Send(Int, End)}
  but Server has: Send(Int, End)
  ```
  (The printed `Server` projection is already the MERGED `Send(Int, End)`, not a `Choose{...}` — the tell that the merge rule fired.) Making the branches type-distinct (`err -> Server -> Client : String`) typechecks clean (exit 0) with no other change — confirmed live. **Impact:** any binary protocol author whose two `choose` branches happen to share a payload type (a common, unremarkable shape, e.g. two branches both replying with a plain `Int` status code) hits a spurious rejection whose message talks about "duals" rather than anything resembling the actual cause. Fix direction: gate the merge-rule branch in `ProtoChoice` on `multiparty`, only merging for the true MPST non-chooser case (a binary protocol's peer role should always get `SOffer`, never a collapsed type). Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice, out of scope for a reference-widening task); a `reject/` corpus program was deliberately NOT added for this repro (it currently, incorrectly, typechecks as accepted when branches differ, and incorrectly rejects when they match — a `reject/` witness for either behavior would codify the bug as intended). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.7.5 for the full writeup and both live-verified repros (reject + accept sides).
- [ ] **F5 — `Chan.offer` always returns the FIRST branch's continuation type, regardless of which branch the peer actually chose at runtime.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.7.9 and §4.1 finding 21 (session-types widening, Task 3, 2026-07-06). Cause: `Chan.offer`'s arm (`typecheck.ml:3614`, requiring `SOffer branches`) computes its result continuation as `match branches with (_, sty) :: _ -> TLin (Linear, TChan (ref sty)) | [] -> TError` (`:3624–3627`) — always the FIRST entry of the branch list, never indexed by the `Atom` label the op also returns. The arm's own comment (`:3607–3613`) is explicit that this is deliberate: "the exact continuation is not known statically without dependent types, so we return the first branch's continuation type as a conservative approximation." **Impact — a real, narrow soundness gap:** for an `SOffer` whose branches lead to DIFFERENT continuations (e.g. `Decision`'s `ok -> Send(Int, End)` vs `err -> Send(String, End)`), the returned channel's static type is simply wrong whenever the peer actually picked any branch other than the first — a program that pattern-matches the returned label and then tries to drive the channel per-branch (e.g. `Chan.send` a `String` after matching `:err`) is rejected at typecheck time even though it is exactly what the *runtime* session requires, because the type never left the first branch's continuation. Repro (live-verified, `specs/lang/types/accept/t43_choose_offer_roundtrip.march` sidesteps this by having the chooser always pick the first-listed branch, so the approximation happens to be exact there — this is not a counterexample, just a witness that doesn't trigger the gap):
  ```march
  protocol Decision do
    choose by Client:
      ok  -> Client -> Server : Int
      err -> Client -> Server : String
    end
  end
  ```
  `Chan.offer(sc)` on the `Server` endpoint is typed `(Atom, Chan(Server, Send(Int, End)))` unconditionally — even on a run where the peer chose `:err` and the true session is at `Send(String, End)`. Fix direction: branch-indexed / dependent typing of the returned continuation on the returned `Atom` value (refine the tuple's second component per `match` arm over the first). Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice); no `reject/` corpus program added (it typechecks — wrongly permissively — today; a `reject/` witness would codify the gap as intended). See `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.7.9 for the full writeup and worked example.
- [ ] **F3 — COMPILED: every MPST (`MPST.*`) program segfaults (exit 139), even with all-String payloads and a correctly-interleaved, typechecking-clean protocol.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 and §4.1 finding 22 (session-types widening, Task 4, 2026-07-06). The interpreter runtime is complete and correct for MPST (`mpst_new`/`mpst_send`/`mpst_recv`/`mpst_close`, `eval.ml:2681/2710/2724/2743`); the compiled backend lowers and links the same ops (`lib/tir/lower.ml`, `defun.ml:161–165` builtin registration, `llvm_builtins.ml:646–664` C-symbol table) but the C runtime (`runtime/march_extras.c:1463+`, the `march_mpst_session` struct and its `MPST_SESSION(ep)` fixed-offset-pointer accessor at `:1564`) is not correctly wired to the lowered representation. Verified live (this task): a 3-role `Client -> Server -> Logger -> Client` all-`String` protocol, roles declared as their own nullary types (no F8 HINT), `--check` exit 0, interprets cleanly to `req-logged-done` (exit 0) — but the SAME program compiled crashes **exit 139 (SIGSEGV)**, deterministically. The in-repo compile tests (`test_session_compile_*`, `test/test_compiler.ml:2350–2434`) only assert `march_mpst_*` symbols *appear* in the emitted IR; none of them run the resulting binary, so this segfault is invisible to the existing green suite. **Impact: MPST is interp-only in practice — no MPST program can be a `golden/` witness** (a crashing compiled binary can never produce byte-identical output). Fix direction: audit the `MPST_SESSION` offset/layout contract between the lowering (`lib/tir/lower.ml`) and the C struct (`runtime/march_extras.c:1359–1620`) — likely the same class of "asymmetric backend wiring" as F1/F2, but in the endpoint/session-pointer plane rather than the payload-tagging plane. Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice, out of scope for a reference-widening task); no `golden/` MPST witness exists or should exist until this is fixed. See `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 for the operational writeup and the live segfault repro.
- [ ] **F6 — SEMANTICS (scope boundary, not a bug to fix): the channel runtime has no scheduler, so a `recv` called before its matching `send` in program order typechecks cleanly but deadlocks/aborts at runtime on BOTH backends.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 and §4.1 finding 23 (session-types widening, Task 4, 2026-07-06). The channel runtime is synchronous, single-threaded, non-blocking: `chan_recv` (`eval.ml:2655`) does not suspend when its `ce_in_q` queue is empty — it raises a hard error immediately (`eval.ml:2660`, `"Chan.recv: channel %s#%d has no pending value — did you run the sender first?"`); there is no coroutine/actor-mailbox integration for channels, so both endpoints of a session must run in the same straight-line thread with every `send` textually ordered before its matching `recv`. Verified live (this task): a program calling a `server_side` function (whose FIRST op is `Chan.recv`) before a `client_side` function (whose `Chan.send` would have satisfied it) — a protocol that typechecks (`--check` exit 0, session-state advancement is purely a static per-op check, oblivious to call order) — dies at runtime on both backends: interpreted, `Chan.recv: channel Echo#0 has no pending value — did you run the sender first?` (exit 1); compiled, `march: Chan.recv on empty channel queue (role 1)` then abort (exit 134). Neither the type system nor any runtime scheduler prevents the ordering mistake; both backends simply agree it is a fatal error once it happens. **This is a documented scope boundary, not a defect to fix:** session types here are a linear protocol-*conformance* checker layered over a same-thread mailbox, not a concurrent-session / coroutine system — the type system guarantees "the protocol steps you take advance a valid session state," not "your calls into that protocol are scheduled in the order the protocol requires." Filed here for reference-completeness (no fix proposed; a scheduler/coroutine integration would be a substantial feature, out of scope for a docs-widening task). See `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 for the full writeup and both live-verified backend error messages.
- [ ] **F7 — LINEARITY: session-channel linearity is enforced by the generic `let`-binding linear tracker, not by session-specific accounting — so two shapes slip through uncaught.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 and §4.1 finding 24 (session-types widening, Task 4, 2026-07-06; the underlying probes are from the session-types survey §3 P8). Verified live (this task), both accepted with `--check` exit 0 (no error, both are HOLES): (1) **dropping an unclosed `SEnd` channel** — a program that never calls `Chan.close` on either endpoint once both reach session state `End` (values `cc3`/`sc3` simply go unused after the final `Chan.recv`) typechecks and runs cleanly; the "every channel must be closed" discipline is not enforced by any linear-drop check. (2) **reusing a linear *parameter* endpoint whose session state coincidentally still matches** — a function `use_twice(ch : Chan(Client, Two))` for a `Two`-role protocol `Client -> Server : Int; Client -> Server : Int` that calls `Chan.send(ch, 1)` and THEN `Chan.send(ch, 2)` — reading the *original* parameter binding `ch` twice rather than threading the first call's returned continuation — typechecks and runs cleanly, because both calls independently see `ch`'s *parameter-declared* type (`Send(Int, Send(Int, End))`) rather than the advanced-by-one-step type the first `Chan.send`'s result actually carries; the generic linear tracker only rejects re-*use* of a `let`-bound name that was already consumed, and a function parameter is not re-bound by a `let`, so this reuse is invisible to it. **Net: the "consume each channel exactly once, at the exact advancing state" guarantee that session types are supposed to provide only actually holds for `let`-bound continuations threaded through in the same scope** — parameter reuse and unclosed-drop both slip through. Filed here (not fixed — docs-only widening slice); no `reject/` corpus program added for either shape (both currently, incorrectly, typecheck as accepted — a `reject/` witness would codify the gap as intended, not demonstrate correct rejection). See `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 for the full writeup and both live-verified repros.
- [ ] **F8 — DIAGNOSTIC noise only (NOT a golden/stdout hazard): an undeclared protocol participant emits a HINT to STDERR at typecheck time.** Found and pinned by `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 and §4.1 finding 25 (session-types widening, Task 4, 2026-07-06). `DProtocol`'s handling (`typecheck.ml:~7057–7066`) hints, for every participant name that is not a known actor or declared type: `"Protocol \`%s\`: participant \`%s\` is not a known actor or type. Did you forget to declare \`actor %s ...\`?"`. Verified live (this task): a protocol naming fresh, genuinely-undeclared roles (`Xylophone`/`Quibble`, chosen to avoid an accidental stdlib-type collision — an earlier probe using bare `Client`/`Server` produced NO hint because `stdlib/http_client.march`/`http_server.march` already declare `type Client`/`type Server`, which is itself a small side-observation about the global type namespace) prints two `-- HINT --` blocks to stderr, both under `--check` and under a plain interpreted run — confirmed with `2>/dev/null` (empty, HINT does not land on stdout) vs `2>&1 1>/dev/null` (both HINT blocks present). **This is cosmetic diagnostic noise, not a correctness or golden-corpus issue**: `specs/lang/golden/verify.sh` compares interpreter STDOUT against the compiled binary's STDOUT+STDERR — the HINT is emitted by the *compiler's own* typecheck pass (to the compiler process's stderr, at compile time), never by the *program*'s own runtime stdout or stderr on either backend, so it lands in neither side of the comparison and cannot cause a golden mismatch. Both `g38`/`g39` (this task) sidestep it anyway by declaring their roles as their own nullary types (`type Client = Client`), which is optional hygiene, not a requirement. Filed here purely for reference-completeness / diagnostic-quality tracking, not because it threatens any corpus. See `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11 for the live-verified stdout/stderr split.

### Runtime: Hot Code Reloading

Spec: `specs/hot-code-reload.md`. Erlang-style live code swapping for deployed Bastion apps. **Two key findings from the design review:** (1) March's whole-program optimization (mono, defun, Perceus/borrow, LTO) is in tension with module isolation — hot reload must *forfeit* those at the boundary; two execution models, **Model A (interpreter trampoline)** shippable now, **Model B (native JIT)** research gated on a spike. (2) The CAS (`lib/cas/`) is the natural HCR substrate — per-SCC granularity, dual `sig_hash`/`impl_hash`, compiler/runtime identity already in the artifact key, two-tier content-addressed store — so it replaces the bespoke ABI table, source-diff, shipping protocol, and integer-ID scheme **once `did_hash` is Merkle-populated**. Phasing is CAS-prerequisite-first, then trampoline. **(RESOLVED prerequisite: the `impl_hash` Merkle-root population is DONE — see Phase 1 below and `progress.md` 2026-06-21 "Hot Code Reload — Phase 1". The CAS substrate is now available; remaining HCR work is Phases 2–7.)**

- [ ] **Phase 0 — Spike (MANDATORY before any Model B work)** — Throwaway prototype proving native code can be swapped across a boundary without memory corruption: two app modules with a `Conn`-style borrowed-record boundary call, frozen RC/borrow ABI, hot-swap callee, run under ASan → zero double-frees/leaks; introduce a new closure shape flowing through the boundary → prove a no-defun-at-boundary scheme works (no defun fault) or conclude Model B can't defunctionalize the boundary; measure the boundary deoptimization on `bench/list_ops.march` + a Bastion request bench. Deliverable: go/no-go on Model B with real numbers. (Model A needs no spike.)
- ✅ **Phase 1 — CAS prerequisite (highest leverage; also fixed a real stale-cache bug)** — `impl_hash` is now a true Merkle root: `hash_module` (`lib/cas/pipeline.ml`) folds, into each definition's hash, both (a) the `impl_hash`es of its earlier-SCC callees (walking SCCs dependencies-first) and (b) the base hashes of every named type it *transitively* references. A callee body change propagates into every direct/transitive caller; a record/variant layout change propagates into every definition that uses the type directly or through another type. This closes the cross-SCC stale-cache hole (`known_call` inlines across SCCs, so `compile_scc`'s key was serving stale inlined callees) **and** the type-layout hole (TIR references types by name, so a layout change moved GEP offsets while leaving the user's bytes/hash unchanged) — the latter is HCR Part 5's shared-type soundness gate, now a hash comparison. **Implementation notes:** threaded callee/type hashes into the hash computation rather than *emitting* `ADefRef` nodes or adding types to `compute_sccs` (same Merkle guarantee, no TIR mutation; per-def callee sets via `Scc.deps_of`, type refs via a TCon walk over sig+body; types are folded by transitive closure of their base hashes, so no type-SCC machinery is needed and recursive types are cycle-safe). Externally-defined types (stdlib/deps) have no base hash and are simply not folded, mirroring the cross-SCC callee rule. Intra-SCC refs (self-recursion, `Group` members) are excluded from the fn fold (covered by the group hash). Regression tests in `test_cas.ml` `def_id` group (6 new): direct + two-hop transitive call propagation, unrelated-def isolation, direct + transitive type-layout propagation, unreferenced-type isolation (cas suite 44→50; compiler/codegen/eval/stdlib all green).
- ✅ **Phase 2 — Boundary + versioned dispatch (compiler + main-build)** — Boundary classifier, NAME_ID interning, runtime dispatch table (`march_dispatch.{c,h}`), `Llvm_emit` dispatch-call emission (enter/leave wrapping), inliner no-inline guard, real `impl_hash`es in `publish`, and runnable end-to-end test all complete. Known limitation: bare entry-file fn names in the single-file compiler path (`@b` not `@App.b`) — `forge build` qualifies by package name so real multi-file builds work; single-file `--hot-reload` only matches nested/library modules.
  - ✅ **Boundary classification** (`lib/tir/hot_reload.ml`, `is_reloadable`): a module is reloadable iff it is app code (under the package module prefix) or force-included, and not force-excluded (excludes win); module-path-aware prefix match (`MyApplication` is not under `MyApp`), so stdlib/deps/lookalikes are excluded. Pure, 6 TDD cases (`test/test_hot_reload.ml`).
  - ✅ **NAME_ID interning** (`Hot_reload.Name_table`): dense `name ↔ id` map with ids assigned in sorted-name order, so the mapping is a deterministic function of the boundary name set (not a source-order int that shifts on unrelated edits). Cross-build reload activation is keyed by name, so dense per-build ids suffice. 5 TDD cases.
  - ✅ **Runtime dispatch table** (`runtime/march_dispatch.{c,h}`): dense slot array indexed by `NAME_ID`, each slot a per-version ring (`MARCH_MAX_LIVE_VERSIONS`=2) of `{fn_ptr, atomic refs, impl_hash, kind, live}`. `march_dispatch_enter(name_id, &v)` pins the current version and reports its ring index so `leave(name_id, v)` unpins *that* version even after a concurrent `publish` advanced `current`; `publish` advances current and reclaims a non-current slot only when its refs have drained (else returns -1 → purge needed, a later phase). Release/acquire ordering on `current`. 7 TDD groups / 23 checks in `test/test_dispatch.c` (wired into `dune runtest`). (Epoch/grace reclamation for the enter-vs-reclaim race is deferred — noted in `march_dispatch_enter`.)
  - ✅ **`Llvm_emit` dispatch-call emission** — `emit_module ?hot_reload:Hot_reload.config option` (default `None` → existing codegen byte-identical). `ctx` carries the config, a `Name_table` of reloadable fn names → NAME_ID, and the current caller module; a `module_of_name` helper derives a fn's module from its qualified name. At a `Tir.EApp` call site, when `Hot_reload.needs_dispatch` holds (caller+callee both reloadable, callee a known top-level fn with a NAME_ID), the direct `call @callee(..)` is replaced by `alloca i32`; `%fp = call ptr @march_dispatch_enter(i32 NAME_ID, ptr %ver)`; indirect `call %fp(..)`; `load %ver`; `call @march_dispatch_leave(NAME_ID, %ver)`. Dispatch externs declared in the preamble. IR-level TDD in `test/test_hot_reload.ml` (`llvm_emit` group): with the flag the boundary call emits enter/leave and drops the direct call; without it the direct call is preserved. **Regression-clean: compiler 296, codegen 320 green** (the real compile path is unchanged with the flag off).
  - ✅ **Inliner no-inline guard** — `Inline.boundary_config` (set by `Opt.run ?hot_reload`) excludes reloadable fns from the inline-candidate set so a boundary→boundary call survives `Opt.run` as a real call site (small boundary callees would otherwise be inlined away before codegen). `module_of_name` promoted to `Hot_reload` as the single source of truth. 2 TDD cases (`test_hot_reload.ml` `inline_guard`): boundary callee not inlined under a config, inlined when off. Regression-clean (compiler 296, codegen 320 with the flag off).
  - ✅ **Runnable end-to-end — first hot-reload binary runs and genuinely dispatches.** Startup registration (`@main` emits `march_dispatch_init(n)` + a `march_dispatch_publish(NAME_ID, @fn, ptr null, NATIVE)` per boundary fn before user main spawns; impl_hash is null baseline, reload server stamps real hashes on activation — IR-tested). `march_dispatch.c` linked into the compile path (`bin/main.ml` `extra_c_files` — note there are *two* runtime-source lists; the `--compile` clang path uses `extra_c_files`, not `extra_files`). `--hot-reload <Prefix>` flag in `bin/main.ml` builds `Hot_reload.default_config Prefix`, threaded into both `emit_module` calls + `Opt.run`, and added to **both** `cas_flags` sites. **Verified:** a nested-module program (`Core.a`→recursive `Core.b`) compiled with `--hot-reload Core` emits the full dispatch sequence (init/publish/enter/leave), `Core.a`/`Core.b` survive the inliner (guard works in the real pipeline), the binary runs and prints identical output to the plain build. Regression-clean (compiler 296, codegen 320).
  - [ ] **Remaining (smaller follow-ups):** **boundary-naming for the entry module** — a single-file program's *top-level* functions are emitted bare (`@b`, not `@App.b`), so `--hot-reload <Prefix>` only matches *qualified* (nested/library) modules; the real `forge build` path qualifies modules by package name so this is the right model there, but the raw single-file compiler can't reload bare entry-file fns. ✅ **Real `impl_hash`es threaded into `publish`** — `emit_module` takes an optional `~impl_hashes:(name → 64-hex)` map; `bin/main.ml` builds it from the same `Pipeline.hash_module` SCC hashing that keys the artifact cache (`hd_impl_hash` per `FnDef`, across `HSingle`/`HGroup`), in both the `--compile` and `--emit-llvm`-only paths. In `hr_setup` each boundary fn's hash is emitted as a private NUL-terminated string global (`@.hr_hashN = [65 x i8]`) and passed to `march_dispatch_publish` as `ptr @.hr_hashN` instead of `ptr null` (null fallback retained for non-CAS paths). ✅ **Automated end-to-end test** (`test_stdlib_suite.ml` `adversarial-regressions`, Slow): compiles the nested-module `Core.a`→`Core.b` program with `--compile --hot-reload Core`, asserts it prints `dispatch works`, that the plain `--compile` build prints identically, and that `--emit-llvm --hot-reload Core` IR contains a real `call ptr @march_dispatch_enter` (not just the preamble `declare`). `--hot-reload` plumbed through `forge` + `config` from `forge.toml [hot-reload]`. Main-build symbol export (`-rdynamic`/un-stripped) — only needed for Model B (Phase 6) JIT resolution, not Model A; defer.
- ✅ **Phase 3 — Native reload server + `--compile-so` (shippable milestone, `dlopen` variant)** — Background pthread on a Unix-domain socket (`MARCH_HOT_RELOAD_SOCKET`). Text protocol: `PING → PONG`, `ABI_QUERY → SLOT id hash...END`, `ACTIVATE name impl_hash so_path → OK impl_hash | ERR reason`. Name registry (`march_dispatch_register_name`) maps human-readable function names to dispatch slot IDs at startup. `--compile-so` compiler flag produces a patch shared library (no `@main`, `-shared -fPIC`, `-undefined dynamic_lookup` / `--allow-shlib-undefined`): boundary functions exported as named symbols (`Server.square`, etc.). ACTIVATE: `dlopen(RTLD_NOW|RTLD_GLOBAL)` + `dlsym(handle, name)` + `march_dispatch_publish` — atomically replaces the function slot at runtime. `scripts/march-reload` client script: `--src` auto-compiles, `--so` uses pre-built. Verified end-to-end: `square(7) = 49 → 343` with live server, zero downtime. Works on Linux + macOS. `-Wl,-rdynamic` (Linux) / `-Wl,-export_dynamic` (macOS) export server symbols for patch resolution. `--compile-so` keyed into both `cas_flags` sites so cache can't silently ignore the mode. Demo: `demo/hcr_server.march` + `demo/hcr_client.march` + `scripts/rpc/hot-deploy.sh`. **Key design finding:** boundary functions must be in NESTED modules (`mod App do mod Server do fn square...end end`) — top-level module names are dropped in TIR, so `--hot-reload Prefix` only matches qualified (`Prefix.fn`) names. Remaining: Model A interpreter trampoline (zero-downtime without pre-compiling a patch); actor state migration (Phase 5); fleet tooling (Phase 7).
- ✅ **Phase 4 — `forge deploy hot` (CAS-native)** — Reload set = `impl_hash` set-diff vs the running process's active set (via `ABI_QUERY`), NOT a source diff. Safety gate = per-changed-def `sig_hash` comparison (ABI break rejected). Distribution = inline CAS over the SSH-tunneled Unix socket (`CAS_CHECK`/`CAS_PUT`). `VERSIONS` for crash-restart drift detection. Ed25519 signing: `march_deploy_hot keygen`/`show-pubkey` (`lib/ed25519/` OCaml library wrapping vendored `runtime/tweetnacl.c`); `--signing-pubkey` embeds the public key in the server binary; `ACTIVATE <name> <impl_hash> <cas_hash> <sig64>` verifies before dlopen. `--compile-so` emits a `.hcr_manifest` sidecar (cas_hash + per-fn impl_hash/sig_hash). `bin/main.ml`: fixed `remote_sig_hashes` (now passed to emit_module for all fns, not just RPC), manifest writing, `tweetnacl.c` linked with main binary for server-side verify. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-24-hcr-phase4-design.md`. Implementation: `runtime/tweetnacl.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}` (sig_hash, baseline_impl_hash, id→name), `runtime/march_reload.c` (CAS_CHECK/CAS_PUT/VERSIONS/signed-ACTIVATE), `forge/lib/cmd_hot_reload.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/lib/project.ml` ([hot-reload] section), `lib/ed25519/`.
- ✅ **Phase 5 — Actor state migration** — Per-actor versioned handler dispatch with migrate-BEFORE-run ordering. **Runtime (Part A):** `MARCH_MIGRATE_TAG` system message + `march_migrate_msg_t` (malloc/free, not RC); `dispatch_name_id` field in `march_actor_meta`; `actor_green_thread` dispatches MIGRATE before the alive gate, calling a `migrate_fn` pointer that swaps the actor's state ptr atomically; `march_actor_set_dispatch_id` (called from spawn wrapper); `march_actor_broadcast_migrate` (two-phase: snapshot actors under lock, inject messages outside lock to avoid deadlock). State indirection in `--hot-reload` builds: actor struct holds `$f_state: TCon(State,[])` at field 2 (pointer-swappable); handlers load state fields from `$f_state`, reuse updates the inner record + writes the new ptr; spawn passes initial state record directly. `__migrate_*` LLVM alias exported from `-so` builds so `march_reload.c` can `dlsym` it. ACTIVATE extended with `migrate_required` flag; `march_reload.c` dlsyms `__migrate_<actor>` and calls `broadcast_migrate`. Spawn wrapper pattern in `llvm_emit.ml`: `*_spawn` emitted as `*_spawn_impl` + thin wrapper calling `march_actor_set_dispatch_id`. **Type guardrails (Part B):** `.schemas.json` sidecar emitted alongside `.so`/`.hcr_manifest` (actor name → `compat` + `state_fields[]`); `forge/lib/schema_diff.ml` (pure OCaml JSON parser + `diff_schemas`/`check_compat`/`requires_migration`); `@compat(forward|full|any)` actor annotation (`lib/ast/ast.ml` `actor_compat` field, parser extended); deploy-time compat check in `cmd_deploy_hot.ml` aborts if new schema is incompatible; `migrate_required` computed from `schema_diff.requires_migration`. Demo: `demo/hcr_actor_demo/counter_v1.march` + `counter_v2.march` (adds `history: List(Int)` field with `migrate_state`) + `run_demo.sh`. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-24-hcr-phase5-plan.md`.
- ✅ **Phase 5C Parts A & B — Capability-safe hot deploys (manifest + monotonicity gate)** — Make hot deploys *authorized*, not just *authenticated* (Phase 4 ed25519 signs but doesn't gate what the patch may *do*; static caps are erased so a signed patch can call any IO). **Parts A & B done (commits df323bb7–f94a5c3e):** **(A)** emit each boundary fn's transitive capability closure (the data `check_module_needs` already computes, `typecheck.ml:5125`) + a `cap_root` digest into the `.hcr_manifest` — `lib/caps/cap_lattice.ml` factors `io_cap_hierarchy`/`cap_subsumes`, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` records fully-qualified per-fn closures via `fn_capability_closures`, `bin/main.ml` emits `caps=<csv>` + `ROOT cap_root=<hex>` (BLAKE3). **(B)** **monotonicity gate** at `forge deploy hot` — a deploy may *narrow* the live system's authority freely but *widening* (a new cap not subsumed by any prior-held cap, via shared `cap_subsumes`) aborts before ACTIVATE unless an explicit signed `--grant-cap <C>` authorizes it (the capability dual of Phase 5 Part B's schema variance table) — `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml` reads `.hcr_manifest.prev` baseline, computes widening/narrowing, aborts if widening; `forge/bin/main.ml` + `cmd_deploy_hot.ml` add `--grant-cap` flag with audit-line printing. Verification: full suite green (384 compiler / 230 eval / 364 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march); `scripts/check-docs.sh` passes. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-hcr-phase5c-capability-safe-deploys.md`.
- ✅ **Phase 5C final whole-branch review fixes (C1/I2/M3/M5)** — a final whole-branch review of Parts A & B (after all six individual tasks were reviewed and approved) found a genuine Critical gap plus three smaller issues. **C1 (Critical):** actor handler capabilities were silently dropped from the manifest — `record_fn_caps` was never called for actor handlers, so a handler doing IO compiled clean with an empty `caps=` manifest field. Fixed in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s `check_module_needs`: handler bodies now feed `record_fn_caps` (keyed by the actor's bare name + `_MsgName`, matching TIR's un-qualified handler naming) and the same missing-`needs` diagnostic `DFn`/`DLet` bodies already trip. **I2:** `compute_cap_narrowing` (`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`) now uses `Cap_lattice.cap_subsumes` instead of exact match (was misreporting narrowing when a broader new cap still covers a prior specific one). **M3:** diagnostic column padding now computed dynamically instead of a hardcoded 12. **M5:** `attribute_widening` now sorts `manifest.functions` by name before searching, for reproducible "(from <fn>)" attribution. Verification: full suite green (391 compiler / 230 eval / 364 codegen / 793 stdlib); `forge/test/test_forge.exe` 112/112. Full report: `.superpowers/sdd/final-review-fix-report.md`.
- ✅ **Phase 5C Part C — Node-local admission (cap_root tamper check, migrate_state IO-free bound)** — Complete across five tasks (C1–C5, all reviewed and approved). **(As-built design):** `forge deploy hot` emits the artifact's capability **union** (tiny, ~11 caps per build) as an unsigned `caps:` field in the `ACTIVATE4` wire message; the server-side admission gate (`march_reload.c`'s `ACTIVATE4` handler) recomputes `cap_root` from this wire-carried set (sort+uniq → `march_cap_normalize` → join `\n` → `march_blake3_hex`) and tamper-checks it against the signed `cap_root` (error `err_cap_tamper` on mismatch). A `MARCH_DEPLOY_POLICY` env var (newline-delimited capability paths; absent ⇒ permissive) gates admission via `march_cap_subsumes` on the union (`err_cap_policy <cap>` if any cap in the union is not subsumed by policy). Plus compile-time **`migrate_state` IO-free bound** enforced at typecheck (any function matching the `_migrate_state` naming convention must have empty own-capability closure; error if any IO cap present). All gates opt-in/additive (legacy artifacts + no policy ⇒ permissive). Follow-ons: IR-level NoAlloc/no_panic `migrate_state` bound (needs `policy_dce`); refinement-verified migration totality (Z3); capability leases across deploys; extract `is_migrate_fn_name` predicate into `march_ast` for a single source (currently duplicated between `Tir_names` and `typecheck.ml`). **C1 done:** `lib/caps/emit_c_table.ml` (new `executable`) reads `Cap_lattice.hierarchy` and writes checked-in `runtime/march_cap_lattice.{h,c}` (static `{path,parent}` table + `march_cap_subsumes`/`march_cap_normalize`, semantics identical to the OCaml functions), so the later node-local admission gate can do capability checks without drifting from the OCaml source of truth. **First OCaml-generates-checked-in-C precedent in the repo**, with anti-drift CI check (`test/dune`'s `cap_lattice_check` alias, wired into `runtest`). Anti-drift verified by regenerating+diffing at test time; committed files carry "DO NOT EDIT" banner. **C2 done:** `runtime/march_blake3.{c,h}` — pure-C BLAKE3 hex helper matching `lib/cas/blake3_stubs.c`, linked into `bin/main.ml`'s runtime clang path; `lib/cas/discover.ml` emits a shared `blake3_flags.ml` for no divergence between `bin` and `lib/cas` libblake3-discovery. **C3 done:** `ACTIVATE4` admission in `runtime/march_reload.c` — after sig-verify, recomputes `cap_root` server-side from wire-carried `caps:` set, tamper-checks vs signed value, then `MARCH_DEPLOY_POLICY` subsumption check. Empty/absent caps ⇒ permissive. Real socket-driven end-to-end test harness. **C4 done:** `forge deploy hot` emits `ACTIVATE4` (cap_root signed, caps unsigned, both before callers) when manifest has cap_root; `--no-cap-gate` and legacy manifests fall back to byte-identical `ACTIVATE3`. Old servers emit `ERR unknown_command` with actionable message. **C5 done:** compile-time `migrate_state` IO-free bound — added own-caps projection to `record_fn_caps` (avoiding false positives from module-wide caps merge), consolidated five inline `_migrate_state` sniff sites to `Tir_names.is_migrate_fn_name`/`is_migrate_fn_for`, added typecheck "Check 8" (any `_migrate_state` fn must have empty own-caps). Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-hcr-phase5c-capability-safe-deploys.md`.
- ✅ **Phase 5C Granularity revision (2026-07-04) — gate per-activated-function, not the whole artifact** — Discovered by a real deploy test: `--hot-reload` links the entire stdlib, so the Part C whole-artifact cap **union** (`cap_root`) was **app-invariant** (~11 caps, byte-identical across a minimal console app and a stdlib-heavy one) — the `MARCH_DEPLOY_POLICY` gate and Part B's monotonicity gate were both effectively inert. Fix: gate each `ACTIVATE4` (already per-function) on THAT function's OWN caps, not the artifact union. **`bin/main.ml`:** `.hcr_manifest`'s `caps=` field now sourced from `fn_own_capability_closures` (C5's own-caps projection) instead of the module-merged `fn_capability_closures`; the whole-artifact `ROOT cap_root=<hex>` line is deleted entirely (no per-manifest cap_root any more). **`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`:** `fn_manifest` gains `fn_has_caps` (true iff the FN line carried a `caps=` field at all, distinguishing a genuine pre-Phase-5C legacy manifest — no fn has the field — from a current manifest where a capless fn legitimately has `fn_caps = []`); new `is_legacy_manifest`; new `fn_cap_root : string list -> string` computing a per-function cap_root (`normalize` → `List.sort` → `concat "\n"` → `Blake3.hash_string`), verified byte-identical to `runtime/march_reload.c`'s `compute_cap_root` recipe (split → sort_uniq → `march_cap_normalize` → join → blake3) for the tested single-cap/multi-cap/subsumed cases; each `ACTIVATE4` now sends its own function's own caps (`caps:`) and that function's own `fn_cap_root` (`cap_root:`), never the artifact union; the monotonicity gate (Part B) is rescoped to the union over `to_activate` (the functions actually being deployed) vs. each same-named function's caps in the `.hcr_manifest.prev` baseline, instead of the whole-manifest union; `manifest_caps` (whole-artifact union helper) retired. `runtime/march_reload.c` is unchanged — its recompute-and-compare logic is generic over any cap set. Verification: full suite green (397 compiler / 230 eval / 364 codegen / 794 stdlib, `forge/test/test_forge.exe` 123/123); new load-bearing stdlib test `test_hcr_manifest_disjoint_fn_caps_not_whole_artifact_union` proves two functions with disjoint IO (`println` vs `file_write`) get disjoint `caps=IO.Console` / `caps=IO.FileWrite` manifest lines, not a shared union; manual compile of a minimal `needs IO.Console` app confirmed its handler's manifest line reads `caps=IO.Console` (not the ~11-cap stdlib union) with no `ROOT` line anywhere in the file. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-hcr-phase5c-capability-safe-deploys.md`, "Granularity revision (2026-07-04)".

Background upgrade of trampolined SCCs to native O2 (keyed by the same `impl_hash`); no-defun-at-boundary scheme per the spike; measured perf.
- ✅ **Phase 7 — Fleet, registry, tooling (P1–P6 of 7)** — `--env` server lists + `--canary <n>` health-check gate (`cmd_deploy_hot.ml`): `deploy_env` fans out to `[[hot-reload.env]]` servers (TOML parser now supports `[[array-of-tables]]`; `project.ml` gains `hr_envs : hot_reload_env list`; sequential deployment with optional canary window + PING health-check polling; rollback on canary failure prints error, leaves remaining servers untouched). Audit log: every ACTIVATE (pass or fail) appends one JSON line to `$MARCH_AUDIT_LOG` (default `$HOME/.local/share/march/audit.jsonl`) in `march_reload.c` — fields: `ts/type/fn/impl_hash/signer/cas_hash/result`. `VERSIONS_DETAIL` protocol command: `SLOT <id> <name> <impl_hash> <activated_at_ms> <signer_hex>`, backed by new `activated_at_ms`/`signer_hex` per-slot metadata in `march_dispatch.c`. `forge hot-reload status`: connects via SSH tunnel, sends `VERSIONS_DETAIL`, prints a dashboard table. `forge hot-reload init`: `git log` analysis → suggest `[hot-reload] exclude = [...]` for rarely-changing modules. `forge hot-reload keygen --rotate`: versioned `ed25519_secret_v<N>.key` file + `forge hot-reload use-key <path>`. Remaining: third CAS tier (`s3://`/`https://` pull-through; P7 of 7).
- ✅ **Phase 8 — Cross-module function signature evolution** — Coordinated upgrade gate: `.hcr_manifest` emits a `callers:` field per exported function; server stores caller set per slot (`callers_str` in `MarchDispatchSlot`); ACTIVATE command parses the optional `callers:` suffix and stores it via `march_dispatch_set_callers`; ABI_QUERY responses include `callers:` when set. Hard sig_hash rejection replaced with: if all server-recorded callers of the changed function are included in `to_activate`, allow the deploy; otherwise error naming the uncovered callers. **Phase 9 (epoch-tagged dispatch):** compiler emits `@__march_hcr_epoch = private global i32 0` (file-static cell) and `@__march_init(i32)` (exported init fn) into every `--compile-so` output; `GET_EPOCH` server command assigns a monotonic epoch per deploy batch; after `dlopen`, the server calls `dlsym(handle,"__march_init")` to stamp the epoch; boundary call sites in `.so` mode call `march_dispatch_enter_gen(NAME_ID, epoch, &v)` which routes to the newest ring slot whose epoch ≤ caller_epoch, so old deployed code naturally calls the old function version until it is redeployed; Phase 8 hard block downgrades to advisory warning when Phase 9 active. Key files: `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `runtime/march_dispatch.c`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-cross-module-versioning.md` (Phase 8) and `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-phase9-epoch-dispatch.md` (Phase 9). **dlclose GC:** `MarchFnVersion.handle` stores the `dlopen` handle; `march_dispatch_set_handle` wires it in after ACTIVATE; `slot_dlclose` releases it on ring-slot reclaim (in `march_dispatch_publish`) and at server shutdown (`march_dispatch_shutdown`). **Phase 10 (cluster-aware hot deploy):** shared epoch — `get_epoch_from_server` fetches once from the epoch-master node (first in `[[hot-reload.env]]` list); all ACTIVATEs in the batch carry the same epoch so cross-node epoch routing is meaningful. Rolling deploy (default strategy) — `rolling_deploy` iterates servers one at a time; after each success forge optionally GETs the configured `health_check_url` (curl exit 0 = healthy) and stops on failure. `strategy = "simultaneous"` option for parallel fan-out. `VERSIONS_DETAIL` response now includes a 6th `epoch` field (`march_dispatch_epoch` accessor + `%u` in snprintf). `forge hot-reload status` extended: multi-node fan-out, Node column, epoch column, `← drift` marker when impl_hash differs across nodes. `--env` flag added to filter by env name. `[hot-reload] health_check_url` and `strategy` fields added to `project.ml`. Key files: `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `forge/lib/project.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/bin/main.ml`. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-clustering.md`.

## P4 — Long-Term / Research

### Metatheory

- [ ] **Lean 4 mechanized metatheory** — Proof of type safety (progress + preservation) for the core March type system. Plan documented in `specs/plans/lean4-metatheory-plan.md`. Requires: formalized grammar, operational semantics, type rules, mechanized proofs of canonical forms lemma, substitution lemma, and soundness theorem.

### Compiler Backend

- [ ] **Query-based/demand-driven compiler architecture** — Current pipeline is linear (parse → desugar → typecheck → eval/TIR). Design goal is a query-based architecture (like `rustc`'s `salsa`) for fine-grained incremental recompilation. Deferred post-v1.
- ✅ **Constraint solver for type-level naturals (v1)** — `normalize_tnat` reduces concrete arithmetic (`2+3 → 5`, `(1+2)*3 → 9`) and applies identity/annihilation laws (`n+0 → n`, `n*1 → n`, `n*0 → 0`). `solve_nat_eq` in `unify` solves linear equations (`a+2=5 → a=3`). Parser extended: `INT` as `TyNat`, `+`/`*` in type position via `ty_nat_add`/`ty_nat_mul` levels. 9 new tests in `type_level_nat` group.
- ✅ **Record field auto-satisfy (Feature 2)** — Zero-declaration rule: when discharging `CInterface(Iface, TRecord[...])`, auto-satisfy single-method accessor-shaped interfaces (`a -> T`) when the record has a matching field. Applies only to anonymous `TRecord` types (not named aliases), single-method interfaces, and exact field name+type match. 9 tests in `record_auto_satisfy` group. Spec: `specs/structural-interface-satisfaction.md`.
- ✅ **`satisfy` declaration (Feature 1)** — New `satisfy Iface for T` keyword that generates an `impl` block from matching functions already in scope. `SATISFY` lexer token, `DSatisfy` AST node, `expand_satisfy` desugar expansion, passthrough in eval/lower/typecheck/format/LSP/coverage/refactor/span_remap. 10 tests in `satisfy_decl` group. Spec: `specs/structural-interface-satisfaction.md`.

### Compiler Optimizations (Planned)

See `specs/optimizations.md` for full catalog with effort/impact/dependency details.

- ✅ **Borrow inference and elision (P7)** — `lib/tir/borrow.ml` + `lib/tir/perceus.ml` integration. Pre-Perceus analysis marks TCon/TString/TPtr params as borrowed when they are only read (pattern-matched, field-accessed) and never stored, returned, or passed to owning positions. Fixpoint inter-procedural: params passed only to other borrowed positions remain borrowed. RC elision: (a) call sites skip EIncRC for borrowed args still live after the call; (b) callee suppresses scrutinee EDecRC for borrowed params; (c) last-use borrowed args get caller-side EDecRC instead of callee-side. Eliminates 100% of RC overhead for read-only middlewares (HTTP Conn pattern). 10 new tests in `borrow_inference` group.
- ✅ **Known-call optimization** — `lib/tir/known_call.ml`: replaces `ECallPtr` with direct `EApp` when the closure allocation is visible in scope. Runs between Defun and Perceus + in Opt fixed-point loop. Inline threshold raised 15→50. Struct update fusion (`Fusion.run_struct`) fuses chains of `EUpdate` operations into a single update. 8 new tests (known_call × 4, struct_fusion × 4).
- ✅ **Mutual TCO** — Shared loop with dispatch: `find_mutual_tco_groups` (Tarjan SCC + qualification), `emit_mutual_tco_group` (combined dispatch fn + thin wrappers), `EApp` back-edge for mutual calls. 5 tests in `mutual_tco_codegen` group. `bench/mutual_recursion.march`.
- ✅ **Case-of-known-constructor / Beta-ADT (P11)** — `lib/tir/beta_adt.ml`: matches `let r = EAlloc(tag, args) in ECase(r, [{tag; vs; body}], default)` and reduces to `let v0=args[0] in … in body`, dropping the dead EAlloc (DCE removes it). Run pre-Perceus (before RC insertion); safe post-Perceus only in the matching branch when the scrutinee is not referenced in the branch body. `tags_match` handles qualified (`Result.Ok`) vs bare (`Ok`) tag naming via `short_name`. 3 new tests in `beta_adt` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P11.
- ✅ **Variable copy propagation (P12)** — Extended `lib/tir/cprop.ml` with `avar_env : (string * Tir.var) list` to propagate `let x = y` variable aliases in addition to `let x = literal`. Excludes closure types (`TFn`/`TVar`) to protect ECallPtr dispatch (llvm_emit is name-sensitive for closures). Uses `~allow_avar:false` for ECallPtr's function argument. 3 new tests in `cprop` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P12.
- ✅ **FBIP cross-tag constructor reuse (P8)** — Extended Perceus FBIP to reuse heap cells across constructors with the same field count (not just the same constructor name). `add_scrutinee_free_for` encodes the consumed ctor's arity as dummy `TUnit` type-args in the DecRC var's type (`TCon(qualified_tag, [TUnit; …])`). `same_arity` replaces `shape_matches` in `try_fbip_sink` and `fbip_expr`, comparing `List.length dummy_args = List.length new_alloc_args`. Cross-ctor reuse is safe: March allocates `[tag + nfields × ptr]` blocks so same-arity ⟺ same block size; the new tag is written into the reused cell by llvm_emit. 5 new tests in `fbip_p8` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P8.
- ✅ **Let-floating past ECase / join points (P1) — Layer 1 (alpha-merge, pre-Perceus)** — `lib/tir/join_points.ml`: hoists `let` bindings appearing identically at the start of EVERY case branch above the `ECase`. Two entry points: `run` (conservative, identical binder names — runs first in `lib/tir/opt.ml`'s post-Perceus fixed-point loop) and `run_pre` (Layer 1 — allows arms to bind the shared RHS under *different* fresh ANF names, floating one `$jp…` binder and substituting each arm's binder onto it via `rename_expr`). `run_pre` is wired in `bin/main.ml` BEFORE `Perceus.perceus` (renaming RC-decorated bindings post-Perceus is unsafe — double-free class) so RC is inserted once for the hoisted binding. Because TIR is in ANF, this captures the spec's motivating shared-inline-subexpr example. 6 tests in `join_points` group (`pre_float_alpha`, `pre_no_float_diff_rhs` added). See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P1 Layer 2 — non-leading / interleaved common lets** — Today only the *first* let of each arm is peeled. Generalize to a common pure binding anywhere in an arm's leading let-chain whose RHS does not depend on arm-specific earlier bindings, with reordering. Medium. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P1 Layer 3 — general non-let-bound cross-arm CSE** — Hoist shared sub-expressions ANF did not already lift to a head let; gate on `Purity.is_pure`. Mostly subsumed by ANF, low marginal value. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P8 Layer 2 — delayed/non-immediate FBIP reuse** — Current cross-tag reuse only fires when destruction and reconstruction are adjacent; extend to reuse across intervening code. Medium-High. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P8.
- ✅ **`HashMap` stdlib module** — HAMT-backed persistent map using structural `==` and polymorphic `hash` — no comparator at any call site. Full API: `new`, `put`, `get`, `get_or`, `has`, `delete`, `size`, `fold`, `keys`, `values`, `entries`, `from_list`, `to_list`, `update`, `map_values`, `filter`, `merge`, `merge_with`. `stdlib/hash_map.march` (~430 lines); 40 tests in `test/stdlib/test_hash_map.march`. `Enum.uniq` and `Enum.uniq_by` rewritten O(n) via HashMap; `Enum.frequencies` rewritten O(n) with first-appearance order preserved; `test_helpers.ml` wired for enum tests. `docs/stdlib.md` and `docs/cookbook/basics.md` updated with HashMap reference.
- [ ] **Fold numeric builtins on literal args** — Extend `lib/tir/fold.ml` to constant-fold pure numeric builtins when all arguments are literals (mirrors the existing `string_byte_length("..")` / int-arith folds). LLVM *cannot* do this — these lower to opaque `call @march_*` instructions, not arithmetic, so the fold must happen at TIR level where the builtin's semantics are known. **Safe set (exact results, zero cross-libm bit-drift):** `int_pow`, `int_abs`, `float_abs`, `int_to_float`, `float_to_int` (truncation), `floor`, `ceil`, `round`. **`int_pow` must reuse the 63-bit range guard** from the `string_to_int` overflow fix — `int_pow(2, 100)` stays a runtime call rather than baking in a corrupt constant; likewise skip negative exponents and `/0`/`%0`. **Defer `sqrt`/`pow`** on non-trivial inputs: compile-time OCaml `libm` and runtime C `libm` aren't guaranteed to agree on the last ULP, so folding could embed an off-by-one-bit value. Each fold must bit-match the interpreter oracle. Lift: ~1 session (~50 lines + ~10 tests), low risk, additive — no new pass or pipeline wiring. Payoff: modest (only fires on builtins over literal constants, e.g. `let max_val = int_pow(2, 31) - 1`) but correctness-clean and removes work LLVM provably can't.
- ✅ **Boolean short-circuit absorber peepholes (P15)** — Extended `lib/tir/simplify.ml` with 3 patterns: `x && false = false` / `false && x = false` (absorber law for AND), `x || true = true` / `true || x = true` (absorber law for OR), `not true = false` / `not false = true` (literal folding). These fire after cprop propagates boolean constants into guards and conditionals. 5 new tests in `simplify` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P14.
- ✅ **Fold boolean comparison identities + integer comparison folding** — Extended `lib/tir/fold.ml` with: `x == true → x`, `true == x → x`, `x == false → not x`, `false == x → not x` (arise after inlining boolean predicates); and `a OP b → bool` for integer comparisons (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `==`, `!=`) on two literal arguments. 4 new tests in `fold` group.
- ✅ **EField of known record/update (P13)** — `lib/tir/cprop.ml`: separate `field_env` tracks `ERecord`/`EUpdate`/alias bindings; `EField(AVar r, k)` folds to the atom at position `k` when `r` is in fenv. `EUpdate` merges new fields over the base record's known fields. 2 new tests in `cprop` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P13.
- ✅ **Extended simplify peepholes (P14)** — `lib/tir/simplify.ml`: `if x then true else false → x`, `if x then false else true → not x`, `x == x → true` (non-float), `x != x → false` (non-float). 5 new tests in `simplify` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P14.
- ✅ **Boolean absorber peepholes + integer-literal comparison folding (P15)** — `lib/tir/simplify.ml`: `x && false → false`, `false && x → false`, `x || true → true`, `true || x → true` (Boolean absorbers; 4 new tests). `lib/tir/fold.ml` (new file): boolean-comparison identities (`x == true → x`, `x == false → not x` and symmetric) and integer-literal comparison folding (`LitInt(a) OP LitInt(b) → LitBool(result)` for `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `==`, `!=`; fires from `cprop` literal-env hits). 5 new tests in `fold` group; folding integrated into `lib/tir/opt.ml` fixed-point loop.
- ✅ **P10 Phase 2 — NativeArray compiled path** — `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `native_int_arr_*` and `native_float_arr_*` builtins registered in `is_builtin_fn` (prevents erroneous RC wrapping), `builtin_ret_ty` (correct i64/double/ptr return types), and the LLVM preamble (16 explicit `declare` lines). `runtime/march_runtime.c`: `native_int_arr_from_list` now untags tagged-integer pointers (`(raw & 1) ? raw >> 1 : raw`) before writing to the flat array; `native_int_arr_to_list` tags before storing (`(v << 1) | 1`) so March's List(Int) untagging recovers the correct value. 2 new IR-level tests in `native_arrays` group confirming all builtins appear with correct LLVM types and call instruction return types. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P10.
- ✅ **P6 Milestone 2 — Option-shaped niche encoding** — Option-like types (one nullary ctor + one single-field ctor) use `None=0, Some(x)=x` — no heap allocation for either variant. `lib/tir/repr.ml`: `Repr` extended with `Niche { payload; tagged }` variant; `niche_payload_ok` admits `TInt`, `TBool`, `TString`, `TPtr`, and heap-Boxed types (all guaranteed nonzero in a ptr slot); excludes `TFloat` (0.0 bitcasts to null), `TUnit` (raw 0), `TVar` (unknown), and nested Niche types (Some(None)=0=None). `payload_needs_tag` covers `TBool` alongside `TInt`. `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `EAlloc` niche fast path emits tagged i64→ptr for scalar payloads, bare ptr for pointer payloads, or null (i1 false→ptr) for the nullary ctor. `EReuse` skips reuse for niche types (no heap cell to reuse). `emit_case` Niche arm: null-check dispatch, then `ptrtoint+ashr 1` for tagged scalars or bare `ptrtoint` for pointers. `llvm_param_ty` suppresses `nonnull dereferenceable(16)` on niche-typed parameters. `record_get` special case passes a payload `kind` char (`'i'` Int/Bool, `'p'` heap ptr, `'f'` Float, `'g'` generic) as 3rd arg to `march_record_get`. Runtime: `runtime/march_extras.c` — `make_some(val)=val`, `make_none()=(void*)0`, kind-aware `rec_some_k`/`rec_none_k` (scalar/ptr → niche null, Float/generic → boxed heap cell); `march_record_get` accepts `int64_t expected_kind` and calls these. New native integration test `test/native/option_niche.march`. 6 new repr unit tests (`niche_int`, `niche_string`, `niche_bool`, `niche_float_boxed`, `niche_unit_boxed`, `nested_niche_boxed`). Adversarial regression test: vault scalar (Bool/Int) round-trips. All tests pass: compiler 281 + eval 224 + codegen 317 (315 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat simplify failures) + stdlib 782.

- ✅ **P6 Milestone 1 — Unboxed single-field constructors (newtypes)** — Single-variant single-field ADTs (`type Counter = Counter(Int)`) are now stored as their raw payload instead of a heap cell. `lib/tir/repr.ml`: new `Repr` module with `repr_of_ty` (classifies monomorphic TCon as `Newtype payload | Boxed`) and `payload_needs_tag` (returns true for `TInt`/`TBool` and newtypes-over-scalars). Scalar payloads are tagged `(v<<1)|1` so `IS_HEAP_PTR` never mistakes raw integers for heap pointers; Float-payload newtypes stay `Boxed` (tagging float bits is unsafe). `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: three fast paths — `EAlloc` for construction (tag & inttoptr for scalar, coerce-to-ptr for pointer payloads); `EReuse` for FBIP-generated reconstruct (skip RC reuse, emit new payload directly with manual DecRC of old box); `emit_case` for matching (ptrtoint + ashr to recover scalar, bare ptr coerce for pointer payloads; strip Perceus-inserted `EDecRC` inside the branch since ownership transfer is net-zero). Global `cur_type_defs` ref keeps `repr_of_ty` available without threading params through all emit functions. New native integration test `test/native/newtype_counter.march` (Counter(Int): `bump`/`unwrap` across function boundaries; expected output 42). All tests pass: compiler 281 + eval 224 + codegen 309 (307 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat simplify failures) + stdlib 782 + native newtype_counter green.
