Interpreter semantics unchanged. **Regression:** 4 compiled parity tests in `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `newtype_derived_method_crash` suite (String-payload `==`/`!=` — RED pre-fix; Int-payload control — green before and after; Boxed-ADT-payload `==` exercising the recursive arm — RED pre-fix; a generic `type Wrap(a) = Wrap(a)` at String+Int exercising the `type_params` substitution branch — the only test that drives a non-identity subst). Differential-oracle `gen_derived_method_newtype_string_module` (`test/test_properties.ml`), previously scoped to named `eq()`/`compare()` only to stay in the working subset, was widened to also exercise the `==` operator (oracle index 12 green, 50/50 matched interp = compiled). **Compiler builds clean; `newtype_derived_method_crash` 10/10 green; newtype-string oracle 50/50.**
- ✅ **Oracle Task 2 (Phase 1b) — top-level internal-compiler-error handler; "internal-failwith-is-always-a-bug" invariant confirmed (`bin/main.ml`, `test/test_properties.ml`, 2026-07-04)** — survey of every `failwith`/`assert false` site in `lib/tir/*.ml` (~33 sites across 13 files) found **zero** genuine "typechecked but deliberately not lowerable yet" markers: every site is an internal-invariant check (the typechecker/desugarer/monomorphizer should have prevented reaching that state — e.g. `llvm_calls.ml`'s unresolved-interface-dispatch guard, `llvm_case.ml`'s type-incorrect-TIR-reached-codegen guard, `mono.ml`'s polymorphic-recursion spec-limit) or closed-set exhaustiveness on a fixed operator-name table (`llvm_emit.ml`'s int/float/cmp/bitwise op lookups). The one plausible "not-yet-lowerable" candidate, `lower_match.ml`'s `PatRecord`-destructuring `failwith`, is unreachable from any parsed program — the grammar has no production for record patterns, so the case only exists to guard against a hand-constructed AST. Per the plan's Outcome-B branch: no `Unsupported of string` exception was introduced (there is nothing distinct to signal); instead, `bin/main.ml`'s `compile` function now wraps the `compile_mode` branch (`Lower.lower_module` → `Mono`/`Perceus`/`Opt` → `Llvm_emit`/clang) in a `try...with` that re-raises the two legitimate diagnostic exceptions reachable in that scope (`March_errors.Errors.ParseError`, `March_tir.Js_emit.Js_emit_error` — both already handled locally before/inside this point) and, for anything else, prints `march: internal compiler error: <exn>` plus a backtrace (`Printexc.record_backtrace true` now enabled at `compile`'s entry) and a user-facing "this is a compiler bug" note, then exits with a new documented code `internal_compiler_error_exit_code = 3` (0/1/2 were already spoken for). Verified end-to-end: a normal typecheck error (unresolved identifier) still renders identically and exits 1 (untouched — diagnostics `exit` directly before reaching the `try`, and `exit` terminates the process without raising, so it can never reach the new handler); a temporarily-injected `failwith` in `Lower.lower_module` (reverted after verification, no lib/tir change shipped) was caught, rendered via the new path, and exited 3. `test/test_properties.ml`'s `classify_compile` (Task 1's heuristic) now checks `rc = internal_compiler_error_exit_code` first — a positive signal, no stderr-sniffing needed — before falling back to Task 1's signal/backtrace-marker heuristics (kept for other/older compiler paths). 2 new unit tests (`classify_compile (unit)` group now 7/7). All six runners green (compiler 392, eval 230, codegen 374, stdlib 766 quick, stdlib_march 53); `test_properties.exe test -e "classify_compile"` green (7/7). Full un-filtered `test_properties.exe -e` (~20min) not required to block the commit per the plan; see report for the spot-check status. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-07-04-differential-oracle-plan.md` Task 2; design: `specs/2026-07-04-differential-oracle-design.md` §4.1.
- ✅ **Compiler robustness: an unreadable sibling directory crashed `--compile`/`--check` with an uncaught `Sys_error` (`lib/resolver/resolver.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, 2026-07-04).** Found while running the differential oracle for real (Task 1 of the differential-oracle work; design at `specs/2026-07-04-differential-oracle-design.md`). The compiler auto-discovers sibling `.march` modules in the entry file's OWN source directory (`resolve_imports` plus the early-CAS sibling hash both walk it via `March_resolver.Resolver.collect_lib_files`). That recursive walk classified a subdirectory with `Sys.is_directory` (which stats fine on a permission-denied dir → `true`), recursed into it, and then called `Sys.readdir` on it **with no exception guard** — so a permission-denied sibling directory raised `Sys_error` uncaught to `bin/main.ml`'s top level, killing the compile (exit 2, no output binary) on an otherwise well-typed program. The trigger is real on stock macOS: `$TMPDIR` (`/var/folders/.../T/`) contains a Spotlight-managed `TemporaryItems` directory that is `Operation not permitted`, so any well-typed program placed directly in `$TMPDIR` failed to compile. Fixed by guarding the recursive `Sys.readdir` in `collect_lib_files` (`try … with Sys_error _ -> [||]` → an unreadable directory is treated as empty and skipped, never aborts the walk). Hardened the parallel unguarded walk `march_files_in` in `bin/main.ml` (the `--check <dir>` path — same latent bug, plus its `Sys.is_directory` is now exception-safe) and the `march test` auto-discovery `Sys.readdir "test"` (permission-denied `test/` now skipped like a missing one). The two other `Sys.readdir` sites (`cache_dir`, `runtime_dir`) were already guarded; the remaining `Sys.is_directory`-in-predicate sites are each short-circuited by a preceding `Sys.file_exists` and never readdir, so they can't hit this class. Regression: new `compiler_robustness` group in `test/test_codegen.ml` (`test_unreadable_sibling_dir_does_not_crash_check`) — a well-typed entry beside a 0000-perm sibling dir must `--check` at exit 0 (`--check` shares the exact crashing early-CAS/resolver walk with `--compile` but needs no clang, so it runs everywhere; perms restored via `Fun.protect`). RED→GREEN verified (unfixed binary: `Sys_error(... TemporaryItems ...)` EXIT:2 → fixed: EXIT:0). **Note:** this was independently worked around in the oracle harness itself (`test/test_properties.ml` isolates each generated program in its own dedicated temp dir), but the compiler must not crash on it — that is what this fixes. Six-runner gates: **392 compiler / 230 eval / 381 codegen (+1 new) / stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots**; the 14 `adversarial-regressions` (stdlib `Unknown module IO/BigInt`) and 1 `llvm_ir_validity_gate` (distributed-program TIR-typing) failures in this worktree were verified **pre-existing on the base commit** (identical failures with the fix reverted) — unrelated to this change and out of scope.

- ✅ **Interpreter: named `eq`/`compare`/`hash` misdispatched with 2+ impls of the same interface in scope (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, 2026-07-04)** — `DImpl` eval (two sites: the module-level `eval_decl` handler and the top-level-let `make_recursive_env` handler) bound each impl method's bare name directly into the env in addition to `impl_tbl`, so a second `derive Eq`/`derive Ord`/`derive Hash` for a different type shadowed the first — `eq(Wrap(1), Wrap(1))` returned `false` (ran the wildcard `_ -> false` arm of `WrapS`'s derived Eq) and `compare(Wrap(1), Wrap(2))` panicked with a non-exhaustive match (`WrapS`'s per-constructor match had no arm for `Wrap`). The infix operators (`==`, `<`) and `Show.show` were unaffected — `cmp_op` always dispatches via `type_name_of_value` + `impl_tbl`, and `Show.show` was already the one method excluded from the env-binding. Compiled mode was unaffected (`lib/tir/lower.ml`'s `resolve_iface_method` resolves per-type mangled impls by argument type). Fix: generalized the `Show.show` special-case into `is_type_dispatched_method` (`Show.show | Eq.eq | Ord.compare | Hash.hash`) at both DImpl sites — these now bind as a plain closure registered only in `impl_tbl`, never as a shadowing bare env binding. Regression tests: `test_eval_eq_multi_type_dispatch`, `test_eval_compare_multi_type_dispatch`, `test_eval_hash_multi_type_dispatch` in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`'s `interface_dispatch` group. Compiler 392 / eval 230 / codegen 380 / stdlib 802 (+3 on rebase onto the same-day lint-fix baseline below, plus unrelated upstream additions from the merge) — all exit 0.

- ✅ **`spawn(<computed expr>)` crashed `--compile` with an uncaught `Failure` on a well-typed program — shape restriction now enforced in the typechecker (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `test/test_compiler.ml`, 2026-07-04).** Found during Task 1 of `specs/plans/2026-07-04-differential-oracle-plan.md` (differential-oracle `classify_compile` work). A well-typed program that spawns a *computed* actor expression — e.g. `spawn(if true do Counter else Counter end)` — passed `--check` with exit 0 but, when compiled, escaped to OCaml's top-level handler with `Fatal error: exception Failure("TIR lower: ESpawn argument must be a plain actor name")` (exit 2, no binary produced). Distinct from the known "Monomorphization limit reached" P0 above: this is compiled-backend-only and specific to the `spawn` argument shape. **Root cause:** the parser (`lib/parser/parser.mly:1222`, `SPAWN LPAREN expr RPAREN`) and the typechecker (`ESpawn` arm) accepted `spawn(e)` for *any* expression with zero shape restriction, but `lib/tir/lower.ml`'s `ESpawn` lowering only handles a bare actor name (`ECon(_,[],_)` / `EVar` — it dispatches by *name* to a statically generated `<Actor>_spawn` function) and hit an unguarded `failwith` for anything else; `bin/main.ml`'s compile path runs the TIR pipeline with no enclosing `try/with`, so the `Failure` propagated straight to the top level. **Why "reject earlier" and not "support it":** there is *no runtime actor-descriptor value* in either backend — the interpreter (`lib/eval/eval.ml:7122-7127`) *also* requires a plain actor name (`eval_error "spawn: expected actor name (got complex expression)"`), so "spawn a computed actor" is a feature that exists in neither backend, not merely a lowering robustness gap. **Fix:** the `ESpawn` arm in `typecheck.ml` now checks the argument is a plain actor name (`ECon(_,[],_)` / `EVar`, mirroring exactly what `lower.ml` accepts) and otherwise emits a structured `Err.error` pointing at the argument span, so `--check` / `--compile` / interpret now all reject the program uniformly with the same clean diagnostic before it can reach the internal `failwith` (which is retained as a now-unreachable invariant backstop). Valid `spawn(Counter)` is unaffected (compiles + runs). **Regression:** 2 new `test/test_compiler.ml` typecheck cases — `spawn computed actor: rejected` (asserts `has_errors` + the diagnostic mentions "spawn" and "plain actor name") and `spawn plain actor name: ok` (no false positive on the valid form). Six runners green: **394 compiler (+2) / 230 eval / 380 codegen / 799 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots** — zero `.expected` diffs; the lone stdlib "failure" (`test_compiled_actor_program_exits_without_kill`) was a load-induced 60s-watchdog timeout during a 109s heavily-loaded run and passes 3/3 in isolation.

- ✅ **`is_lib_file` off-by-one dead-coded the `_test.march` exclusion in `safety/no-panic-in-lib` (`lib/lint/lint.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, `test/test_helpers.ml`, `docs/coding-standards.md`, 2026-07-03)** — the test-file guard compared the 11-char literal `"_test.march"` against only the last **10** characters (`String.sub base (len-10) 10`), so the equality was always false and every `*_test.march` file was classified as library code — making `safety/no-panic-in-lib` fire on test files that legitimately `panic`/assert (e.g. via `stdlib/test.march` helpers). Fixed with `Filename.check_suffix base "_test.march"` and, since `forge lint` scans both `lib/` and `test/` passing full paths (`forge/lib/cmd_lint.ml`), additionally exempting any file under a `test/`/`tests/` directory — a plain `test/foo.march` carries no `_test` suffix, so the enclosing directory is the more reliable signal (basename-suffix alone would still misfire on it). New `lint/safety/no-panic-in-lib` test group in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` (4 cases, via a new filename-taking `lint_check_named` helper since `lint_check` hardcodes `"test.march"`): panic in `foo_test.march` and under `test/` must NOT be flagged; the same source in `foo.march` and under `lib/` MUST be (RED 2/4 → GREEN 4/4, proving the positive cases still detect real lib panics). `docs/coding-standards.md` detection note corrected to match (also fixed its pre-existing "module with no `fn main()`" imprecision — the check is basename `main.march`). Six runners green: compiler 385 / eval 230 / codegen 374 / stdlib 797 (+4) / stdlib_march 53 / snapshots 29; doc-lint clean.

- ✅ **P0: compiled actor runtime — niche-message SIGSEGV, `run_until_idle()` no-op, exit hang, kill UAF, concrete-niche untag (`runtime/march_runtime.c`, `runtime/march_scheduler.{c,h}`, `lib/tir/{repr,llvm_case,llvm_emit}.ml`, 2026-07-03)** — a deterministic compiled-only SIGSEGV (spawn → send `Inc(10)` → `run_until_idle()` → `kill`) unpacked into five defects: (1) `actor_green_thread`'s hot-reload migrate check dereferenced niche-represented messages (tagged scalar 0x15) guarded only by `msg != NULL` — now gated on `meta->dispatch_name_id && IS_HEAP_PTR(msg)`; (2) `run_until_idle()` was a no-op in compiled code (main is a green thread; `g_in_scheduler` guard returned immediately) — new cooperative `march_sched_wait_idle()`; (3) the scheduler only exited at `g_live_procs == 0`, so any program ending main() with a live actor hung forever (`examples/actors.march`) — actor loops are now daemon procs, woken to exit at shutdown once no non-daemon procs remain (`march_sched_spawn_daemon`/`wake_idle_daemons`, registry walks under new `g_registry_mu`); (4) `meta->green_thread` dangled after the actor proc died (kill → wake on freed proc) — cleared on loop exit; (5) `emit_case`'s concrete-niche recovery for non-generic Option-shaped ADTs (every actor message type) hardcoded `tagged=false`, handing branch bodies the raw tagged word (handler computed 21+11=32 instead of 10+5=15) — new `Repr.niche_repr_of_concrete` classifies from the variant def's field type, and emit_case/EAlloc-nullary/EReuse-nullary all key encode/decode on it (also closes the latent concrete-Float niche encode/decode mismatch). Regression: 2 compiled parity tests in the adversarial-regressions group (RED 139/139 → GREEN), full six-runner suite + benchmarks clean. See `specs/progress.md` Current State (same date) for the full evidence trail.

- ✅ **Named derived-method calls (`eq`/`compare`/`hash`) crashed compiled on `Newtype`-repr variants — SIGSEGV / non-exhaustive panic (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, `lib/tir/llvm_case.ml`, 2026-07-04).** Fixed the P1 filed at Wave 4 Task 5 (verified matrix at `f8b25df7`). Two independent, STACKED defects, both required to reproduce the crash — fixing only one left the other's symptom intact:

  **Defect 1 (derive-specific): shared `dummy_span` collision in the typechecker's span-keyed `type_map`.** `expand_derive`'s `derive_impl` (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`) minted every AST node in a derived `Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash`/`Show` impl body with the SAME `dummy_span` (`mk_name`/`mk_fn_def` both hard-code it). The typechecker records inferred types keyed by span (`env.type_map`, `Hashtbl.replace` — last write wins), and `Lower_state.ty_of_span` reads them back during TIR lowering — so every node across every derived method for a type collided on ONE table entry. Lowering saw whatever type happened to be the last one recorded for that shared span (e.g. `Ord$Wrap.compare(a : (Wrap) -> Int, ...)` — a function type where an `Int` was expected), not the node's real type.

  **Defect 2 (general, not derive-specific): `lower_match` mints destructured sub-pattern variables with `unknown_ty`.** Independently of defect 1, `lower_match.ml`'s match-compilation matrix (`compile_matrix`, the `tir_branches` builder) creates fresh `Tir.var`s for each constructor's destructured fields with `v_ty = Lower_types.unknown_ty` (a `TVar`) — it has no way to know the field's concrete type at that point. `llvm_case.ml`'s `emit_case` already had a narrow recovery for exactly this shape when the scrutinee is Option-shaped (`branches_match_niche_shape`, added for the niche repr) but nothing analogous for `Newtype`-repr scrutinees: a nested match on a Newtype-repr value (e.g. derived `Eq`'s `match (a, b) do (Wrap(x), Wrap(y)) -> ... end`, or — confirmed via a **hand-written** `impl Eq(Wrap)` with a nested tuple-destructure match and real, non-dummy spans — ANY nested destructure on a Newtype value) reached `emit_case` with the scrutinee typed as `TVar`, which `Repr.repr_of_ty` conservatively classifies `Boxed`. The Boxed strategy loads a ctor tag at `scrut+8` — a value that has no heap header (Newtype-repr values ARE their raw payload, `lib/tir/repr.ml`): an `Int` payload is a tagged scalar (wild deref → SIGSEGV, lldb confirmed `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in `Ord$Wrap.compare` at exactly the ctor-tag offset); a `String` payload is a heap pointer whose header byte at that offset is garbage (no arm tag matches → non-exhaustive-match panic, not a crash — matches the verified matrix's `WrapS(String)` row).

  The hand-written-impl repro is what separated the two defects: it has real per-node spans (so defect 1 cannot apply) yet still crashed identically to the derived case — proving defect 2 was independently sufficient and had to be fixed regardless of defect 1.

  **Fix — defect 1:** new `respan_derived_decl` (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`) walks every AST node inside a derive-generated `DImpl`/`DFn` and assigns each a fresh, structurally-unique span (`fresh_synthetic_span`, a monotonic counter under the sentinel file `"<none>"` — preserves every existing synthetic-span filter, e.g. `lib/coverage/coverage.ml`'s file check and the LSP's `= dummy_span` checks, which compare the whole record and never match a numbered synthetic span to a real document). Applied once in `expand_derive`, right after `derive_impl` returns, before the results are appended to the module's decls. The decl's own top-level span (the derive site, a real user span used for error attribution) is left untouched.

  **Fix — defect 2:** a Newtype analogue of `emit_case`'s existing niche TVar-recovery (`llvm_case.ml`'s `newtype_recovery_payload`): when the scrutinee type is unresolved (`TVar`) and the match has exactly one branch whose ctor tag identifies the type unambiguously — every type definition owning a ctor with that name classifies as `Repr.Newtype` AND they all agree on payload tagging (`Repr.payload_needs_tag`) — commit to the `Newtype` decode strategy with that payload type, exactly mirroring `EAlloc`'s encode side. Any ambiguity (a Boxed type sharing the ctor name, or disagreeing tagging) falls back to the pre-fix `Boxed` behavior, so no other match shape is affected.

  Both fixes were independently verified via a controller-reproduced discriminator: the derived-impl repro crashed pre-fix-2 even with fix-1 alone (span uniquification alone typechecks correctly but `lower_match`'s TVar sub-pattern vars still hit the Boxed path); only fix 2 — combined with fix 1 so the derived body typechecks against its OWN correct type rather than a collided one — resolves both stacked defects together.

  Preserves interpreter semantics EXACTLY, including the separately-documented payload-IGNORING behavior of derived `Ord`/`Hash` on variants (unchanged — `syntax_reference.md` § "Derived `Ord`/`Hash` ignore constructor payloads", updated to remove the now-fixed compiled-crash half of that section). Six new compiled regression tests in `test/test_codegen.ml`'s new `newtype_derived_method_crash` suite (all interpreter/compiled parity via `assert_compiled_interp_parity`): the `==`-operator-vs-named-`eq` discriminator pair on the same value back-to-back (isolates the named-method-only nature of the bug — `==` always worked via the Repr-aware `ensure_adt_eq_fn`/`llvm_eq.ml`, unaffected by either fix); derived `compare`/`hash` named on an Int-payload newtype (was SIGSEGV); derived `Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash` named on a String-payload newtype (was non-exhaustive panic); a Boxed 2-field-ctor negative control (`Pair(Int, Int)`, never crashed, must stay unaffected); a multi-ctor negative control (`Circle(Int) | Square(Int)`, never crashed, must stay unaffected); and the hand-written-impl nested-destructure repro that isolated defect 2. **All six runners green: 385 compiler / 230 eval / 380 codegen (+6 new) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — zero `.expected` diffs** (no snapshot in the 14-file corpus exercises `derive`/`impl`, so zero churn was expected and confirmed). `bench/tree_transform.march` (104857600) and `bench/binary_trees.march` (all check values) compiled and ran correct — this fix's `emit_case` change is on a narrow, previously-dead TVar-scrutinee branch that ordinary concrete-typed matches (which both benchmarks exercise) never take, so no regression risk was expected and none was found.

  **Bonus finding, spawned as a separate background task (not fixed here — out of scope, interpreter-only, pre-existing):** while building the verification matrix, the INTERPRETER was found to misdispatch named `eq`/`compare`/`hash` calls when 2+ types in scope derive the same interface (picks the wrong type's impl instead of dispatching on the argument's runtime type) — reproducible independently of this fix, pre-existing at main-repo HEAD before this session. Filed to a spun-off session; not part of this fix's scope (compiled-mode Newtype crash) and does not affect any of the regression tests above (each uses only one type per interface per test program).

- ✅ **`march_project_root` off-by-one — resolved to `_build`, not the repo root (`test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_ir_verify.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — the helper was `Filename.dirname` ×3 of `Sys.executable_name`, which for `<root>/_build/default/test/run_*.exe` lands on `<root>/_build` — one dirname short. Its doc comment claimed the compiled-regression tests `cd` there "so the compiler resolves its CWD-relative runtime/ and stdlib/ directories", but that never happened: from CWD `<root>/_build` the compiler's CWD-relative `stdlib`/`runtime` candidates are dead, so resolution silently depended entirely on the exe-relative `_build/default/` copies (populated only by a FULL `dune build` — after a targeted `dune build test/run_codegen.exe bin/main.exe` every compiled-regression test failed with confusing stdlib-missing errors), `--compile` runs dropped `.march/cas` trees inside `_build/`, and the `march_project_root`-based fallbacks in `test_snapshots.ml`/`test_ir_verify.ml` were dead. Rewritten to walk the exe's ancestors to the parent of its `_build` directory, verified by `dune-project` at that root, loud `Alcotest.failf` otherwise (never silently wrong; correct at any depth, incl. dune `.sandbox` trees). Verified by the CAS relocation (`.march/cas` now created at the repo root, none in `_build/`); codegen 346, stdlib 791 (Slow incl.), snapshots — all exit 0.

- ✅ **Z3 solver process leak — one immortal `z3 -in` orphaned per compiler/test process (`lib/refine/solver.ml`, `lib/refine/refine.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — two compounding defects: (1) `Refine.shared_solver`, the lazily-created per-process singleton behind every `Refine.discharge`, was never closed on any production path (`Solver.close` had zero callers outside two test files), so every `march --check`/`--compile` run and every test binary left its z3 child running at exit; (2) `Solver.create` built its pipes without `~cloexec:true`, so the z3 child inherited the write end of its own stdin pipe — after the parent died the pipe never delivered EOF and the orphan blocked in `read(0)` forever (1,174 immortal orphans at 0% CPU, ages 2h–24h, found reparented to PID 1 on 2026-07-02). Fixed: cloexec on all four pipe ends; `Solver.close` SIGKILLs before `waitpid` (reaping can never block on a wedged solver); new `Refine.shutdown` closes + clears the singleton, registered `at_exit` on first spawn and called explicitly at the end of both refinement-pass scopes in `bin/main.ml`; `discharge` supervises the solver (dead/wedged → reap, respawn once, then mark unavailable for the run); SIGPIPE ignored (only if at default) so solver death is a catchable error, not parent death. Regression tests in `test/test_refine.ml` ("lifecycle" suite, pgrep-based, loud-skip without z3): spawn-count check (3 discharges → 1 child during, 0 after `shutdown`) and an orphan probe (re-exec via `MARCH_Z3_ORPHAN_PROBE=1`, exit without cleanup, assert z3 dies ≤5s; verified RED against pre-fix pipes). End-to-end verified with a `MARCH_Z3` pid-logging shim: one z3 per `--check` run, reaped before exit. Full suite green (compiler 384, eval 230, codegen 337, stdlib 792 incl. Slow, stdlib_march 53, refinecheck 66, refine 16).

- ✅ **P0: RC underflow (double-free) when the SAME scrutinee is re-matched inside a sibling arm's sub-path (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — found during Wave 2 Task 4's TIR snapshot audit (`test/snapshots/perceus/scrutinee_borrowed_conservatism.expected`), fixed here. Minimal repro (compiled, `xs = Nil`):
  ```march
  mod MinimalScrutDbl do
    fn f(xs : List(Int), flag : Bool) : Int do
      match xs do
        Cons(h, t) ->
          if flag do
            h
          else
            match xs do
              Cons(h2, _) -> h2
              Nil -> 0
            end
          end
        Nil -> -1
      end
    end
    fn main() do println(f(Nil, true)) end
  end
  ```
  aborted with `march: RC underflow (rc was 0) at 0x... — aborting` (exit 134) when compiled; the interpreter printed `-1` correctly (compiled-only). Root cause (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`'s `ECase` handling, `insert_rc_expr`): the `Cons` arm re-matches the SAME scrutinee `xs` inside its `else` sub-path, so the documented "scrutinee-borrowed conservatism" (~line 1058-1080; a deliberately path-INSENSITIVE approximation the comment calls "memory-safe, minor leak") marks `xs` borrowed for the `Cons` arm and re-adds it to that arm's `live_before_br`. `add_cross_decrcs` (~line 1146-1182) then unions `live_before_br` across ALL sibling arms and emits a `dec_rc` at the head of any arm where a variable is "dead here, live elsewhere" — so the `Nil` arm (and the synthesized non-exhaustive-match `_` default) each got a cross-branch `dec_rc xs` **in addition to** the normal `add_scrutinee_free_for` scrutinee-free every arm already receives when the scrutinee is dead-after-case (~line 984-1017), producing two `dec_rc xs` on the same reference. **Fix:** `add_cross_decrcs`'s `dead_here` computation now excludes the scrutinee's own variable name (derived from the `ECase`'s atom) from the cross-branch liveness set — the scrutinee's fate is exclusively owned by `add_scrutinee_free_for`, which already correctly decides per-arm whether to free it, so it must never be treated as ordinary cross-branch "dead here, live elsewhere" liveness (that liveness was itself only an artifact of the scrutinee-borrowed re-add, not a real use by the sibling arm). The Case-2 "leak, not crash" conservatism (single sub-path scrutinee use, no re-match) is untouched; niche/newtype scrutinee-free skip and the `$fbip$` arity-marker encoding are unmodified. `MARCH_DEBUG_PERCEUS=1` confirms exactly 60 fewer `dec_rc` insertions module-wide (14766→14706), `inc`/`cancelled`/`reuse` unchanged; the repro now exits 0 printing `-1`, matching the interpreter. Snapshot regenerated (`UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 ./run_snapshots.exe`): `test/snapshots/perceus/scrutinee_borrowed_conservatism.expected` loses exactly the two redundant `dec_rc xs;` lines — audited byte-for-byte; no other snapshot in the 29-test corpus churned. New regression test `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `scrutinee_borrowed_cross_branch_dec_codegen` suite (compiled + interpreter parity, exit 0). **All six runners green: 384 compiler / 230 eval / 347 codegen (+1) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots.** RC benchmarks unaffected (tree_transform/list_ops/binary_trees/mutual_recursion all correct output, timings within noise). Bonus re-test of the known pre-existing `bench/heapsort.march` crash: still crashes both before and after this fix (same call site, `Sort.heap_merge_h`, confirmed via `lldb`), but the exit code/signal changed (134 RC-underflow → 139 SIGSEGV) because this fix shifted the binary's memory layout — the underlying bug is a separate, deeper, pre-existing RC issue in `heap_merge_h` unrelated to this fix (a small hand-reduced repro of the same merge shape does not crash; only the full 10,000-element heapsort volume triggers it), not introduced by this change.
- ✅ **W2.0 — kill vacuous greens: in-repo failures fail loudly, only genuine tool-absence may skip (`test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — Wave 2 Task 2. Three vacuous-green classes fixed (harness-only; `lib/` untouched). **(1)** `setup_jit_runtime` returned `None` on ANY `.so` link failure with stderr discarded, indistinguishable from "clang not installed" — RED-demonstrated via file-copy sabotage (temporarily dropping `march_dispatch.c` from the runtime source list, confirmed to genuinely break the link) showing the codegen/stdlib suites reported full "Test Successful" (341/791 tests) anyway. Fixed: `clang_available ()` PATH probe distinguishes legitimate skip (counted via `record_jit_skip` + an `at_exit` summary printer) from a real link failure (`Alcotest.failf` with the captured linker stderr, no longer redirected to `/dev/null`). **(2)** ~23 near-identical `if not (Sys.file_exists main_exe) then () else ...` / `if compile_rc <> 0 then ()` silent-skip guards across both files consolidated into shared helpers (`find_main_exe`, `march_project_root`, `compile_march`/`compile_march_or_skip`/`compile_march_raw`) with the same clang-absence-vs-real-failure policy. **(3)** Making (2) loud immediately caught two previously-hidden issues: a test using an unparseable `Int.min` literal (fixed: compute arithmetically instead of via a literal whose magnitude exceeds March's max positive literal) and a genuine `lib/tir/mono.ml` bug ("Monomorphization limit reached" compiling a trivial self-recursive nested closure) — out of scope to fix here, converted to a documented, message-matched `Alcotest.skip` (P0 entry added above). **(4)** New canary `test_setup_jit_runtime_gate_is_live` fails if the JIT gate is ever silently broken again. Step-1 sabotage re-run post-fix now correctly FAILS the suite (was vacuously green); restored clean. **384 compiler / 227 eval / 342 codegen (+1) / 791 stdlib (790 pass + 1 documented skip) / 53 stdlib_march all exit 0.**

- ✅ **record_put/record_update_dyn int-vs-pointer misclassification (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `runtime/march_extras.c`, 2026-07-02)** — compiled `record_put(r, k, v)` SIGSEGV'd for any even Int `v >= 4096`: the call site passed scalars in NATURAL (untagged) repr and `rec_field_norm_in`'s `REC_PLAUSIBLE_HEAP` sniff misclassified them as heap pointers, so `march_incrc` dereferenced the integer's value (reported as a "TCO loop crashes above 10k iterations" — really the value `i*2` crossing 4096). Call sites now tag scalars (uniform repr, `coerce i64→ptr`); the runtime untags unambiguously via `rec_field_norm_uniform` (even plausible-heap bits still downgrade to `'g'` for lying-static-type flows). Merge reconciliation: Wave 1's `march_record_update_dyn` (erased-`{ with }`) superseded this branch's chained-put lowering but shared the natural-repr crash — its emit site now passes uniform too; the runtime needed no extra change (it normalizes through the shared `rec_field_norm_in`). Compiled Slow regression test in adversarial-regressions ("record_put even Int >= 4096…": single 4096 put + 20k put loop + 20k `{ with }` loop vs closed-form sum).

- ✅ **JIT REPL fragments dropped `ctx.extra_fns` + JIT test infra resurrection (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`, `test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — `emit_repl_expr`/`emit_repl_decl` never appended `ctx.extra_fns` to their output (the B11 sibling bug), so a top-level function referenced as a first-class value from a plain REPL expression or `let` RHS stored the address of an undefined `@<fn>$clo_wrap` trampoline → clang rejected the fragment. Fixed to match the three sibling emitters; new clang-gated regression test "top-level fn as first-class value" (`repl_jit_cross_line`), RED-verified. Along the way: (1) `setup_jit_runtime`'s runtime source list was missing `march_dispatch.c`/`march_monitor_registry.c` (needed since dist-OTP `85b9b471`), so the runtime `.so` link silently failed and **every** clang-gated JIT test had been skipping vacuously — added both files; (2) the shared `$TMPDIR/march_jit` dir raced across concurrently-running test executables once the JIT tests were live again — `Repl_jit.create` now uses a per-pid dir with a stale-pid sweep, and `cleanup` never raises (a vanished dir was masking real test exceptions). Full five-runner suite green run concurrently (compiler 381, eval 224, codegen 325, stdlib 791 incl. Slow, stdlib_march 53).

- ✅ **Archive task MARCH_LIB_PATH parity with `forge build` (`forge/lib/archive_store.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — `lib_paths_for_root` (used by `find_task` to assemble MARCH_LIB_PATH for `forge <archive>.<task>`) omitted the archive root's `config/` and `.forge/generated/` directories, unlike `cmd_build.lib_path_env` — so an archive task whose modules import `Config` (e.g. forgepm's seed task with forgepm registered as a path archive) failed typecheck with "Unknown module `Config`". Now mirrors cmd_build: both dirs are appended when they exist. Also made `dep_lib_paths_for_archive` **transitive**: it previously read only the archive's own `[deps]`, so a dep's modules that import modules from *its* deps failed to resolve; it now recurses into each path/git dep's `forge.toml` (cycle-guarded via a `Unix.realpath`-canonicalised visited set seeded with the archive root). 4 new tests in `forge/test/test_cas_package.ml` ("archive-lib-paths" section: config/generated inclusion, missing-dir omission, transitive dep paths, dep-cycle termination). Verified end-to-end: `forge forgepm.seed` under a typecheck-only shim resolves `Config` and all transitive dep modules (remaining errors are forgepm's own calls to nonexistent `Path.join_all`/`assert_no_traversal`). All 9 forge suites + quick compiler/eval/codegen/stdlib suites green.

- ✅ **Dotted-sibling module dependency ordering (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` `module_refs_in_decls`, 2026-07-02)** — `dependency_order_dmod_run`'s edge collector recorded only the first dotted segment of a qualified reference, so a dotted sibling module (`mod Depot.Schema`) never sorted before its callers; with own-lib-first MARCH_LIB_PATH order (the `forge <task>` / archive run_task path) all callers of an unannotated fn unified against one shared pass-1 Mono placeholder and the first call site pinned its type (bogus "expected {first caller's record} but got {own record}" errors in forgepm via `forge forgepm.seed`). `add` now records every dotted prefix; `StringSet.inter name_set` already filters non-module prefixes. Regression test "dotted sibling module order" in `test/test_compiler.ml`. Full suite green (compiler 373, eval 224, codegen 323, stdlib 788).

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — P4 (load gossip + work dispatch) + P5 (anti-entropy, cross-node monitors, dist supervisors) (`stdlib/swim_driver.march`, `stdlib/cluster_load.march`, `stdlib/work_dispatch.march`, `stdlib/global_registry.march`, `stdlib/net_kernel.march`, `stdlib/dist_link.march`, `stdlib/dist_supervisor.march`, `runtime/march_monitor_registry.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`, 2026-06-25)** — two independent deliverables landed together. **P4 (L6 load-aware dispatch):** `ClusterLoad.NodeLoad` gains a `sampled_at : Int` timestamp; `to_ints`/`from_ints` wire-codec updated to 5 elements; `local/2` takes a caller-supplied `now_ms` for testability. `SwimDriver` extended with `peer_loads : Map(String, NodeLoad)`, `GossipWithLoad(Member, NodeLoad)` event, `SwimGossipLoad` wire msg (tag=3, 5-element array with piggybacked load), `load_stale_ms()` / `anti_entropy_period_ms()` constants, `anti_entropy_peers`/`bump_anti_entropy`/`peer_load`/`fresh_peer_loads` public API (anti-entropy timer without changing `step`'s return type). `WorkDispatch` module: `pick_dispatch_target` (power-of-two-choices over fresh `NodeLoad` samples), `pick_two` (caller-sampled indices), `dispatch` (load-aware RPC with local fallback + `NoConnection`/`DeadlineExceeded` retry), `dispatch_affine` (ConsistentHash affinity with load-aware fallback). **P5a (Merkle anti-entropy):** `GlobalRegistry` gains `root_hash` (Merkle tree over sorted name→entry pairs), `diff_entries` (CRDT join when hashes differ, no-op when equal), `all_entries` (full snapshot for wire transfer), plus the private `entry_to_bytes` serialiser. **P5c (cross-node monitors):** `net_kernel.march` adds MONITOR_REQ (tag=7) and MONITOR_FIRE (tag=8) frame codecs + `frame_tag` peek helper. New `runtime/march_monitor_registry.{h,c}`: mutex-guarded per-pid watcher table, `march_dist_monitor_register`/`fire_pid`/`fire_nodedown`/`clear_fd`, mini msgpack encoder for MONITOR_FIRE frames. `march_runtime.c` hooks `march_dist_monitor_fire_pid` on actor exit. `stdlib/dist_link.march`: `DownReason = Normal | Killed | Crash(String) | NodeDown`, `MonitorRef = MonitorRef(Int)`, `PendingMonitor`, `MonitorTable`, wire codecs (encode/decode req + fire), table operations, `make_monitor` factory, `impl Eq(MonitorRef)` + `impl Eq(DownReason)`. **P5d (dist supervisors):** `stdlib/dist_supervisor.march`: `RestartStrategy = Permanent | Transient | Temporary`, `SupervisionStrategy = OneForOne | OneForAll | RestForOne`, `should_restart`, `restart_indices`, `find_child_by_ref`, `update_child`, `child_count`. Tests: `test_swim_driver.march` +9 tests (SwimGossipLoad round-trip, msg_to_event, peer_loads-via-step, stale detection, anti_entropy_peers, bump_anti_entropy); `test_global_registry.march` +8 tests (root_hash properties, diff_entries merge); `test_work_dispatch.march` (11 tests: pick_dispatch_target, pick_two, ClusterLoad scorers); `test_dist_link.march` (17 tests: reason codecs, MONITOR_REQ wire, MONITOR_FIRE wire, MonitorTable ops); `test_dist_supervisor.march` (11 tests: should_restart × 3 strategies, restart_indices × 3 strategies, find_child_by_ref, update_child, make). All 53 stdlib_march test suites green (was 49; +3 new suites = 52; swim_driver + global_registry existing suites extended). Full suite green — compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786, stdlib_march 53/53.

- ✅ **`node_rpc` interpreter gap closed — `remote_check`/`remote_invoke` eval builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, 2026-06-23)** — `node_rpc.march`'s `handle_request` C-registry fallback called `remote_check`/`remote_invoke`, which were codegen-only → `unbound variable: remote_check` under the interpreter (`test_stdlib_march` 46/47). Added them as eval builtins consistent with the interpreter's empty/no-op registry (`remote_register_stub`/`remote_count` are already no-ops): `remote_check` → 0 (NoTarget), `remote_invoke` → None — matching `march_remote_check_march` on an empty registry and the "unknown target yields NoTarget" test. `test_stdlib_march` now 47/47; full suite green (compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786).

- ✅ **Capacity-aware routing demo — end-to-end cross-host (`demo/load_server.march`, `demo/load_client.march`, `scripts/rpc/route-smoke.sh`, 2026-06-23)** — closes the original goal: a client queries each node's live CPU/memory headroom over RPC (`report_load`), picks the node with most spare CPU via `ClusterLoad.pick_by`, and routes the expensive call there. Verified cross-host: `load_server` cross-compiled for linux/amd64 on a 1-core droplet; the Mac client (14 cores, heavily loaded) queried both over an ssh tunnel and routed `square(7)=49` to the droplet (more spare CPU). Fixed a demo naming collision (`connect` helper vs `Socket.connect` → renamed `dial`). Local smoke test added. Full suite green. Depends on the whole arc: resource builtins + ClusterLoad + niche codegen fix + stable cross-binary RPC impl_hash.

- ✅ **RPC CAS impl_hash stable across separate compilations (`lib/cas/serialize.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, `test/native/rpc_hash_*`, 2026-06-23)** — the two-node RPC admission check (`remote_ref_hashes` + `march_remote_register`) previously only worked for trivial leaf functions: a non-leaf body (calling stdlib) got a different impl_hash in the caller vs server binary, so admission returned NoTarget and the call hung. Two root causes fixed: (1) the impl serializer hashed generated temp-variable names verbatim → now alpha-normalizes bound variables to binding indices (free callee names kept), so the hash is invariant to temp-name counters that vary with surrounding code; (2) RPC hashes were taken from post-opt SCC hashes for live functions but pre-opt `hash_fn_def` for eliminated ones → now built uniformly from pre-opt `hash_fn_def` for all functions (a dedicated `rpc_impl_hashes`/`rpc_sig_hashes` passed to `emit_module`, separate from the HCR map). Result: identical impl_hash across separate client/server compilations of the same non-trivial source; a real `report_load` RPC over TCP returns metrics instead of hanging. NoTarget already returns an error (verified). Regression: two separately-compiled modules with an identical stdlib-calling `probe` (one live via `__rpc_stub`, one eliminated with padding) must print equal impl_hashes. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786; rpc_auto_enroll + node_call_loopback pass). Unblocks the capacity-aware routing demo.

- ✅ **Codegen hardening — niche match on an unresolved (`'_`) scrutinee type (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `test/native/lazy_niche`, 2026-06-23)** — fixes a silent miscompile/segfault: a lazily-loaded stdlib module (not in `stdlib_file_list`) has its body lowered without type inference, so a `match List.nth_opt(...)`-style niche-`Option` match reached codegen with a `TVar "_"` scrutinee → `repr_of_ty` returned `Boxed` → heap-tag match strategy dereferenced a tagged int → crash. `emit_case` now recovers the niche shape from the branch constructors and, for an unresolved scrutinee, uses the niche strategy with `tagged=false` (payload taken verbatim; the erased-i64 convention conditionally untags it at concrete use sites). Regression fixture `stdlib/lazy_niche_probe.march` (intentionally excluded from `stdlib_file_list`) + native compile-and-diff test. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786). Complements the earlier `cluster_load.march` `stdlib_file_list` registration — that fixed the one module; this hardens codegen for *any* lazily-loaded niche match.

- ✅ **Per-node CPU/memory introspection + `ClusterLoad` capacity-routing API (`stdlib/cluster_load.march`, `stdlib/system.march`, 2026-06-23)** — lets a language user route an expensive call to whichever cluster node has CPU/memory headroom. New cross-platform `sys_*` builtins (`sys_cpu_load_milli`, `sys_mem_total_bytes`, `sys_mem_available_bytes`) sample real local resources (runtime/march_extras.c via getloadavg/sysctl/host_statistics64 on macOS, getloadavg/sysinfo//proc/meminfo on Linux; wired through llvm_emit + defun + eval), exposed as `System.cpu_load_milli/0` etc. `ClusterLoad` provides `NodeLoad`, `local/1` sampling, `spare_cpu_milli`/`spare_mem_mb` scorers, `pick_by/2` (argmax with caller-supplied selector — raw metrics exposed), and `to_ints`/`from_ints` wire marshalling. **Also fixed a silent-miscompile compiler bug:** a new stdlib module not registered in `stdlib_file_list` is loaded lazily (signatures only, no body typecheck) → expression types stay `'_` → niche-`Option` matches lower to the heap-tag strategy → segfault; fixed by registering `cluster_load.march`. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786); deeper lazy-load hardening tracked separately.

- ✅ **Two-host RPC over TCP — name-resolution + remote-hash fixes, Linux portability, repeatable harness (`demo/two_node_server.march`, `demo/two_node_client.march`, `scripts/rpc/`, 2026-06-23)** — two independent executables (not one in-process demo) doing real RPC: the server registers `square` via the `__rpc_stub` mechanism and serves a TCP accept loop; the client connects, derives the CAS hash pair with `remote_ref_hashes`, and calls `square(7)`/`square(12)` over the wire → `49`/`144`. **Two compiler fixes made this work:** (1) `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` now resolves a bare call against `ctx.top_fns` **before** `unqualified_fns` at every call site, so a same-module top-level function (the server's recursive `serve_loop`) shadows a cross-module suffix match (`NodeCall.serve_loop`) — previously the server exited right after `tcp_listen`; (2) `bin/main.ml` adds a second hash pass over all remaining pre-opt functions so `remote_ref_hashes` constant-folds to the correct hash even when the caller-side `pfn` is optimized away (previously it folded to empty strings → admission `NoTarget` → call failed). **Linux portability:** `/dev/urandom` fallback (`march_runtime.c`), `_GNU_SOURCE` for `MAP_ANONYMOUS`/`RTLD_DEFAULT` (`march_scheduler.c`, `ffi_marshal.c`, `eval_ffi_stubs.c`), `dlopen` `./`-prefix for bare `.so`, and `-lLLVM-${VER}` (`detect_llvm.sh`) — all `#if defined(__linux__)`-guarded. **Harness:** `scripts/rpc/{smoke-local,build-linux,deploy-droplet}.sh` (local assert 49/144; Docker `linux/amd64` cross-compile; droplet-localhost + macOS-client-over-`ssh -L`-tunnel cross-host proofs). Full macOS suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786). Known-orthogonal pre-existing gap: `node_rpc` eval test fails `unbound variable: remote_check` (admission builtins are codegen-only, not yet interpreter builtins — tracked separately).

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 compiler-emitted `@main` enroll/stub integration test (`test/native/rpc_auto_enroll.march`, 2026-06-23)** — closes the L4 feature loop: the compiler auto-registers marshalling stubs at program startup without any user-written `remote_register_stub` call. Server defines `add(a, b) : Int` + `pfn add__rpc_stub` (MessagePack shim); compiler (a) detects the `__rpc_stub` suffix and pins stubs in `tm_exports` so `Dce.run` keeps them alive, (b) saves `pre_opt_tir` before `Opt.run` so trivially-inlined base functions (`add = a + b`) can still be hashed, (c) for each stub whose base is absent from `hr_impl_hashes` after CAS hashing, looks it up in `pre_opt_tir` via `March_cas.Hash.hash_fn_def`, and (d) emits `march_remote_register(impl_hash, sig_hash, &add__rpc_stub)` in `@main`. Two new builtins (`remote_check`, `remote_invoke`) lower to `march_remote_check_march` / `march_remote_invoke_march` in `runtime/march_remote_registry.c`; `NodeRpc.handle_request` falls through to them on `Targets` miss. Client calls `NodeCall.call` with `remote_ref_hashes("RpcAutoEnroll", "add")`, receives `result:15` (= 7 + 8). Port 29851. Test added to `test/dune`. All 1630 tests pass.

- ✅ **`stdlib/ring_buf.march` — mutable RingBuf with non-sendable enforcement (2026-06-23)** — `RingBuf(a)`: fixed-capacity circular buffer backed by the existing `'a ring` OCaml struct in `eval.ml`. All 10 operations O(1); allocation-free after `make()`. `push` overwrites oldest when full. `pop` removes oldest (FIFO). Index 0 = oldest. Shared-reference semantics within one actor. `VRingBuf of value ring` value constructor (ring type hoisted before `value` ADT); `ring_pop_oldest` helper added. `check_sendable`/`non_sendable_types` added to `typecheck.ml`; fires at every `send()` callsite and rejects `RingBuf` payloads with a diagnostic pointing to actor init state as the correct sharing mechanism. 28 tests in `test/stdlib/test_ring_buf.march`. All quick-suite tests pass.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — P2 two-node SWIM discovery integration test (`test/native/node_discovery.march`, 2026-06-22)** — end-to-end integration test of the authenticated peer-connection + SWIM discovery stack over real TCP loopback. `node-a` accepts, `node-b` connects; both run `ClusterConn.handshake` over the live socket and confirm each other in their `PeerRegistry`; `node-a` sends a `SwimPing` via `SwimDriver.encode_msg`, `node-b` replies with `SwimPingAck`, and both call `Swim.on_gossip` + `Membership.alive_members` to confirm the peer is `Alive` (`live_peers=1`). All I/O is real TCP; the green-thread scheduler interleaves acceptor and connector naturally. Also fixed `swim_driver.march` bug: `String.of_int` → `String.from_int` in `int_to_status` and `decode_msg` (the undefined symbol was silently preventing linker resolution of those functions from other modules). Test added to `test/dune` as a native compile-and-diff rule on port 29760.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 Node.call loopback (`test/native/node_call_loopback.march`, 2026-06-22)** — integration test for the full RPC call path: NodeCall transport shim (`stdlib/node_call.march`) layered over NodeRpc dispatcher and TCP sockets. Three scenarios over real TCP on port 29850: matching hashes → `ok:7`, sig_hash mismatch → `err:TypeMismatch`, unknown target → `err:NoTarget`. Both server and client in one process, interleaved by the green-thread scheduler. `NodeCall.serve_one` / `NodeCall.call` wired to `NodeRpc.handle_frame` / `RemoteCall.interpret`. Test added to `test/dune` as a native compile-and-diff rule.

- ✅ **FBIP niche self-reference fix (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, 2026-06-22)** — The FBIP (frame-based in-place) optimization in `llvm_emit.ml` wrongly generated a self-referential object for niche-encoded patterns like `Some(result) -> Ok(result)`. For niche `Option`, the payload IS the scrutinee (no wrapper allocation), so the FBIP reuse branch overwrote the payload's own structure (`store ptr result, ptr result+16`) creating a cycle. The fresh branch also wrongly called `march_decrc` on the reuse atom, decrementing the very value about to be stored as Ok's field. Fix: in the `EReuse (reuse_atom, TCon(ctor,_), args)` handler's fallback branch (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`), detect when `reuse_atom`'s parent type is niche-shaped (`Repr.is_niche_shaped`) and skip FBIP entirely — allocate fresh without touching the reuse atom's RC (ownership flows from niche payload → branch var → Ok field). Root cause: Perceus's `add_scrutinee_free_for` insertes `EDecRC(scrutinee)` because the scrutinee and branch var have different TIR names even though they're the same runtime pointer under niche encoding; `args_alias_reuse` can't catch value aliasing. Verified: `dune build` clean, 300 compiler + 320 codegen tests all pass. This unblocks `NetKernel.recv_frame` which was hanging due to the self-referential Ok object confusing `List.length` in the caller.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — clustering & RPC reference guide (`docs/clustering.md`, 2026-06-22)** — 391-line reference covering all five distributed layers: node identity (`NodeIdentity`, `NodeId = sha256(pubkey)`, incarnation), authenticated handshake (`ClusterAuth` HMAC challenge/response, `Handshake` nonce binding), `Membership` CRDT (incarnation-ordered merge, SWIM `Alive|Suspect|Dead` states), SWIM failure detection (`Swim` pure FSM + `SwimDriver` I/O glue, probe/indirect/gossip lifecycle), `GlobalRegistry` CRDT (vector-clock per name, deterministic concurrent tiebreak), `GlobalPid` wire type, `RemoteRef`/`CallError` taxonomy, and the `NodeRpc` responder pattern. Notes the current pure-module status (no live socket wiring yet) and what remains for P2 (two-node integration test) and P3 (compiler-emitted enroll/stub).

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 compiler enroll/stub (`remote_ref_hashes` + `remote_register_stub` + `remote_count` builtins, 2026-06-22)** — the compiler touchpoint that closes the CAS-hash gap: functions now carry their real identity into the runtime. Three new builtins wired through all three layers (typecheck `builtin_bindings`, eval `builtin_env`, codegen). **`remote_ref_hashes(mod, fn)`** is constant-folded at codegen time — the `emit_expr` handler intercepts the `EApp` before it becomes an LLVM call, looks up the Blake3 64-char hex hashes from the CAS pipeline's `hash_module` output, and emits a heap-allocated `(String, String)` tuple. Two fixes were required: (1) added all three builtins to `defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` so defun doesn't wrap them in closures (keeping them as `EApp`, not `ECallPtr`); (2) top-level user-module functions have no module prefix in their TIR `fn_name` (`"fib"` not `"TestRRH.fib"`) so the lookup tries the unqualified name when the qualified lookup misses. **`remote_register_stub(impl, sig, stub)`** maps to `march_remote_register` (runtime C registry). **`remote_count()`** maps to `march_remote_count`. **`march_remote_init()`** is auto-emitted in `@main` before user code runs. Eval path uses FNV-1a 32-char hex (deterministic, non-empty). Tests: 8 eval tests in `test_stdlib_march` (`test_remote_ref_hashes.march`) + 1 compiled native test (`test/native/remote_ref_hashes.march` — verifies 64-char Blake3 hashes with a recursive `fib` to survive DCE). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 function-by-identity registry (`runtime/march_remote_registry.{c,h}`, 2026-06-22)** — increment 3a: the one genuinely net-new runtime mechanism (the spec flagged `runtime/` had no function-by-identity registry). A mutex-guarded chained hash table keyed by a target's CAS `impl_hash` → its marshalling stub (`void *`), recording `sig_hash` for the admission check. `register` (idempotent on identity) / `lookup` / `sig_hash` / `has` / `count` / `init` / `shutdown`. Distinct from the HCR `march_dispatch` table (NAME_ID-indexed + versioned); this is identity-keyed for cross-node dispatch. Linked into compiled binaries at both `bin/main.ml` sites; CAS key auto-includes it (dir-glob). 7-group C unit test (`test/test_remote_registry.c`, in `dune runtest`); codegen 320 still green. **Next L4:** `remote_ref` lowering (sig_hash/impl_hash from CAS — Touchpoint 1), serializable-signature gate, compiler-emitted marshalling stubs + `@main` register calls + DCE pinning, then wire to the live net-kernel socket. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 RPC dispatcher (`node_rpc.march`, 2026-06-22)** — increment 2 of L4: the pure receive-side dispatcher + caller-side logic between the net-kernel transport and a node's enrolled targets. `Target = {sig_hash, impl_hash, invoke : args-bytes -> result-bytes}`; `Targets` is an injected `name → Target` table (modelling what the compiler-emitted enroll/stub mechanism must supply). `handle_request` → lookup → `RemoteCall.verify` admission → invoke-or-map-failure (`NoTarget` / `TypeMismatch` / `VersionSkew` / `RemoteExit` / `Returned`); `handle_frame` decodes + dispatches. Caller side: `interpret`, `matches` (correlation), `timed_out`, `peer_down_error`. Added `NoTarget` to `RemoteCall.CallError`. 12 TDD asserts with a real invoker stub; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 43→44). **Next (final L4 increment):** the enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism (function-by-identity registry + marshalling stubs + DCE pinning + `remote_ref` builtin — Compiler Touchpoint 1), then wire to the live net-kernel socket. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 remote-call safety core (`global_pid.march` + `remote_call.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the first increment of the headline RPC layer: the wire protocol + CAS admission decision (enroll/stub runtime mechanism + socket transport are follow-on increments). `GlobalPid` (`Pid = {node_id, local_pid, creation}`): the serializable cluster-wide process handle (`creation` distinguishes a restarted node's reused pids); `make`/`eq` + MessagePack codec. `RemoteCall`: `RemoteRef = {module_name, fn_name, sig_hash, impl_hash}` + **the safety core `verify(local_sig, local_impl, fref)`** → `Accept` / `Reject(TypeMismatch)` (sig mismatch, never invoke) / `Reject(VersionSkew)` (body mismatch); `CallError` taxonomy (`DeadlineExceeded|NoConnection|RemoteExit|TypeMismatch|VersionSkew`) for failure isolation; full MessagePack codecs for `CallRequest`/`CallReply` (framable in a NetFrame). 13 TDD asserts; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 41→43). **Next:** the enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism (function-by-identity registry + marshalling stubs + DCE pinning + `remote_ref` builtin — Compiler Touchpoint 1), then `Node.call` dispatch + failure isolation over the net-kernel. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L5 global registry CRDT (`global_registry.march`, 2026-06-22)** — `GlobalRegistry`: the cluster-wide name registry as a composite CRDT mapping `name → {node_id, pid}`. Each binding carries a `VectorClock` (causally orders updates to that name) + a presence bit so `unregister` converges like an LWW-element-set removal. Per-name resolution: causally-newer wins (`VectorClock.compare` → `After`); older/`Equal` keeps existing (idempotent); `Concurrent` broken by a deterministic `(node_id, pid)` tiebreak → every replica converges. `merge` is associative/commutative/idempotent (the gossip/anti-entropy join). API: `register`/`unregister`/`lookup`/`contains`/`names`/`size`/`observe`/`merge`; built on the `VectorClock` primitive. 11 TDD asserts (`test_global_registry.march`: register/lookup, tombstone, causal override + stale-ignore, concurrent convergence both orders, idempotence, union, removal-converges). Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 40→41). **Remaining:** ConsistentHash-partitioned ownership + Merkle anti-entropy (`diff()`); serializable `Pid` (H5); load-aware dispatch (L6). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L3 SWIM runtime driver (`swim_driver.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the I/O glue for the pure Swim FSM. `State = {Swim.State, Rng, period_ms, period_end}`; `Event = PingAck(node_id) | GossipFrame(Member) | PeerDown(node_id)`; `step(state, now, events)` drives probe periods (end → expire suspects → refute self → begin new with random target), mid-period indirect escalation, and event folding. `PeerDown` skips Suspect and marks Dead at the current incarnation so the verdict supersedes any prior Alive. Wire protocol: Msgpack arrays with integer tag dispatch (0=SwimPing, 1=SwimPingAck, 2=SwimPingReq, 3=SwimGossip); `encode_msg`/`decode_msg` round-trip. `dispatch`/`dispatch_all` fan Actions to peers via `PeerRegistry` + `NetKernel`. 17 TDD asserts; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 44→45). Gotcha: `else if` chains are not yet implemented — use `match n do 0 -> ... end` for multi-branch integer dispatch. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L3 SWIM probe loop (`swim.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the pure SWIM failure-detection state machine over the `Membership` CRDT. Each transition takes state + clock + injected randomness (probe target, helper set) and returns the next state plus the I/O `Action`s the runtime must perform (`SendPing`/`SendPingReq`/`Gossip`); the socket/timer glue lives in the runtime layer (same split as `NetKernel`/`ClusterConn`), keeping detection deterministic + unit-testable. Protocol period: `begin_period` → direct ping; overdue ack → `escalate_indirect` (k-way ping-req, once); period end unacked → Suspect + Gossip + arm suspect→dead timer; `expire_suspects` → Dead; `on_gossip` folds deltas (higher-incarnation Alive refutes + cancels the timer); `refute` bumps our incarnation when falsely suspected. All state rides `Membership.observe` (incarnation-ordered CRDT merge) so views converge order-independently. 11 TDD asserts (`test_swim.march`); pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 39→40). Parser gotchas recorded: `init` is a reserved keyword; `let x = V` before a `(`-led tuple return glommed as application `V(...)` (fix: bind to bare name). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — P1 net-kernel (L1+L2) + L3 membership CRDT (2026-06-21)** — Eight new stdlib modules building the authenticated peer connection + member set, plus a compiler change to expose raw TCP accept. **L1/L2 (transport + net-kernel):** `net_frame` (`NetFrame`, length-prefixed partial-read-safe framing), `node_identity` (`NodeIdentity`, `{name, NodeId=sha256(pubkey), incarnation}` + msgpack codec), `cluster_auth` (`ClusterAuth`, shared-secret HMAC challenge/response), `handshake` (`Handshake`, `Hello` codec + `authenticate` — proof must bind to the nonce we issued), `net_kernel` (`NetKernel`, byte bridge `List(Int)`↔Socket `String` + framed `send_frame`/`recv_frame` + symmetric authenticated `handshake` over a socket), `peer_registry` (`PeerRegistry`, one-connection-per-peer table by NodeId), `cluster_conn` (`ClusterConn`, `listen`/`connect_to_peer`/`accept_one` lifecycle). **L3:** `membership` (`Membership`, SWIM-style member view `{Alive|Suspect|Dead, incarnation}` with a CRDT merge = lexicographic max over `(incarnation, severity)`; associative/commutative/idempotent so gossip converges). **Compiler:** `tcp_listen`/`tcp_accept` are exposed to March (`Int -> Result(Int, String)`) — typecheck sigs + `IO.NetListen` cap, eval `Unix` impls returning a tagged `Result` (the runtime + codegen already had the raw-int64 primitives; the typechecker didn't, so the server-accept side was unwritable). Exposed concurrently on `main`; an earlier branch commit that exposed them as `Int -> Int` was superseded by this Result-typed design. The C `march_tcp_listen/accept` still return raw int64, so the compiled path's codegen ret-type stays `TInt` — a known surface/runtime divergence for the (unexercised) compiled path. All pure logic eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 30→38); socket I/O typecheck-clean + runtime-exercised (eval harness doesn't run sockets, per `ChannelServer`). Also fixed `char.march` (Phase-2 `Char`-migration left `char_to_int(c:Char)` mismatching the `String`-typed builtin → realigned to String). Specs: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`, `specs/progress.md`. **Remaining:** SWIM probe loop (ping/timers), global registry CRDT (L5), load-aware dispatch (L6), a loopback integration test, constant-time proof compare.
- ✅ **Refinement types: record param refinements in postcondition VCs — Gap #1 Part B (2026-06-25)** — `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`: the `scope` entry gains an optional record-sort tag `(binder, pred, sort_opt)`. `refined_scope_ty` admits both `Int` and registered-TyCon record refinements. `scope_add_param`/`scope_add_binding` use it; `sig_of_clause` keeps `refined_int_ty` (Decision R3 — no call-site VCs for record params). `scope_facts` returns `(decls, assumptions, has_record: bool)`: record entries declare an opaque `SData sort_name` const, build a `make_field_resolver`, and reflect the predicate via field selectors. `check_post` triggers the preamble when RETURN OR scope is record-sorted; a composite `scope_field_resolver` threads into `scalar` so `old.count` in the return resolves via selectors. With `scope_has_record = true`, a SAT counterexample from the first discharge is a real violation and is reported. `make_field_resolver` moved before `scope_facts`; `reflect_scalar` updated to 3-tuple. **62 tests** (61 pass; `resolution.0` pre-existing). B1: `keep(old:{s:State|s.count>=0}):{v:State|v.count>=0} → {count:old.count}` verifies; B2: `drop(...) → {count:old.count-1}` refuted (SAT cex: old.count=0); B3: `migrate → {count:0,history:Nil}` with `v.count>=0 && len(v.history)==v.count` verifies; `→ {count:old.count,history:Nil}` refuted (cex: old.count=1). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-gap1-full.md`.
- ✅ **Refinement types: record postconditions + measures over record fields — Gap #1 Part A (2026-06-25)** — `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml` extended to discharge predicates over record-typed return values, including measure applications on record fields (e.g. `len(v.history) == v.count`, `mlength(v.history) == v.count`). (1) `TDRecord` types registered as 1-ctor SMT datatypes (`M_<name>` sort, ctor name = type name, selectors `<Ctor>_<idx>`) in the existing two-pass `register_adt_names`/`register_field_sorts` machinery; new `ctor_field_names` table maps ctor → field names in declaration order. (2) `smt_of` gains optional `?resolve_field` and `?resolve_measure_app` parameters; `EField(EVar x, fname)` emits field selectors; `ECon(ctor, args)` reflects zero/multi-arity constructors (`Nil`, `Cons`) to SMT applications when the ctor is registered in `ctor_field_sorts`; measure application on non-variable args is routed through `resolve_measure_app`. (3) `return_refine_ext` extends `return_refine` to also return the SMT sort name for record-typed return refinements. (4) `check_post` extended with `?record_sort`, `reflect_record_literal`, `make_field_resolver`, and `resolve_measure_app`; `concrete_len`/`concrete_measure_app`/`selector_reduce` evaluate measure applications at the OCaml level (avoids Z3 quantifier axioms for concrete list/ADT terms); `needs_axiom_preamble` flag selects between full quantified preamble and lightweight `type_only_preamble()` (avoids Z3 `unknown` from `forall` axioms on SAT queries). (5) `measure_arms` now accepts `PatWild` (`_` in pattern positions like `Cons(_, t)`) by assigning generated names (`_w1`, …), fixing a root-cause bug that prevented any measure with a wildcard arm from being axiomatized; this unblocked axioms-ma, list-axioms-mb, mutual-mc, flag-gating tests simultaneously. Verified: `len(v.history)==v.count` and `mlength(v.history)==v.count` both discharge correctly on `{count:0,history:Nil}` (pass) and `{count:1,history:Nil}` (violation reported). **58 tests in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`** (+7 in `record-postconditions` group, 57 pass; `resolution.0` is a pre-existing failure). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-record-spike.md`. **Remaining (Gap #1 full):** lift `is_int_base` gates for record-typed *param* refinements; `@invariant` on `State` type. Gaps #2–3 (persist invariant in `.schemas.json`, `--check-migration` CLI mode): `specs/2026-06-25-refinement-verified-state-migration-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types Path 1: use/alias-aware call resolution (2026-06-21)** — replaced the fragile "qualified key OR globally-unique bare name" matching (`build_table`/`collect_sig_list`) with proper lexical resolution that mirrors the typechecker. `collect_all_defs` records EVERY function (refined or not) under its fully-qualified key; a new `resolve_call` resolves a call name through (1) lexical module scope — the nearest enclosing module that defines the name wins, with shadowing, (2) alias expansion (`alias X.Y as P` → `P.foo` resolves to `X.Y.foo`), (3) `use`-imported bare names (`use M.{f}` / `use M.*`). The resolution context (module path + in-scope aliases/uses, inherited by nested modules) is threaded through `visit`/`visit_decls`. Recording non-refined defs is what makes it sound: a bare call resolving to a non-refined sibling no longer falls through to a refined function of the same name in an enclosing module (which would check the wrong predicate — a false positive). This both CATCHES violations the old code dropped on any cross-module name collision AND stays false-positive-free. Wrong resolution can at worst *skip* (only a refined resolution triggers a check). Verified end-to-end through the full pipeline (a `use`-imported `take_pos(-7)` is caught). 5 new tests (51 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`: collision-resolution, local-vs-colliding, alias, use, shadowing-safety); a new desugaring test harness (`has_refine_error_d`) for qualified calls, since the parser emits `EField` and desugar flattens to a dotted `EVar`. **Remaining Path 1:** concrete unfolding of user measures over literal constructors; use/alias module *paths* resolved relative to the top scope only (deeply-nested `use` of an inner-of-inner module isn't prefix-walked — rare). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-refinement-types-state-and-forward-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: measure axioms M-c (cross-measure + mutual recursion) + performance/flag-gating (2026-06-21)** — **M-c**: lifted M-b's self-recursion-only restriction. A `@[measure]` may now call *another axiomatisable measure* (argument still required to be a structural component, so every call — self or cross — decreases and the whole system is total). Axiomatisability is resolved by a **greatest fixpoint** over the candidate set (a measure calling a non-axiomatisable one cascades to symbolic), and all `declare-fun`s precede all axioms so forward/mutual references resolve. **Mutually-recursive datatypes** are emitted as the transitive ADT closure in one combined `declare-datatypes`, and a constructor field of *any* registered ADT (not just self) reflects to that ADT's sort so cross-ADT calls are well-sorted. Verified: `tsize`/`fsize` over mutually-recursive `RTree`/`RForest` compute structurally (depth-2 forest → size 2, out-of-bounds caught). Gate still covers self-recursion; a non-structural mutual cycle isn't a hard error but stays unaxiomatised (sound symbolic fallback). **Performance + flag-gating**: measured the cost (cold VC cache) — 40 concrete-structure measure-bound VCs take **69.9 s** with axioms vs **0.45 s** symbolic (~155×; many near the 3 s timeout), but warm-cache rebuilds are ~0.5 s. Key fix: the datatype/quantifier preamble is now attached to a VC **only when it references an axiomatised measure** (postconditions, which reflect measures symbolically, never get it), so 150 plain Int/Bool VCs alongside a defined measure cost ~1 s — the axiom tax is isolated to measure-using VCs. New `--no-measure-axioms` flag reflects measures symbolically and disables the gate (changes only diagnostics, not the artifact, so not in the CAS key). Axioms stay **on by default** (non-measure code unaffected; measure consumers are rare; warm cache covers iterative dev). 6 new tests (46 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`: 3 mutual-recursion, 3 flag-gating). **Remaining:** M-d (relational postconditions — needs reflecting an operation's *result* as a datatype term); a measure call on the whole matched value (`A(t) = B(t) + …`); per-query timeout tuning. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: measure axioms M-a (quantified recursion equations) + M-b (soundness gate + built-in `List`) (2026-06-21)** — **M-a**: a `@[measure]` over a user ADT is *axiomatised*, not just reflected symbolically. The ADT is modelled with `declare-datatypes` (element type opaque `Elem`, self-recursive fields), the measure as an uninterpreted `declare-fun`, and each `match` arm as a `:pattern`-triggered recursion-equation axiom — so the solver computes measure values *structurally* (`size(Node(Leaf,x,Leaf)) = 1`, out-of-bounds tree indices caught). 3s solver timeout keeps hard quantified queries `unknown` → definite-failure skip (never hang, never unsound). **M-b**: (1) the **hard soundness gate** — a `@[measure]` is a promise of a total, terminating, pure function; the encoding trusts it, so a measure that is effectful (`spawn`/`send`/`dbg`/`assert`), divergent (`panic`/`todo`/`exit`), non-total (a `/`/`%` that can divide by zero, or a non-exhaustive `match` on its ADT param), or not structurally recursive (a recursive call whose argument is not a component of the matched parameter) is now a **compile error**, not a silent symbolic fallback (a sound-but-unaxiomatisable measure — multi-arg, element-inspecting, `let`-bodied — still falls back silently). (2) **built-in `List(a)`** modelled as `Nil | Cons(a, List(a))`, so user list measures (`length`) axiomatise like user ADTs (`length([10,20]) = 2`). (3) datatype sort names moved to a private `M_` namespace so no ADT name collides with a z3-reserved sort (`List`, `Array`, `Seq`, …); axiom bodies restricted to self-recursion only (cross-measure/`len` calls deferred to M-c — their symbols aren't declared). 6 new tests (40 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`). **Remaining (per spec):** M-c (measures calling measures, mutual recursion, mutually-recursive datatypes), M-d (relational postconditions like `size(insert(t,x)) == size(t)+1`), performance measurement / flag-gating. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types Path 1: collision-safe resolution (P1a) + assert-as-assume (P1c) + user measures (P1b) (2026-06-21)** — P1a: `build_table` registers qualified names always but a bare name only when one definition uses it, so colliding bare names across modules are conservatively skipped (no false positives); qualified resolution unchanged. P1c: `assert(p)` acts as an `assume`, extending the path context (reuses `EAssert`). P1b: `@[measure]` functions register as measures; any `m(x)` reflects to a consistent SMT symbol `m$x`, so guarded bounds over user-defined structure (`size`/`depth`) verify via path sensitivity (`len` keeps concrete-for-literals + non-negativity; user measures symbolic, no axiom in v1). P1d: counterexamples rendered for humans (`m$x` → `m(x) = v`) on pre- and post-condition violations, plus a `note:` line guiding the fix. P1b follow-up — non-negativity inference: a syntactically non-negative `@[measure]` gets the sound `m(x) >= 0` axiom (so count measures work unguarded — a `size`-returning function verifies a `_ >= 0` postcondition; `_ < 0` is caught). 31 tests total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`. **Remaining Path 1:** P1b quantified recursion-equation axioms (SMT datatype modeling — the one large follow-up; **design speced** in `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`, gated on a hard totality/termination/purity soundness check) + concrete unfolding of user measures over literal constructors. Known limit (RESOLVED 2026-06-21): qualified-string matching was fragile for *nested* modules — now replaced with use/alias-aware lexical resolution (see the Path 1 use/alias resolution entry above). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-refinement-types-state-and-forward-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: TRefine carried in the internal type (2026-06-21)** — increment 1 (`TRefine of ty * string * Ast.expr`, transparent via `repr`-strip, 8 match sites) merged; increment 2a (`generalize`/`instantiate` preserve the wrapper) on branch. Behavior-preserving foundation for a future refinement-subtyping core. **Key finding:** transparent-`repr` TRefine cannot do higher-order checking (unification strips the refinement).

- ✅ **Refinement types: path sensitivity, postconditions, stdlib hardening (2026-06-21)** — (1) **Path sensitivity**: `if`/`when`/`match` guards become SMT assumptions (then-branch gets the condition, else gets its negation); variable args reflected as stable constants so a guard about `i` discharges a precondition about `i`. Guarded code (`if i >= 0 && i < len(xs) do at(xs, i)`) now verifies; partial guards skip; contradicting guards are rejected. (2) **Postconditions**: a function's return-position (tail) expressions are checked against its return refinement (`fn f(..) -> {Int | _ >= 0}`), threading path/scope; refined params contribute assumptions. (3) **Hardening**: `--check` over the *entire* annotated stdlib (all 57 modules walked) yields **zero false positives**, while genuine violations (`List.chunks(xs, 0)`) are still caught. All three keep the definite-failure soundness stance (report only when a predicate can never hold). 12 new tests (28 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`). **Finding:** clean *checkable* stdlib consumers are rare — defensive clamping + recursion (un-provable without induction) limit them to a few (`chunks` size, `Decimal.div` precision). Remaining gap: higher-order/indirect calls + interface dispatch, which need TRefine-in-the-type (35-site typecheck.ml rewrite, deferred).

- ✅ **Refinement types A2 (minimal): `len` measure + bounds checking (2026-06-21)** — extend `lib/refinecheck` with the `len` measure, cross-argument predicate resolution (a predicate on `i` can reference sibling param `xs`), and static list-literal length. Bounds like `{Int | _ >= 0 && _ < len(xs)}` are checkable: `at([10,20,30], 5)`/`3`/`-1` rejected (out-of-bounds, off-by-one, negative), `1`/`0` pass, **unknown-length args conservatively skipped**. **Soundness fix:** a violation is reported only when the precondition can *never* hold (definite-failure check — `discharge(¬goal)` Verified), so symbolic lengths never false-positive. 6 bounds tests (`test/test_refinecheck.ml`). Next: a non-Option stdlib indexer as a bounds consumer; then TRefine-in-type for robust integration.
- ✅ **Refinement types A1b (minimal): call-site precondition checking (2026-06-21)** — `lib/refinecheck/`: post-typecheck pass collects `{Int | predicate}` params and discharges precondition VCs at call sites through the A0 Z3 bridge. Catches literal violations (`take_n(-3)`, `chunks(xs, 0)`) with counterexamples; passes valid literals and refined-local propagation; conservatively skips unconstrained args (no false positives). Wired into `bin/main.ml` (`--check`/`--compile`). 5 integration tests (`test/test_refinecheck.ml`). Stdlib annotations as genuine consumers: `List.chunks(xs, size : {Int | _ > 0})` (size<=0 infinite-loops) and `Decimal.div(..., precision : {Int | _ >= 0})`. **Finding:** most March stdlib `Int` params defensively *clamp* negatives, so they have no true precondition — naive `_ >= 0` would be wrongly strict; genuine preconditions are rarer (unguarded divisors, chunk sizes). **Scope/limits:** Int/Bool over `_` only, direct named calls, no path sensitivity, bare-name sig collisions possible; needs `MARCH_STDLIB` in the worktree (the `_build` stdlib copy is stale). Next: TRefine-in-internal-ty for robust HO/cross-module integration + the `len` measure (A2).
- ✅ **Refinement types A1a: surface syntax + erasure (2026-06-21)** — `{T|p}` / `{v:T|p}` parse to `Ast.TyRefine` and erase to their base type through the whole pipeline (typechecks identically; no checking yet). `_` is now an expression atom. 11 exhaustive `Ast.ty` matches updated; faithful formatter rendering. 3 parser tests; full suite green. **Next: A1b** — emit Int-only precondition VCs at call sites through the A0 bridge, discharge via `Refine.discharge`, report counterexamples. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-21-refinement-types-a1-typechecker-wiring.md`.
- ✅ **RRB.Vec v2 + atomic RC in scheduler threads (2026-06-24)** — `stdlib/rrb_vec.march` rewritten to Array-backed 32-way trie (`ptype Vec(a) = Vec(Array(a))` / `ptype Slice(a) = Slice(Array(a), Int, Int)`). `chunk(v, n)` zero-copy creates `n` Slices all pointing to the same Array; `fold(s, z, f)` iterates `[lo, hi)` without allocating. Fixed `array.march` off-by-5 in `descend`: `trie_slot` must receive `s - 5` not `s` when entering an existing branch. Runtime fix: `march_incrc_local`/`march_decrc_local` now delegate to atomic `march_incrc`/`march_decrc` when `march_sched_in_scheduler()` — multiple tasks sharing an Array via zero-copy slices raced on the RC field across 4 OS scheduler threads causing segfaults ("local RC underflow" aborts). Stress-tested: `Parallel.psum(RRB.range(1, 130))` × 20 runs clean; n ∈ {10,50,64,100,129,256,1000,10000,100000} with 4–8 workers all pass. Test count unchanged: **321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march**.
- ✅ **Perceus dead br_var EDecRC leak fix (2026-06-24)** — In the Perceus pass (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`), pattern-match branches that bind constructor fields but never use them (e.g. `PVec(n, _, _, _) -> n`) caused a permanent RC leak.
