## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 4: `llvm_builtins.ml` — declarative builtin table (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`, HAZARD H2)

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml`** replaces llvm_emit.ml's four historically-unsynchronized builtin structures (`is_builtin_fn`'s 272-name `List.mem` list, `builtin_ret_ty`, `mangle_extern`, and the ~400-line hand-written preamble `declare` blob — source of ≥5 "missing builtin" fix commits per `specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md` §5) with one ordered table (`builtin` record: `march_name`; `c_name : string option`; `ret_ty : Tir.ty option`; `in_is_builtin : bool`; `declare_sig : string option`), 318 rows, in the preamble's real declaration order (286 rows with a preamble presence, then 32 declare-less rows — arithmetic/comparison operators and `int_and`/etc bitwise ops emitted as native LLVM instructions, `task_spawn`/`task_await`/etc and `pmap_threshold`/`get_work_pool`/`remote_ref_hashes` synthesized/constant-folded by dedicated `EApp` match arms — appended in their original `is_builtin_fn`-list order since they have no preamble position). All three consumers (`is_builtin_fn`, `builtin_ret_ty`, `mangle_extern`) are hashtables built once from the table at module load, answer-identical to the deleted functions (verified against a throwaway probe executable calling both old and new implementations over the full 318-name universe plus negative-control names: 0 mismatches). `Llvm_emit.mangle_extern` stays re-exported (lib/jit/repl_jit.ml references it as `March_tir.Llvm_emit.mangle_extern`); `Llvm_emit.emit_preamble` becomes a thin wrapper translating `target_config` to the `is_wasm`/`triple` values `Llvm_builtins.emit_preamble` needs (kept decoupled from `target_config` to avoid a module cycle, since `bin/main.ml` depends on `Llvm_emit.target_config`/`Native`/etc extensively and moving those was out of this task's scope).
- ✅ **HAZARD H2 (byte-identical preamble): satisfied by construction, not by a formatting function.** The original blob's per-line whitespace is hand-tuned and inconsistent (e.g. `declare i64  @foo` vs `declare i64    @bar` — return-type column padding varies within the same block to visually align with neighboring `double`-typed lines), so a generic `declare_sig`-from-fields formatter would risk silent reformatting drift. Instead each row's `declare_sig` holds the EXACT historical `declare ...` line text (copied verbatim); `Llvm_builtins.emit_preamble` replays a fixed per-target list of `preamble_item`s (`PComment`/`PDeclare`/`POther`/`PBlank` markers mirroring the original blob's line-by-line structure 1:1, including its native/WASM/REPL conditional branches) and resolves each `PDeclare` marker's text via a C-symbol lookup across the table + a 57-entry `runtime_only_declares` table (RC ops, Hot-Code-Reload/dispatch machinery, the test harness, LLVM intrinsics, task/scheduler internals — preamble declares with no March-callable name at all, hence no `builtin` row). Verified byte-identical for all three target/repl combinations (native non-REPL, native REPL, WASM) against the actual pre-refactor `emit_preamble` output via a throwaway probe executable, AND pinned as a permanent regression test: `test/test_codegen.ml`'s new `llvm_builtins_preamble_golden` suite (4 tests) embeds the four deleted `Buffer.add_string buf {| ... |}` literals verbatim as goldens and diffs `Llvm_builtins.emit_preamble`'s actual output against them byte-for-byte (confirmed the test actually catches drift, not just passes vacuously, by transiently corrupting one declare line's text and observing 3 of 4 sub-tests fail, then reverting).
- ✅ **Membership drift FILED, NOT repaired (Step 3 — the drift the table now makes visible):** two drift classes found, both preserved exactly as historical behavior via the table's optional/bool fields (no behavior change in this task):
  - **38 names have a `builtin_ret_ty` + `mangle_extern` + preamble entry but are ABSENT from `is_builtin_fn`'s recognition set:** `chan_new`/`chan_send`/`chan_recv`/`chan_close`/`chan_choose`/`chan_offer`, `mpst_new`/`mpst_send`/`mpst_recv`/`mpst_close`, `record_keys`/`record_values`/`record_entries`/`record_get`/`record_has_key`/`record_put`/`record_from_list`, `ws_handshake`/`ws_recv`/`ws_send`/`ws_select`, `http_parse_request`/`http_serialize_response`/`http_server_listen`, `tcp_recv_http`/`tcp_send_all`, `html_auto_escape`, `panic`, `send_linear`, `sha1_bytes`, `stdlib_gzip_encode`/`stdlib_gzip_decode`/`stdlib_deflate_encode`/`stdlib_deflate_decode`/`stdlib_zstd_encode`/`stdlib_zstd_decode`/`stdlib_brotli_encode`/`stdlib_brotli_decode`. Consequence: `is_leaf_callee` (`is_builtin_fn name || Tir_names.has_runtime_prefix name`) treats a direct call to any of these BY ITS MARCH NAME as non-leaf, forcing an unnecessary reduction-check emission (`emit_reduction_check`) in any caller whose body is otherwise leaf — a missed optimization, not a correctness bug (the reduction check is always safe to over-emit). Likely explanation: these names are dispatched through dedicated non-generic `EApp` match arms (session-typed channels, MPST, record introspection, WS/HTTP, compression) that never needed `is_builtin_fn`'s "is this a no-op-for-RC-purposes operator/value" check, so nobody noticed the recognition-set gap. Repair (if ever done) is a `is_builtin_fn` membership fix, tested via `expr_has_call`/reduction-check IR assertions — out of scope here.
  - **7 names (`march_compare_int`/`march_compare_float`/`march_compare_string`/`march_hash_int`/`march_hash_float`/`march_hash_string`/`march_hash_bool`) have a `builtin_ret_ty` + preamble entry but NO `mangle_extern` case and are ABSENT from `is_builtin_fn`:** these are already-mangled C-runtime names used directly as March-level call targets (no renaming needed, so `mangle_extern`'s identity fallthrough naturally does the right thing) — the reduction-check gap above doesn't apply to these specifically since `Tir_names.has_runtime_prefix` (the `is_leaf_callee` OR-clause) already recognizes the `march_` prefix. Recorded here only because the table's per-structure membership flags make the `is_builtin_fn`-absence visible; no known consequence.
  - Separately noted (NOT drift, verified as intentional dedicated-codegen design, no filing needed): arithmetic/comparison operators (`+`/`-`/`==`/etc, native LLVM instructions), `int_and`/`int_or`/`int_xor`/`int_not`/`int_shl`/`int_shr`/`int_popcount` (native bitwise instructions), `native_int_arr_*`/`native_float_arr_*` (18 names, `mangle_extern`-bypassing direct-emit arms), `task_spawn`/`task_await`/`task_await_unwrap`/`task_yield`/`task_spawn_steal`/`task_reductions`/`pmap_threshold`/`get_work_pool`/`remote_ref_hashes` (constant-folded or synthesized by dedicated `EApp` arms), and `main` (the user's own compiled entry point, not a runtime builtin).
- ✅ **Performance side-effect (the one permitted non-cosmetic change):** `is_builtin_fn` was `List.mem` over a 272-element list on hot paths (`emit_atom`, `is_leaf_callee`/`expr_has_call`'s reduction-check analysis, the `EIncRC`/`EDecRC`/`EFree` shadow guards) — O(n) per call. Now backed by a `Hashtbl.t` built once at module load — O(1) per call, answer-identical (verified above). No other behavior change.
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) at 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen (370 base +4 new `llvm_builtins_preamble_golden` tests) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — zero `.expected` diffs. Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm` on all four benchmarks (fib, binary_trees, tree_transform, list_ops) against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `50c30f18` — byte-identical `.ll` in all four (0 diff lines) — the preamble is part of every `.ll`, so this is the loudest possible H2 check. Compiled (`--compile --opt 2`) benchmark outputs byte-identical to expected values (fib=102334155, tree_transform=104857600, list_ops=333333666666, binary_trees check=65535); timings within noise (largest delta tree_transform +4.6%, no regression >10%). `scripts/check-docs.sh` passes clean. Full report: `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-4-report.md`.

## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 5 (BACKFILLED by Task 10): `llvm_eq.ml`/`llvm_data.ml`/`llvm_case.ml` split (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`)

*This entry was not added in the same commit as the work (`ec294fb8`) — Tasks 5 and 6 shipped without their own todos.md/progress.md entries, filed by Task 7 (see its "Filed, not this task's scope" bullet above) and backfilled here by Task 10.*

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/llvm_eq.ml`** (477 lines: `mangle_ty_for_eq`, `ensure_adt_eq_fn` — the niche/boxed/tuple/record structural-equality-function generator, plus `field_load_llty`/`apply_ty_subst`), **`lib/tir/llvm_data.ml`** (207 lines: GEP/alloc helpers, `ctor_entry` lookup, record-field lookup, the record-shape-metadata family), **`lib/tir/llvm_case.ml`** (792 lines: `emit_case` — repr-strategy dispatch for newtype/niche/boxed-ADT matches, FBIP-aware RC bookkeeping on scrutinees). `llvm_emit.ml` 6051 → 4606 lines. All verbatim moves (round-trip verified: mechanical qualification substitutions stripped back out and diffed clean against the pre-move extraction). `emit_case`'s mutual recursion with `emit_expr` de-cycled via two labeled callbacks (`~emit_expr`, `~emit_atom`) — 2 total, well under the plan's ">4 = BLOCKED" threshold. Two deviations beyond the brief's literal list, both forced by fan-in from all three new modules and both moved to `llvm_ctx.ml` instead of staying in `llvm_emit.ml`: the `MARCH_REPR_AUDIT` diagnostic block (`repr_audit_on`/`_repr_audit`/`repr_audit_record`/`repr_audit_report`) and `alloca_name` (a pure ctx-hashtable helper Task 3 had explicitly left in place until a real non-`llvm_emit.ml` consumer existed — `emit_case` is that consumer).
- ⚠️ **Process note: a false completion claim was caught and corrected during this task, then amended into the same commit (`ec294fb8`).** The original report claimed the two byte-identical copies of `is_trivial_dec_chain_returning` had been deduplicated into one; `git diff 1bfbe83e ec294fb8`'s first pass showed zero lines touching the function — the dedup was planned and identity-verified but never executed. Caught by review; the actual dedup (deleting the second copy, all three callers already resolving to the surviving first definition, doc comment consolidated) was applied and the commit amended (`git commit --amend --no-edit`) before landing. Gates were re-run post-amendment (six runners green, zero snapshot churn, byte-identical IR ×4 vs base). Recorded here as one of Task 10's three folded-in fix-cycle findings (see the chunk-2 wrap-up entry in `specs/progress.md`).
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) — 791 stdlib via `scripts/run-tests.sh` (58.6s first run, 41.6s repeat after a forced clean rebuild). Zero `.expected` snapshot churn. Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm --opt 2` on all four benchmarks against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `1bfbe83e` — byte-identical `.ll` in all four. Compiled (`--compile --opt 2`) outputs correct; timings within noise (largest delta tree_transform +1.8%). Full report (incl. the addendum documenting the false-claim correction): `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-5-report.md`.

## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 6 (BACKFILLED by Task 10): `llvm_calls.ml`/`llvm_tco.ml` split (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`)

*Same backfill note as Task 5 above — this entry was not added in the same commit (`ce2fb5aa`).*

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/llvm_calls.ml`** (160 lines: `ok_payload_ty`, `emit_raises_wrapper`, `fail_if_unresolved_iface_method`, `clo_wrap_define`) and **`lib/tir/llvm_tco.ml`** (500 lines: `tail_calls_in`, `has_non_tail_group_call`, `tarjan_sccs`, `find_mutual_tco_groups`, `mutual_tco_combined_name`, `has_self_tail_call`, `emit_mutual_tco_group`, plus `is_trivial_dec_chain_returning`/`is_trivial_dec_chain` moved as the single post-Task-5-dedup definition). `llvm_emit.ml` 4610 → 4015 lines. The brief's literal EApp/ECallPtr "helper" description turned out to describe logic inline within `emit_expr`'s match arms themselves (general call path, blocking-extern dispatch, HCR versioned-dispatch routing, `is_apply_fn`-aware ret_ty resolution) rather than separable standalone functions — left in place per the "arms stay, only whole functions move" discipline. Two deviations moved to `llvm_ctx.ml` (same "pure primitive, real non-`llvm_emit.ml` consumer" criterion as Task 5): `llvm_ret_ty` (needed by `emit_raises_wrapper` and by `llvm_tco.ml`'s group-return-type resolution) and `emit_reduction_check` (needed by `emit_mutual_tco_group`). One new callback (`~emit_expr`, at `emit_mutual_tco_group`'s single call site in `emit_module`) — well under the BLOCKED threshold. Recent test-pinned behavior (B7/B8/B11 fixes, the `in_tail` fix) verified verbatim-preserved by the move.
- ⚠️ **Process note (self-disclosed, no residue): a `git stash` was used mid-investigation of an IR-diff false alarm, against the plan's "no stash" process rule.** Caught before any command relied on the stashed state; `git stash pop` immediately restored the tree, confirmed via a clean build before continuing. `git stash list` empty from that point forward. The false alarm itself (§D of the task report) was a test-methodology trap, not a real regression: comparing `.ll` output from a scratch directory with no adjacent `stdlib/` made `bin/main.ml`'s CWD-relative `find_stdlib_dir` fallback silently resolve to zero stdlib, producing a spurious IR diff; fixed by pointing `MARCH_STDLIB` at each worktree's own `stdlib/` explicitly. Recorded as the second of Task 10's three folded-in fix-cycle findings.
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) — 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots (incl. `mutual_tco` and `self_tco_loop` TIR snapshots explicitly checked). Zero `.expected` snapshot churn. Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm --opt 2` on all four benchmarks against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `ec294fb8` (both compilers invoked with `MARCH_STDLIB` pinned per the trap above) — byte-identical `.ll` in all four. Compiled outputs correct; timings within noise (largest delta fib +4%). `scripts/check-docs.sh` re-run as a sanity check, exit 0. Full report: `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-6-report.md`.

## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 7: `llvm_toplevel.ml`/`llvm_repl.ml` split; `llvm_emit.ml` becomes orchestrator (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`)

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml`** (881 lines): `emit_fn` (incl. self-TCO loop transform), `fn_declare_str`, `build_ctor_info`, `emit_module` (module-level assembly: HCR name-table setup, extern/main/test/WASM entry emission), plus `is_leaf_callee`/`expr_has_call` (emit_fn's Phase-4 leaf-detection chain, moved as a unit since emit_fn was their only consumer). **New `lib/tir/llvm_repl.ml`** (394 lines): the five REPL/fragment emitters (`emit_repl_expr`, `emit_repl_decl`, `emit_repl_fn`, `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot`, `emit_fns_fragment`) plus `repl_slot_info` and the slot/global bridge helpers (`emit_prev_slot_bridges`/`emit_store_to_slot`/`emit_slot_loader_fns`/`emit_prev_global_bridges`). `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` shrinks from 4015 to 2957 lines (net includes new header-doc pointers) and becomes a thin orchestrator: the `emit_expr`/`emit_atom` core recursive emitters stay (the one piece every split in this chunk explicitly leaves in place), and everything else is a bare re-export or a callback-pre-application shim.
- ✅ **`target_config` (+ `Native`/`Wasm64Wasi`/`Wasm32Wasi`/`Wasm32Unknown`/`Js`), `is_wasm_target`, `is_wasm32`, `target_triple`, `target_ptr_size`/`target_ptr_ty`/`target_int_ty`, and the `emit_preamble` thin wrapper all moved to `llvm_toplevel.ml`** — `emit_module`/`emit_preamble` are their one real cross-module consumer, same "pure primitive, real external consumer" promotion criterion Task 6 used for `llvm_ret_ty`/`emit_reduction_check`. Re-exported bare (the type via `type target_config = Llvm_toplevel.target_config = Native | ...`, matching the existing `ctor_entry`/`ctx` re-export pattern) so external callers (`bin/main.ml`'s ~20 `March_tir.Llvm_emit.Native`/`.target_triple`/etc references, `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `llvm_builtins_preamble_golden` suite) see zero signature change.
- ✅ **Three callbacks, no BLOCKED condition.** `emit_fn`/`emit_module` (llvm_toplevel.ml) and all five REPL emitters (llvm_repl.ml) need `Llvm_emit.emit_expr` (the core recursive emitter that stays put per the brief) to descend into function/fragment bodies; since `llvm_emit.ml` in turn calls `Llvm_toplevel.emit_module`/`Llvm_repl.emit_repl_*` as its public API, a direct back-reference would cycle. Both new modules take `~emit_expr` as a labeled callback — the same de-cycling pattern `Llvm_case.emit_case` (Task 5) and `Llvm_tco.emit_mutual_tco_group` (Task 6) established. `llvm_repl.ml`'s five emitters ALSO call `Llvm_toplevel.emit_fn`/`build_ctor_info`/`fn_declare_str`/`emit_preamble` — these are plain forward-qualified references, not callbacks, since the dependency is one-directional (llvm_repl.ml → llvm_toplevel.ml; confirmed by grep that `emit_module` never calls any REPL emitter, and `llvm_toplevel.ml` has zero references to `Llvm_repl.`). Total: 1 callback name (`~emit_expr`) reused across both new modules — well under the ">4 = BLOCKED" threshold, so no BLOCKED report was warranted.
- ✅ **Dead code carried verbatim, not deleted, and filed here (refactor discipline: bugs/gaps found while moving get filed, not fixed):** `emit_main_wrapper` (llvm_toplevel.ml), `repl_globals`/`emit_repl_globals_decl` and `llvm_ty_of_tir` (llvm_repl.ml) have zero callers anywhere in the tree (grepped bin/, lib/, test/ before and after the move) — pre-existing dead code, moved as-is per verbatim-move discipline rather than deleted as a drive-by cleanup. Also confirmed `emit_repl_fn` (one of the "five REPL emitters" named in the brief) has zero external callers (only a stray doc-comment mention in test/test_codegen.ml) but is still moved/re-exported since the brief explicitly names it as part of the REPL emitter set.
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) at 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib (incl. Slow JIT/parity tests) / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — zero `.expected` diffs (test counts unchanged — this task moves code, adds no tests). Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm --opt 2` on all four benchmarks against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `ce2fb5aa` (temp worktree `/tmp/w3c2-task7-kapitsa`, removed after use), each compiler invoked with `MARCH_STDLIB` pointed at its own worktree's `stdlib/` (per Task 6's documented CWD-fallback trap) — byte-identical `.ll` in all four. Compiled (`--compile --opt 2`) outputs match expected values (fib=102334155, tree_transform=104857600, list_ops=333333666666, binary_trees checks match Task 6's baseline); timings within noise of Task 6's baseline (fib -2.6%, binary_trees -3.1%, tree_transform -6%, list_ops -0.9%, all well under ±10%). `scripts/check-docs.sh` clean.
- ✅ **Module-cycle check:** `grep -c "Llvm_repl\." lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml` = 0 (confirms the dependency is one-directional, llvm_repl.ml → llvm_toplevel.ml, not a cycle); `lib/tir/dune`'s `modules` list places `llvm_toplevel` then `llvm_repl` then `llvm_emit`, matching this direction.
- ✅ **Reference:** `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md` Task 7. Full writeup in `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-7-report.md`. **Six-runner counts: 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all exit 0, zero `.expected` diffs, doc-lint passes.**
- ✅ **RESOLVED by Task 10:** Wave 3 Chunk 2 Tasks 5 and 6 (the `llvm_eq`/`llvm_data`/`llvm_case` and `llvm_calls`/`llvm_tco` splits) did not add their own `specs/todos.md`/`specs/progress.md` "Current State" entries when they landed (commits `ec294fb8`/`ce2fb5aa`) — only Tasks 1, 2, and 4 did. Backfilled by Task 10: see the "(BACKFILLED by Task 10)" Task 5/Task 6 entries above, and the corresponding `specs/progress.md` entries.

## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 8: `lower.ml` env-record threading (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`)

- ✅ **`lib/tir/lower.ml`'s 16 module-level mutable refs/Hashtbls inventoried and classified per Chunk-1 Task-4's transformation law**, IN PLACE (no file split — that's Task 9). Full per-ref table, every save/restore mapping, and throw-path analysis in `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-8-report.md`.
  - **2 converted to `env` fields**, threaded as an explicit first parameter through the entire CPS lowering recursive group and its orchestration callers: `type_map` (was `_type_map_ref` — module-scoped, read-only for the whole run) and `current_module_aliases` (was `_current_module_aliases` — TWO save/restore dances: `lower_mod_decls`' was `Fun.protect`-guarded and exception-safe; `collect_tests`' `DMod` dance was NOT — see the filed unobservable-behavior-change note below).
  - **14 stay refs, each with an in-code "NOT converted" rationale comment** — 13 here plus `_fn_param_types` (next bullet): 8 accumulators (`_use_aliases`, `_module_alias_snapshots`, `_alias_candidates`, `_alias_reported`, `_iface_methods`, `_fns_ref`, `_types_ref`, `_lowered_modules`), 1 process-lifetime forward-ref (`_ensure_module_lowered`, now `(env -> string -> unit) ref`), 1 cross-call cache (`_saved_iface_methods`), 1 fresh-name counter (`_lower_counter`), and the 2 remaining members of the **NOT-reset trio** (`_current_module_fns`, `_default_dispatch`).
  - **`_fn_param_types` NOT converted — restore-gap.** All 6 hand-rolled save/restore dances around it bracket a call to `lower_expr`, which can `failwith`, and none use `Fun.protect` — a thrown exception mid-dance leaves the table dirty (current behavior). An `env`-field version would silently NOT reproduce this (a callee's exception can't dirty the caller's own `env` binding). Preserved bit-for-bit per the plan's throw-path rule.
- ⚠️ **[FILED — unobservable behavior change on `_current_module_aliases`' second (unprotected) dance, erased by the env conversion.]** `_current_module_aliases` had TWO save/restore dances, and only one was exception-safe: `lower_mod_decls`' used `Fun.protect` (safe; the env version is equivalent by construction), but `collect_tests`' `DMod` case (base `c37e81df` ~:2605–2613) was a BARE `let saved = !r in (r := snapshot); recurse; r := saved` around throw-capable recursion (`collect_tests` → `lower_test_body` → `lower_expr`, which can `failwith`) — on the throw path the OLD code left the ref dirty (pointing at the module's alias snapshot). The env conversion does NOT reproduce that dirty state. **This was converted anyway (not reverted) because the dirt was provably UNOBSERVABLE on every path** — the observability trace, verified against base `c37e81df`: (1) every read site of `!_current_module_aliases` (`resolve_use_alias` :235, the `DUse` registration :2381–2382, the snapshot save :2429, the two dance saves :2304/:2605) executes only within `lower_module`'s dynamic extent — grep confirms zero external references to the ref or to any internal reader (`resolve_use_alias`/`lower_fn_def`/`lower_actor`/`lower_stdlib_mod_decls` have no callers outside lower.ml; the one test poke, `test_eval.ml`'s `_ensure_module_lowered` call, passes a nonexistent module so no lowering/read occurs); (2) a throw escaping `collect_tests` propagates out of `lower_module`, whose exit cleanup (base :2687, `_current_module_aliases := Hashtbl.create 0`) was NOT exception-protected — so the dirt did survive the frame — but the NEXT `lower_module` call's entry preamble (base :1941, `_current_module_aliases := Hashtbl.create 16`, the 5th statement, before Pass 1's first possible `resolve_use_alias` read) reset it BEFORE any read; (3) in batch mode there is no next call — the exception propagates to `bin/main.ml` and the process exits on the diagnostic. So no execution could ever distinguish the old dirty-on-throw state from the new clean one. Unlike the `_fn_param_types` case (where the dirty table IS read by the same in-flight traversal's sibling dances before any reset), this erasure changes no observable behavior — documented at the `env.current_module_aliases` field doc and at the `collect_tests` `DMod` site in `lib/tir/lower.ml`. If a future change adds a reader of per-module aliases outside `lower_module`'s dynamic extent (or catches lowering exceptions and resumes WITHOUT re-entering `lower_module`), this note is the record that the throw-path state differs from the pre-Task-8 code.
- ❓ **[FILED — reentrancy question, analysis doc `specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md` lower.ml High #2]** `_current_module_fns`/`_default_dispatch`/`_fn_param_types` staleness across `lower_module` calls (impl-method bodies lowered in Pass 1 read whatever these tables held at the END of the PREVIOUS `lower_module` invocation, since Pass 1 runs before Pass 2 rebuilds them) is preserved exactly as this task's mandate requires — but WHETHER the REPL JIT (`lib/jit/repl_jit.ml:286`, calls `lower_module` per fragment) actually relies on this staleness, tolerates it, or is silently miscompiling in a way nobody's hit yet, is still open. A future task empowered to CHANGE (not just document) lower.ml's reentrancy behavior needs to answer this first — e.g. by instrumenting a REPL session with 2+ fragments that each define an impl method and a default-arg function, and checking whether the second fragment's Pass-1 lowering sees the first fragment's tables or empty ones.
- ✅ **Sharp gate: zero TIR snapshot churn.** All 14 post-lower + 14 post-perceus corpus snapshots + the determinism check pass byte-identical (`run_snapshots.exe`: 29/29 green).
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) at 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots (1861 tests) — zero `.expected` diffs. Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm --opt 2` on all four benchmarks (tree_transform/list_ops/binary_trees/mutual_recursion) against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `c37e81df` — byte-identical `.ll` in all four (0 diff lines). Compiled (`--compile --opt 2`) outputs match expected values; timings moot given byte-identical IR.
- ✅ **Test-file mechanical fallout:** `test/test_eval.ml`'s `test_tir_lower_qualified_auto_load` calls `Lower._ensure_module_lowered` directly to test its lazy-stdlib-loading mechanism in isolation — its signature change (`string -> unit` → `env -> string -> unit`) forced updating that one call site to pass `Lower.empty_env`. Applied the same `bin/`-exception precedent chunk-1 Task 4 used (test-file coupling to an internal ref is a real, if unusual, external dependency — flagged, not routed around).
- ✅ **Reference:** `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md` Task 8. Full writeup in `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-8-report.md`. **Six-runner counts: 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all exit 0, zero `.expected` diffs.**

## Wave 3 Chunk 2 — Task 9: `lower.ml` split into 5 focused modules; `lower.ml` becomes orchestrator (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md`)

- ✅ **`lib/tir/lower.ml` (2868 lines) split into 6 files**, all verbatim whole-function/whole-section moves per the plan's discipline: `lower_types.ml` (97 lines — both `lower_ty`/`convert_ty` converters, side by side, cross-referencing comments pointing at the filed arrow/Nat encoding disagreement, analysis doc lower.ml High #4 — **unifying them stays explicitly forbidden**), `lower_state.ml` (429 lines — new, not in the plan's named list; see "kept-refs placement" below for why it exists), `lower_match.ml` (423 lines — `compile_matrix`/`compile_matrix_impl`, guards, join points, `pat_tag_and_subs`, `bind_trivial_pat`, `nonexhaustive_panic`'s wave-1 hoisted form, `collect_pat_names`), `lower_decls.ml` (242 lines — `lower_fn_def`, `lower_type_def`, `rename_tir_vars`, the lazy stdlib-module loader, the deduped extern-lowering helper), `lower_actor.ml` (362 lines — `lower_actor` + glue), `lower_tests.ml` (150 lines — DTest/DSetup/DSetupAll/DDescribe collection, including Task 8's `DMod` observability comment, moved verbatim). `lower.ml` itself is now 1399 lines: `lower_expr`/`lower_to_atom_k`/`lower_atoms_k` (the mutual-recursive core, per the plan's explicit carve-out) + `lower_module`'s three decl-walkers (Pass 2's top-level `List.iter`, the nested `lower_mod_decls`, `collect_iface_impls`) + the public re-export shim.
- ✅ **`lower_state.ml` justified (the brief's "small lower_state.ml if the cut demands" clause, invoked):** `env` (the record itself, not just its fields) and every table `resolve_use_alias`/`resolve_iface_method`/`ty_of_span` close over (`_fn_param_types`, `_use_aliases`, `_module_alias_snapshots`, `_alias_candidates`/`_alias_reported`, `_current_module_fns`/`with_current_module_fns`, `_fns_ref`/`_types_ref`/`_lowered_modules`, `_ensure_module_lowered`, `_iface_methods`/`_saved_iface_methods`, `_default_dispatch`, the fresh-name counter, `nonexhaustive_panic`) are consumed by FOUR of the five new modules (`lower_match`, `lower_decls`, `lower_actor`, `lower_tests`) plus `lower.ml` itself — no single one of those four owns `env`, and OCaml's acyclic-module-graph requirement means whichever module defines it must build before all its consumers. Picking one arbitrary consumer (e.g. folding `env` into `lower_match.ml`) would make the unrelated others (`lower_decls`, `lower_actor`) depend on a module named after match-compilation. Every ref kept its original Task 8 "NOT converted" rationale comment verbatim — only the file changed.
- ✅ **`lower_match.ml` <-> `lower.ml` cycle, mapped and broken via forward ref (not left in lower.ml, not duplicated).** Dependency-map found exactly 5 edges between `lower_expr` (staying in `lower.ml`) and the match-compilation cluster (assigned to `lower_match.ml` by the plan's boundary): 2 IN (lower.ml's `EBlock`/`ELet` arm calls `collect_pat_names`; its `EMatch` arm calls `lower_match`) and 3 OUT (`lower_branch_body_with_pat` and the guarded-match path both call back into `lower_expr`). Well under the plan's ">4 in a module = BLOCKED" threshold, and `collect_pat_names` itself needed no wiring (pure function of `Ast.pattern`, no cycle) — only the 3 OUT edges needed breaking. Used the SAME forward-ref idiom `lower.ml` already used for `_ensure_module_lowered`: `Lower_match._lower_expr_ref`, installed once via `Lower_match.install_lower_expr lower_expr` right after `lower_expr`'s definition in `lower.ml`. Pure wiring change (indirect call through a ref cell vs. a direct same-file `and`-chain call) — verified by the byte-identical IR gate below, which would catch any behavioral drift from the rewiring.
- ✅ **The three module-decl walkers — moved as columns, NOT unified into one parameterized function (plan's fallback path, taken deliberately).** Investigated whether Pass 2's top-level `List.iter` over `m.mod_decls`, the nested `lower_mod_decls` (recurses into `DMod`), and `lower_stdlib_mod_decls` (the lazy stdlib-loader's copy, which the analysis doc's lower.ml High finding documents as handling FEWER decl kinds — no `DUse`/`DActor`/`DExtern` — than the other two) could become one parameterized walker. They could NOT provably preserve each caller's exact current decl-kind coverage as explicit parameters without either (a) re-implementing the lazy-stdlib path's silent-drop behavior as an opt-out flag (which is itself indistinguishable from "fix the bug" — exactly what this task is forbidden from doing) or (b) risking a parameter list so large the "one walker" would be less readable than three named ones. Per the plan's explicit fallback ("if parameterization can't provably preserve each path, move the copies as-is and file"): the two full walkers (Pass 2's inline iter, `lower_mod_decls`) stayed in `lower.ml`'s orchestrator body (they assemble the returned `Tir.tir_module`'s accumulators directly — same rationale `emit_module` used to stay in `llvm_toplevel.ml`/the orchestrator in the llvm split); `lower_stdlib_mod_decls` moved to `lower_decls.ml` verbatim, unchanged, still lazily invoked via the `_ensure_module_lowered` forward ref. **Filed (already on record, re-confirmed unchanged by this task):** the lazy-stdlib walker's narrower decl-kind coverage remains a live bug, analysis doc lower.ml High finding, cross-referenced in `Lower_decls.lower_stdlib_mod_decls`'s doc comment — not fixed here.
- ✅ **The two DExtern blocks — verified byte-identical (module leading whitespace from nesting depth), deduped into `Lower_decls.lower_extern_fns`.** Diffed the nested `lower_mod_decls` DExtern arm against the top-level Pass 2 DExtern arm with the indentation normalized: identical. Extracted the shared per-`extern_fn` → `Tir.extern_decl` conversion into one function; both call sites now do `externs := List.rev_append (Lower_decls.lower_extern_fns edef edef.ext_fns) !externs` — verified the `List.rev_append` reproduces the original `List.iter (fun ef -> externs := entry :: !externs) edef.ext_fns` prepend-in-iteration-order semantics exactly (a small `ocaml` scratch check: `List.iter (fun x -> r := x :: !r) [1;2;3]` yields `[3;2;1]`, matching `List.rev_append [1;2;3] initial`).
- ✅ **`lower_tests.ml`'s `collect_tests`/`lower_test_body` extraction: closure-capture → explicit parameter, not a behavior change.** The original code was closures defined INLINE inside `lower_module`, capturing `lower_module`'s own local `fns`/`test_pairs` refs and `env`/`m` parameters directly. `Lower_tests.run env fns test_pairs decls` takes those as explicit arguments instead — every expression inside is the verbatim original body; only the calling convention (closure capture vs. parameter passing) changed, which cannot be observed from outside the function.
- ✅ **Public API unchanged.** `Lower.lower_module`, `Lower.env`, `Lower.empty_env`, `Lower.lower_ty`/`Lower.convert_ty`/`Lower.lower_type_def`, `Lower.get_iface_methods`, and the four refs `test/test_eval.ml` pokes directly (`Lower._fns_ref`/`Lower._types_ref`/`Lower._lowered_modules`/`Lower._ensure_module_lowered`) are all re-exported bare from `lower.ml` (same `let x = Module.x` convention `llvm_emit.ml`'s Task 7 re-export shim used) — every external caller (`bin/main.ml`, `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`, `test/`, `lsp/lib/analysis.ml`) compiles unchanged.
- ✅ **Sharp gate: zero TIR snapshot churn.** All 14 post-lower + 14 post-perceus corpus snapshots + the determinism check pass byte-identical (`run_snapshots.exe`: 29/29 green) — the sharpest gate of the wave for this file, per the plan's own framing.
- ✅ **Standard gates, all green:** six runners exit 0 (isolated `HOME=.w3home`) at 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots (1861 tests) — zero `.expected` diffs; `test_properties.exe` (not one of the six, but exercises `lower_module` heavily via QCheck generators) also green at 36/36. Baseline IR diff: `--emit-llvm --opt 2` on all four benchmarks (fib/binary_trees/tree_transform/list_ops, matching Tasks 3–8's benchmark set) against a clean sibling-worktree build of base commit `fe046757` — byte-identical `.ll` in all four (0 diff lines), re-verified deterministic across a second run. Compiled (`--compile --opt 2`) outputs match expected values exactly; timings within ±10% of baseline (fib 0.76s→0.75s, binary_trees 0.30s→0.32s, tree_transform 9.07s→9.38s, list_ops 0.33s→0.37s).
- ✅ **Reference:** `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk2-emit-lower.md` Task 9. Full writeup in `.superpowers/sdd/w3c2-task-9-report.md`. **Six-runner counts: 384 compiler / 230 eval / 374 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all exit 0, zero `.expected` diffs.**

## Dev tooling — spec-search Claude skill (2026-07-07)

- ✅ **spec-search Claude skill — FTS5 index over specs/lang + specs/impl + specs/features** — `scripts/spec_index_build.py` (markdown H1-H3 section parser + FTS5 db builder) + `scripts/build-spec-index.sh` (orchestrator: vendors `specs/{lang,impl,features}/*.md` into `.claude/skills/spec-search/docs/`, rebuilds `spec-search.db`) + `.claude/skills/spec-search/spec-search.sh` (self-locating bash query script, human + `--json` modes, bm25-ranked, sanitized FTS5 MATCH) + `.claude/skills/spec-search/SKILL.md`. Self-contained and portable: installs once at `~/.claude/skills/spec-search/`, works in any March project with no dependency on that project's own `specs/` (docs + db are bundled, not referenced). `lineno`/`end_lineno` per section let the skill `Read` just the matched slice instead of whole chapters. `meta` table stamps `built_from_commit`/`built_at_date`; no active version-matching against a project's pinned toolchain (March versioning is still pre-1.0/dormant). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-06-spec-search-fts5-design.md`.

## Compiler bug — JS backend Int `/` never truncated (2026-07-09)

- ✅ **`lib/tir/js_emit.ml`'s `inline_binop` shadowed the type-aware Int-division
  guard for the `/` operator.** `inline_binop("/")` matched first (a fast-path
  table for symbolic operators) and unconditionally emitted raw `(a / b)` —
  JS's true division — for EVERY `/` call, Int or Float. The correct,
  type-aware logic a few lines below (`"/", [a; b] when atom_ty a = TInt ->
  Math.trunc(a / b)`) was dead code, unreachable for the symbolic `"/"` name
  since `inline_binop` always won first. Reproduced minimally:
  `fn half(n: Int): Int do n / 10 end; half(15)` printed `1.5` under
  `--target js` (correct answer: `1`, and correct natively/interpreted).
  Found while wiring `TetrisLogic.level_for_lines`/`drop_interval_ms` into
  the Tetris playground demo's tick loop (`total_lines / 10` was silently
  producing fractional levels, e.g. "Level: 1.5").
- **Fix:** removed `"/"` from `inline_binop`'s combined `"/" | "/." -> Some "/"`
  arm (kept `"/."` — float division needs no truncation and was never
  ambiguous), forcing all Int `/` through the existing type-aware match arm.
  One-line-plus-comment fix in `lib/tir/js_emit.ml`.
- **Verified:** full suite green (805 tests, exit 0) including the existing
  `adversarial-regressions` int/float-division-by-zero cases; minimal repro
  now prints `1`; Tetris playground's level display and drop-speed ramp both
  correct in the browser (level text, and measured tick-interval deltas
  matching `drop_interval_ms(level)` exactly for level 0 and level 1).
- **Likely impact:** any `--target js` program dividing two `Int`s via the
  bare `/` operator (not the `div_int`-named builtin, nor `Int.div`-style
  helpers) got a fractional JS result instead of a truncated one — silent,
  no compiler warning, no type error (JS has no static Int/Float
  distinction at runtime). Plausibly present since `/`'s Int-truncation
  guard was added; not otherwise caught because the browser playground demo
  is the first `--target js` consumer in this repo to exercise Int
  division on a value that wasn't already a multiple of the divisor.

## Compiler bug — multi-head fn recurses infinitely under --target js (filed, not fixed, 2026-07-10)

- ❌ **A multi-head function mixing a literal-Int pattern in one argument
  position with a plain-variable pattern in another loses its base case
  under `--target js` and recurses infinitely** (stack overflow, not just
  missing TCO — confirmed via a minimal repro with only 2 levels of true
  recursion depth, far too shallow to be a real stack-depth issue):
  ```march
  mod MultiHeadTest do
    fn rotate_n(cells, 0) do cells end
    fn rotate_n(cells, n) do rotate_n(cells, n - 1) end

    fn main() : Unit do
      println(int_to_string(List.length(rotate_n([1,2,3], 2))))
    end
  end
  ```
  Native (`march file.march`) prints `3` correctly. `--target js` throws
  `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` immediately — the emitted
  JS recurses through a join-point apply wrapper
  (`rotate_n$List_Int$Int` ↔ `$jpNNNNN$apply$NNNN`) that never resolves the
  `n == 0` base case, so it truly never terminates rather than just
  overflowing a deep-but-finite native stack.
- **Workaround (used in `demo_app/tetris/lib/tetris.march`'s `rotate_n`):**
  rewrite as a single function body with an internal `match n do 0 -> ...
  | _ -> recurse end` instead of two `fn rotate_n(cells, 0) do ... end` /
  `fn rotate_n(cells, n) do ... end` clauses — same minimal repro shape with
  this rewrite compiles and runs correctly under `--target js` (prints `3`).
- **Scope narrowed:** single-arg multi-head (`fn fib(0) do 0 end` / `fn
  fib(1) do 1 end` / `fn fib(n) do ... end`, `examples/pattern_matching.march`'s
  existing style) compiles and runs correctly under `--target js` (prints
  `55` for `fib(10)`, no stack overflow) — the bug is specific to
  **multi-argument multi-head where only one parameter position varies by
  pattern across clauses** (`rotate_n(cells, 0)` / `rotate_n(cells, n)`: the
  first position is a plain variable in every clause, only the second
  differs). Not investigated further: whether this is in the TIR
  join-points pass specifically (the emitted symbol names —
  `rotate_n$List_Int$Int` ↔ `$jpNNNNN$apply$NNNN` — suggest that's where the
  base case gets lost) or `lib/tir/js_emit.ml`'s tail-call handling for the
  resulting match-desugared shape.
